Source session: `7e41ee97-7abe-4f29-a382-a038935fae91`; workflow: `wf_13a34057-17a`.

Recovered from the persistent workflow result and per-agent journal on 2026-09-07 (America/Denver). These are inherited audit claims and verdicts, not a new independent audit. Original classifications are preserved below; fixes from the resumed session are tracked separately here.

## Recovery status

26 audit/diff units completed. 83 raw candidates; 49 have all required affirmative verifier votes; 23 were refuted; 11 remain unverified because 14 verifier executions hit the prior session limit. Candidates include overlapping reports and have not been deduplicated.

The original reducer incorrectly put candidates with no verifier results into `refuted` and accepted one P2 with only one of two required votes as `confirmed`. This archive reconstructs status from the journal and requires both votes for P1/P2 and one for P3. Missing verification is never a refutation. Refutations of dead-code findings must still be checked against the SOP requirement to remove unreachable surface.

PR: https://github.com/BitHighlander/keepkey-firmware/pull/631 (base `alpha`). The checkout and remote PR head were both at the audited SHA when recovered. CI was still running; completed ARM, format, static-analysis and unit-test jobs were passing. No clean-pass or upstream-readiness claim is made.

## Codex continuation: first fix group

The resumed session reproduced and fixed C3-010, C3-011 and C3-012:

- **C3-010:** Commit to each named type's canonical segment at first receipt; verify every later discovery, type-hash and member request, including a child's definition already included in its parent's type hash. Five attack regressions failed before the fix (renaming, deleting members, retyping a struct array as an opaque hash, and mutating a nested definition).
- **C3-011:** Refuse leaves before review when their formatted body cannot fit the confirmation source buffer. Strings of 352 bytes and dynamic bytes of 172 bytes are refused; 351-byte strings and 171-byte dynamic values remain reviewable. Removed the misleading claim that generic pagination could recover already-truncated source text.
- **C3-012:** Clear the streamed signing state and pending request on Cancel and session teardown, covering Initialize, ClearSession and auto-lock. Ack handlers require the cached PIN authorization. The verifier caveat still applies: the old auto-lock path did not produce a signature on a PIN-protected locked device; Cancel/Initialize resumption was the demonstrated signing defect.

Validation: 595/595 firmware-unit and 21/21 board-unit tests passed, including the ten tests added for this fix group. An additional repeated-struct-array control was then added for the compacted frame layout and included in the final EIP-712 test run. Clang-format 20 and cppcheck (warning/style/performance/portability, inconclusive, all configurations) passed on the touched translation units.

Device-memory constraint: the existing ARM artifact has a 16,480-byte reserve, only 96 bytes above the 16,384-byte floor. The fix shares mutually exclusive struct/array frame fields and request/result fields, drops unused session fields, and moves the immutable error table into flash. A local ARM layout comparison estimates a 64-byte net SRAM increase. This is an estimate, not a linked device build; the new commit's CI SRAM gate remains required. Local ARM linking is unavailable because the installed compiler lacks its C library and Docker is not running.

This is a partial fix group. The other 46 originally confirmed reports, all 11 incompletely verified reports, and the dead-code/SOP discrepancies still require triage. Overlapping reports remain undeduplicated. No full cycle-3 re-audit, zero-finding pass, second-model clean pass, alpha merge, or upstream action has occurred.

## Findings

### C3-001 — CONFIRMED — P3: lock_time, expiry, version and nSequence are signed but never disclosed on the final Bitcoin confirmation

Unit: `audit:bitcoin-signing`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/signing.c:1237`.

signing_init() copies msg->version, msg->lock_time and msg->expiry verbatim from the host SignTx (signing.c:670-672) and every input's sequence from TxAck; all four are committed to every sighash (tx_serialize_footer / signing_hash_bip143 'nLockTime' at :1333, zip143/zip243 at :1371, bip341_sighash lock_time, hash_sequence/hash_sequences_tr) and to the serialized transaction returned to the host. The only summary screen, confirm_transaction(total_amount_str, fee_str) at signing.c:1237, shows amount and fee; no screen in signing.c, transaction.c or app_confirm.c renders lock_time, expiry, version or any nSequence. This is the UTXO sibling of the already-reported cosmos-tendermint 'account_number and sequence are signed but never displayed' P3: host-controlled bytes flow into the signature with no disclosure and no refusal.

Host trace / impact: A compromised host sends SignTx with lock_time set to a block height/timestamp far in the future (and inputs with sequence 0xFFFFFFFE so the locktime is enforced). The user sees only 'Do you want to send X ... fee Y' and approves. The host now holds a fully valid, non-broadcastable-until-then transaction over the user's UTXOs that it can release at a time of its choosing (or use to hold the UTXOs hostage against fee-bumping, since nSequence/RBF signalling is equally invisible). Outputs are still the approved ones, so this is a disclosure-invariant violation rather than direct theft.

Proposed remediation: Mirror Trezor's behaviour: if lock_time != 0 (or expiry != 0 on Decred/Zcash), show a mandatory confirm screen before the fee summary ('Locktime set to block N / time T'; 'Locktime set but ineffective' when all sequences are 0xFFFFFFFF), and refuse non-default version values the coin does not use. At minimum fold lock_time/expiry into the confirm_transaction() body so every signed header field is on-screen.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Reproduced at HEAD 71ba84436. signing_init() (lib/firmware/signing.c:670-672) copies version/lock_time/expiry straight from host SignTx; per-input sequence is taken from TxAck and hashed unvalidated (signing.c:1086, transaction.c:794/876). All four feed every sighash (bip341 at :1291, bip143 nLockTime at :1310, zip143/zip243 at :1332-1335/:1370-1373) and the serialized footer (transaction.c:972-1002). The sole summary screen is confirm_transaction(total_amount_str, fee_str) at signing.c:1237; its implementation (lib/firmware/app_confirm.c:208-218) prints only amount and fee. No code in signing.c, transaction.c, app_confirm.c or fsm_msg_*.h displays or rejects lock_time/expiry/version/nSequence, so a host can obtain a signed far-future-locktime (or RBF-disabled) tx the user never saw as such. Disclosure-invariant gap, not theft; P3 rating is appropriate.

### C3-002 — CONFIRMED — P3: fsm_getDerivedNode() leaves the root/partially-derived private node in static RAM when derivation fails; fsm_msg_coin.h callers return without scrubbing

Unit: `audit:bitcoin-signing`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm.c:283`.

fsm_getDerivedNode() loads the seed-derived root into the static CONFIDENTIAL fsm_derived_node (fsm.c:272) and then runs hdnode_private_ckd_cached() in place (fsm.c:283). If that returns 0 it sends a Failure and returns NULL without clearing fsm_derived_node, so whatever private key was derived up to the failing step (root, or an intermediate hardened child) stays resident. All three unit callers -- fsm_msgGetPublicKey (fsm_msg_coin.h:19-21), fsm_msgGetAddress (:218-220) and fsm_msgSignMessage (:301-303) -- do `if (!node) return;` with no memzero, unlike every success/cancel path in the same handlers which memzero(node). fsm_clearDerivedNode() is only invoked from signing_abort() and storage lock/wipe, so the residue persists until the next successful derivation or a lock.

Host trace / impact: Host sends GetPublicKey with has_ecdsa_curve_name=true, ecdsa_curve_name="ed25519" (accepted by get_curve_by_name) and address_n=[0x80000000|44, 0] -- the second, non-hardened index makes hdnode_private_ckd() return 0 (bip32.c:246, curve->params==NULL). The device answers 'Failed to derive private key' and fsm_derived_node now holds the m/44' ed25519 private key + chain code in RAM indefinitely. No message reads it back, so this is RAM-residue only (relevant to fault/side-channel or a later memory-disclosure bug), not a remote key leak.

Proposed remediation: In fsm_getDerivedNode(), call fsm_clearDerivedNode() (or memzero(&fsm_derived_node, ...)) in both failure branches (storage_getRootNode false and hdnode_private_ckd_cached == 0) before returning 0, so callers can keep their `if (!node) return;` shape.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/fsm.c:272 storage_getRootNode() writes the seed-derived root directly into static CONFIDENTIAL fsm_derived_node, then fsm.c:283 hdnode_private_ckd_cached() derives in place; on return 0 (fsm.c:284-287) it sends Failure and returns 0 with no memzero. Callers fsm_msg_coin.h:21 (GetPublicKey), :220 (GetAddress), :303 (SignMessage) do `if (!node) return;` with no scrub, while their success paths memzero(node) (:51/:78/:229/:277/:312-322). fsm_clearDerivedNode() is only called from storage.c (reset/wipe/session_clear) and signing.c:2220 (signing_abort). Reachability: GetPublicKey accepts host ecdsa_curve_name (fsm_msg_coin.h:15-17); get_curve_by_name accepts "ed25519" (bip32.c:1024); ed25519_info.params == NULL (bip32.c:60); storage_getRootNode for non-secp256k1 uses hdnode_from_seed(session.seed, curve) (storage.c:2572) so the ed25519 root lands in fsm_derived_node; address_n=[0x8000002C, 0] goes to the i_count-1>... / count==2 cached path which calls hdnode_private_ckd for index 0 -> public derivation with curve->params NULL returns 0 (bip32.c:246-247) after the hardened m/44' step already succeeded in place. Result: m/44' ed25519 private key + chain code remain in fsm_derived_node until the next successful derivation, signing_abort, or lock/wipe. Impact as stated by the claim: RAM residue only, no host-readable path (P3).

### C3-003 — CONFIRMED — P3: Derived private key left in fsm_derived_node on error exits of process_ethereum_xfer and on every exit of fsm_msgEthereumSignTypedHash / eip712_pump

Unit: `audit:ethereum-core`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_ethereum.h:68`.

fsm_getDerivedNode() derives into the static CONFIDENTIAL fsm_derived_node (lib/firmware/fsm.c:109) and nothing clears it per dispatch — fsm_clearDerivedNode() is called only from storage lock/wipe and bitcoin signing.c, so each handler must memzero the node itself (the Cosmos siblings do so on every branch). In this unit the following exits leave the freshly derived private key resident in SRAM: (a) process_ethereum_xfer lines 68-69 (`if (!hdnode_get_ethereum_pubkeyhash(node, to_bytes)) return TXOUT_COMPILE_ERROR;`) and 72-74 (recipient-mismatch `return TXOUT_COMPILE_ERROR;`) return before the `memzero((void*)node, sizeof(HDNode))` at line 81, and the caller fsm_msgEthereumSignTx bails at line 108-113 without deriving again; (b) fsm_msgEthereumSignTypedHash (lines 443-499) never memzeros `node` on any path, including the success path after ethereum_typed_hash_sign; (c) eip712_pump EIP712_REQ_DONE (lines 577-613) derives, signs with node->private_key, and returns on all four paths without clearing; (d) fsm_msgEthereum712TypesValues (lines 523-539) likewise, though currently gated off. Path (a) is host-triggerable with no user interaction beyond the transfer confirm: an ERC-20 TRANSFER whose calldata recipient differs from the device-derived to_address_n key hits line 72 after the key is derived. Sibling pattern of the memzero discipline already applied to fsm_msgEthereumSignTx (line 120), fsm_msgEthereumGetAddress and fsm_msgEthereumSignMessage — the fix missed these exits.

Host trace / impact: Host sends EthereumSignTx{address_type=TRANSFER, to_address_n=<victim account>, ERC-20 transfer calldata whose recipient word != derived address}. The device derives the to_address_n private key into fsm_derived_node, fails the memcmp at line 72, and returns TXOUT_COMPILE_ERROR with the key still in RAM. Any subsequent memory-disclosure primitive (e.g. the class of frame-arena over-read fixed in the prior audit's board-io item, a debug-link read, or physical SRAM extraction while unlocked) recovers a live account private key. Same residue after every legitimate EthereumSignTypedHash / EthereumSignTypedData signature.

Proposed remediation: Zero the node on every exit: in process_ethereum_xfer memzero before each early return after line 63 (or restructure to a single cleanup), and add `memzero((void*)node, sizeof(*node))` before every return in fsm_msgEthereumSignTypedHash, eip712_pump's EIP712_REQ_DONE arm, and fsm_msgEthereum712TypesValues. Consider clearing fsm_derived_node centrally after each message dispatch in fsm.c so no handler can regress.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Reproduced at HEAD 71ba84436. fsm_getDerivedNode() (lib/firmware/fsm.c:259-290) derives into the static CONFIDENTIAL fsm_derived_node and the only callers of fsm_clearDerivedNode() are storage.c (lock/wipe: 1792/1837/1852) and signing.c:2220 — no per-dispatch clearing exists, so each handler must memzero itself. In lib/firmware/fsm_msg_ethereum.h the only memzero sites are lines 81, 120, 296, 340, 348, 381. process_ethereum_xfer derives at 63-65 and has two error returns (68-69 pubkeyhash failure; 72-74 ERC-20 recipient memcmp mismatch) that bypass the memzero at 81; the caller fsm_msgEthereumSignTx then returns at 108-113 without touching the node. fsm_msgEthereumSignTypedHash (414-499) derives at 456 and has zero memzero on any path including the success path; ethereum_typed_hash_sign (ethereum.c:1311) takes const HDNode* and does not clear it either. eip712_pump EIP712_REQ_DONE (577-613) derives, calls ecdsa_sign_digest with node->private_key and returns on all branches without clearing; fsm_msgEthereum712TypesValues (523-539) likewise. Host reachability: EthereumSignTx{address_type=TRANSFER, to_address_n=X, standard ERC-20 transfer calldata whose recipient word != derived address} passes the transfer confirm then hits line 72 with the to_address_n private key still resident; every EthereumSignTypedHash / EthereumSignTypedData signature leaves the signing key resident on the success path. Contrast with fsm_msg_cosmos.h which memzeros on every branch.

### C3-004 — CONFIRMED — P3: Test-only EthTestToken sentinel (0xEE..EE, ticker ' ETH') reachable from the wire: a call to 0xEE..EE is displayed as 'Send X ETH'

Unit: `audit:ethereum-core`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/ethereum_tokens.c:54`.

tokenByChainAddress() falls back to `EthTestToken` for the contract address 0xEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEEE on ANY chain_id (line 54-56), returning a TokenType with ticker "  ETH" and 18 decimals. This sentinel exists only for tests but ships in device firmware and is consulted by ethereum_signing_init (ethereum.c:911-919) and process_ethereum_xfer (fsm_msg_ethereum.h:45). An ERC-20-shaped transfer(recipient, amount) calldata to 0xEE..EE with value=0 therefore renders the primary confirm as "Send <amount> ETH to 0x<recipient>" (title 'Send' or 'Transfer'), and because token != NULL && token != UnknownToken the raw-calldata hash review and AdvancedMode gate at ethereum.c:961-988 are skipped; the fee screen says "Send the tokens from your wallet". What is actually signed is a zero-value call to the 0xEE..EE placeholder address carrying transfer calldata — no ETH moves. Display (an ETH transfer) and signed transaction (a no-op call) disagree, and the device treats an unknown-contract call as a recognized token without the blind-sign disclosure every other unknown contract gets.

Host trace / impact: A host wanting a signed, broadcastable transaction that the victim believes is an ETH payment (e.g. to fabricate 'proof of payment' during a social-engineering exchange, or to burn the victim's gas repeatedly) sends EthereumSignTx{to=0xEE..EE, value=<empty>, data=a9059cbb||pad(recipient)||amount, chain_id=N}. The device shows 'Send 1.0 ETH to 0x<recipient>' with no unknown-contract warning; the user approves; the signed tx transfers nothing. Conversely the same sentinel bypasses the AdvancedMode/raw-data gate for calldata sent to 0xEE..EE on every chain.

Proposed remediation: Remove the EthTestToken fallback from tokenByChainAddress() in production builds (guard with a unit-test/emulator-only define, or inject the test token through the tokens table in the test harness) so 0xEE..EE resolves to UnknownToken and takes the ordinary unknown-contract disclosure path.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Reproduced in code at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/ethereum_tokens.c:53-56: tokenByChainAddress() falls back to EthTestToken (ticker "  ETH", decimals 18) for address 0xEE..EE on ANY chain_id after the table miss; no #ifdef/test gate, it ships in device firmware. lib/firmware/ethereum.c:911-919: ethereum_signing_init calls tokenByChainAddress for any 68-byte standard ERC-20 transfer/approve calldata from the host EthereumSignTx, so token=EthTestToken. Line 921-925: token != NULL -> layoutEthereumConfirmTx(calldata recipient, calldata amount, token) -> ethereumFormatAmount (ethereum.c:412-417) uses token->ticker "  ETH" and 18 decimals -> body "Send 1.0  ETH to 0x<recipient>" with title "Send"/"Transfer". Line 961-988: the raw-calldata keccak review + AdvancedMode block is gated on (token == NULL || token == UnknownToken); EthTestToken is neither, so the unknown-contract disclosure and policy gate are skipped. The signed tx is a zero-value call to 0xEE..EE with transfer calldata; display and signed intent disagree. Same fallback reachable via process_ethereum_xfer (fsm_msg_ethereum.h:45). Fully host-reachable, no device policy required.

### C3-005 — CONFIRMED — P3: EthereumGetAddress show_display blanks the derivation-path label when the host path does not fit NODE_STRING_LENGTH

Unit: `audit:ethereum-core`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_ethereum.h:336`.

When show_display is set, the path label is built by bip32_node_to_string() and, failing that, bip32_path_to_string() into node_str[NODE_STRING_LENGTH=50]. bip32_path_to_string (coins.c:250-274) returns false whenever the formatted path exceeds the buffer; the handler then does `memset(node_str, 0, sizeof(node_str))` (line 336) and proceeds to confirm_ethereum_address("", address) with an empty description. address_n is host-controlled up to max_count:8; eight hardened indices such as 2147483647' format to ~97 characters, so the overflow is reachable from the wire. The address itself is still shown, but the screen that exists to tell the user WHICH account this address belongs to silently loses that field instead of refusing — the same blank-on-format-failure pattern the prior audit removed from ethereumFormatAmount / bn_format.

Host trace / impact: Host sends EthereumGetAddress{address_n=[44',60',0,0,0,0,0,0] or any 8-deep path with large indices, show_display=true}. The device shows the checksummed address with an empty path caption. A user who verifies receive addresses by matching the displayed path (e.g. 'm/44'/60'/0'/0/0') against their wallet cannot detect that the host substituted a non-standard path, and cannot see that the label is missing rather than short. Funds sent to it are recoverable only if the odd path is later rediscovered.

Proposed remediation: Fail closed: if neither formatter succeeds, send FailureType_Failure_SyntaxError (e.g. 'Derivation path too long to display') and return instead of memset+confirm; alternatively bound address_n_count/indices so every accepted path fits, or enlarge NODE_STRING_LENGTH to cover 8 hardened 10-digit levels (>= 100 bytes).

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. fsm_msg_ethereum.h:328-337: when show_display is set, bip32_node_to_string then bip32_path_to_string are tried into node_str[NODE_STRING_LENGTH=50]; bip32_path_to_string (coins.c:250-274) returns false on any truncation (len <= cx), after which the handler does memset(node_str,0,...) and still calls confirm_ethereum_address(node_str, address). layout_ethereum_address_notification (app_layout.c:538) skips drawing the title entirely when desc is "", so the address is displayed with no derivation-path caption and no refusal/error. address_n is host-controlled with max_count:8 (messages-ethereum.options:1) and fsm_getDerivedNode derives any such path, so an 8-deep path with large hardened indices (~97 chars) or even a moderately long one trivially exceeds 50 chars from the wire. Reachable, silent blank-on-format-failure as described; severity P3 is appropriate (address itself still shown correctly, only the account label is lost).

### C3-006 — REFUTED — P3: Dead surface: JSON structured-EIP-712 path (e712_types_values, failMessage, failMsgReturn, eip712.c) and stale lib/firmware/ethereum.h retained behind a permanent fail-closed gate

Unit: `audit:ethereum-core`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/ethereum.c:1402`.

ethereum_structured_eip712_enabled() is hardcoded `return false` (ethereum.c:66), so fsm_msgEthereum712TypesValues refuses every message at line 509 and e712_types_values() (ethereum.c:1402-1565), failMessage()/failMsgReturn[] (1341-1400), and the entire eip712.c JSON parser (encode/parseVals/dsConfirm) are unreachable from the wire. The prior audit dismissed three real defects in that code (domain name/version hashed in full but truncated on screen; unsorted encodeType; static have_ds/domainSeparatorHash state spanning messages) solely because the gate makes them unreachable, and its remediation recommended retiring the path — it is still present at HEAD. Also dead: lib/firmware/ethereum.h (a stale duplicate header with the obsolete 2-arg ethereum_signing_init and ethereum_isThorchainTx prototypes, included by nothing), and the undefined declarations format_ethereum_address(), ethereum_get_decimal(), ethereum_path_check() in include/keepkey/firmware/ethereum.h. Per invariant (5) this is a maintained attack surface with known display-vs-hashed divergences that a single one-line flag flip re-exposes.

Host trace / impact: Not exploitable at HEAD. Risk is regression: any future change flipping ethereum_structured_eip712_enabled() (or a build variant defining it true) immediately re-enables a signer that hashes strings it truncates on screen and emits non-canonical typeHashes; the static have_ds/domainSeparatorHash carried across messages would additionally let a host pair a domain separator from one request with a message hash from another.

Proposed remediation: Delete e712_types_values, failMessage, failMsgReturn, ethereum_structured_eip712_enabled, fsm_msgEthereum712TypesValues' body (leave an UnexpectedMessage/Failure_Other stub if the wire type must stay registered), lib/firmware/eip712.c + its header, lib/firmware/ethereum.h, and the three undefined prototypes in include/keepkey/firmware/ethereum.h; keep only the streamed eip712_stream path.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Verified at HEAD 71ba84436: ethereum_structured_eip712_enabled() is hardcoded `return false` (lib/firmware/ethereum.c:66) and fsm_msgEthereum712TypesValues (lib/firmware/fsm_msg_ethereum.h:509) fails closed before any of e712_types_values/eip712.c executes. The claim itself concedes "Not exploitable at HEAD"; the retained code (e712_types_values, failMessage, eip712.c parser, stale lib/firmware/ethereum.h, undefined prototypes in include/.../ethereum.h) is unreachable from host input and does not affect the device compile. This is a code-hygiene / regression-risk observation, not a defect reachable from the wire at HEAD, so it does not meet the bar for real=true.

### C3-007 — CONFIRMED — P2: DSProxy path never displays msg->to: a payable attacker contract receives the ETH the screen attributes to a CDP

Unit: `audit:ethereum-contracts`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/ethereum_contracts/makerdao.c:658`.

makerdao_isMakerDAO() never inspects msg->to. In makerdao_confirmMakerDAO() the non-proxy branch (line 663) does put msg->to on screen via confirmSaiProxyCreateAndExecuteAddress(), but the proxy branch (lines 658-661 -> confirmProxyCall, lines 196-206) only validates/confirms the *target* address embedded in the execute(address,bytes) calldata (word 0), and never shows msg->to (the DSProxy) at all. Every other decoder in this unit pins msg->to to a known address (0x, Sablier, Uniswap router, THOR/Maya routers); the DSProxy is user-specific and therefore unpinnable, which is exactly why it must be disclosed. Trace: host sends EthereumSignTx{to=ATTACKER, value=10 ETH, data=execute(0x526af336..., lockAndDraw(TUB_MAINNET, wad))} on chain 1. ethereum_contractHandled: to.size==20, data_total==chunk size, makerdao_isMakerDAO -> isProxyCall (selector 1cff79cd, offset 0x40) -> isMethod lockAndDraw(2 args) true. ethereum_contractConfirmed: confirmProxyCall -> confirmSaiProxyCreateAndExecuteAddress(known mainnet address) returns true with NO screen; makerdao_confirmLockAndDraw2 -> confirmParamIsTub(known mainnet Tub) true with NO screen; single screen 'Create CDP, deposit 10 ETH, and generate N DAI from it?'; then ethereum.c's fee screen 'Send 10 ETH from your wallet...'. msg->to is signed on no screen. needs_confirm=false (ethereum.c:853) suppresses the only generic screen that would have shown the recipient.

Host trace / impact: Compromised host waits for a MakerDAO SCD action (or simply presents one) and substitutes `to` with a contract exposing `function execute(address,bytes) external payable {}` that keeps msg.value. The device narrates a legitimate-looking CDP deposit of 10 ETH into the real Tub via the real SaiProxyCreateAndExecute; the user approves the deposit and the fee screen's 'Send 10 ETH'; the ETH goes to the attacker contract. All other proxy-path variants (lockAndDraw3, lock, createOpenLockAndDraw) are equally usable since each shows the ETH as an expected deposit.

Proposed remediation: In confirmProxyCall() (or at the top of makerdao_confirmMakerDAO's proxy branch) add a mandatory confirm of msg->to, e.g. confirm(..., "MakerDAO", "Via proxy:\n%s", checksummed(msg->to)) so the user can compare it to their own DSProxy. Alternatively refuse the proxy path unless msg->to equals the ProxyRegistry-derived address (not computable on-device), so display is the practical fix. Consider also pinning makerdao_isMakerDAO to chain_id 1/42 like its siblings, since nothing in the decoder is meaningful elsewhere.

Verification: 2/2 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. makerdao_isMakerDAO() (makerdao.c:626-652) checks only has_chain_id, data_total==chunk size, and calldata shape; it never reads msg->to. ethereum_contractHandled() (ethereum_contracts.c:67) only requires to.size==20, so any 20-byte attacker address passes. In makerdao_confirmMakerDAO() (makerdao.c:658-661) the proxy branch calls confirmProxyCall() (196-206), which validates/confirms ONLY the address embedded at calldata word 0 (the execute(address,bytes) target) via confirmSaiProxyCreateAndExecuteAddress(); with the real mainnet 0x526af336... address that returns true silently (140-149). msg->to is never displayed on this path; only the non-proxy branch (line 663) passes msg->to.bytes. makerdao_confirmLockAndDraw2 then confirmParamIsTub (silent for the real Tub) and shows a single 'deposit X ETH ... generate N DAI' screen. Back in ethereum_signing_init (ethereum.c:846-854), a handled contract sets needs_confirm=false, which suppresses the only generic layoutEthereumConfirmTx that would print msg->to (ethereum.c:920-929); the remaining fee screen (layoutEthereumFee, 605-645) prints only value and gas, no recipient. Host input to=ATTACKER, value=10 ETH, data=execute(0x526af336..., lockAndDraw(TUB, wad)) on chain 1 is therefore narrated as a legitimate CDP deposit while the ETH goes to an arbitrary payable attacker contract. Reachable purely from host input; no AdvancedMode gate applies on this path.

Verifier 2: REAL. Reproduced at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/ethereum_contracts/makerdao.c: the only reference to msg->to in the whole file is line 663 (non-proxy branch). makerdao_isMakerDAO (l.620-651) never inspects msg->to; the proxy branch of makerdao_confirmMakerDAO (l.656-661) calls confirmProxyCall (l.196-206), which only validates calldata word 0 via confirmSaiProxyCreateAndExecuteAddress -- which returns true silently for the known mainnet address (l.150-154) with no screen. makerdao_confirmLockAndDraw2 (l.345-367) then calls confirmParamIsTub (silent for known mainnet Tub) and shows a single screen 'Create CDP, deposit %s, and generate %s from it?' where deposit = msg->value. ethereum_contracts.c only gates to.size==20 and data_total==initial chunk size (l.67, l.77) before dispatching to makerdao_isMakerDAO (l.93). ethereum.c l.846-854: contractHandled true -> needs_confirm=false, data_needs_confirm=false, so layoutEthereumConfirmTx (l.928, the only generic recipient disclosure) is skipped; the final fee screen (layoutEthereumFee, l.639-641) prints only 'Send %s from your wallet, paying up to %s for gas?' with no address. Host-controlled input: EthereumSignTx{to=ATTACKER (20 bytes), value=N ETH, chain_id=1, data=execute(0x526af336..., lockAndDraw(TUB_MAINNET, wad))} passes isProxyCall (selector 1cff79cd, offset 64, size 4+96+4+64+28) and isMethod lockAndDraw(2). Result: the tx is signed with msg->to = attacker contract shown on no screen, while the device narrates a CDP deposit of the attached ETH. Reachable from host input with no policy gate.

### C3-008 — CONFIRMED — P3: Proxied bytes length word never validated: DSProxy forwards truncated calldata so the displayed parameters differ from the executed ones

Unit: `audit:ethereum-contracts`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/ethereum_contracts/makerdao.c:184`.

isProxyCall() reads the ABI `bytes` length word of execute(address,bytes) (lines 184-191) but the only test on it is `if (size == 4+3*32+(len-4)) return false;` which is not a validation (for any well-formed proxied call len-4 == count*32 while size == 4+96+(count+1)*32, so the equality never holds; for len<4 the uint32 subtraction wraps). hasProxiedParams() bounds the received chunk, but the length word that DSProxy actually uses to slice `_data` is host-controlled and unconstrained. DSProxy (solc 0.4.23) decodes `bytes memory _data` with calldatacopy(length) and no calldatasize check, and the SCD SaiProxy targets are 0.4.x where reading past calldata yields zeros. Trace: data = execute(SaiProxy, len=4+2*32, give(tub, cup, newOwner)) with the full 3-word body present so hasProxiedParams(3) passes; the device reads getParam(msg,2) and shows 'Move CDP N to 0x<newOwner>' (confirmGive), but the delegatecall receives only give(tub,cup) and SaiProxy sees newOwner == address(0).

Host trace / impact: Host declares a shorter bytes length than the words it appends. Screen shows 'Move CDP 42 to 0xUserAddr?' while the executed give() hands the CDP to address(0) (permanently unrecoverable). Same trick zeroes `wad` in lockAndDraw/draw/wipe so the narrated draw/repayment silently does nothing while the tx is still signed and mined.

Proposed remediation: In isProxyCall(), require the bytes length word to equal exactly 4 + count*32 for the proxied selector (i.e. compute the expected length in isMethod()/hasProxiedParams() and compare `bn_write_uint32(&length) == 4 + arg_count*32`), and delete the meaningless `==` test at lines 189-191.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436 in lib/firmware/ethereum_contracts/makerdao.c. isProxyCall() (lines ~172-195) reads the execute(address,bytes) `bytes` length word into `length` and the only use is `if (size == 4 + 3*32 + (bn_write_uint32(&length) - 4)) return false;`. For a consistent proxied call with N words, size = 4+96+4+N*32+28 = 4+96+N*32+32 while the compared value is 4+96+N*32 — never equal, so a correct length passes; a host-shortened length (e.g. 68 for give(tub,cup,lad) where 3 words are appended, size=228 vs compared 164) also passes. hasProxiedParams() only checks the received chunk size against the selector's expected arg count and never consults the length word; getParam() then reads all N words to render the confirm text (e.g. confirmGive shows the newOwner word). DSProxy slices `_data` by the declared length word, so the delegatecall receives truncated calldata and 0.4.x SaiProxy reads missing args as zero (newOwner=address(0), or wad=0 for draw/wipe/lockAndDraw). The declared length is fully host-controlled via data_initial_chunk, so the displayed parameters can differ from those executed. Defect exists and is reachable from host input.

### C3-009 — REFUTED — P3: Dead surface: unreachable router-label fallback in thor_confirm_deposit_tx and unused ETH_NATIVE macro

Unit: `audit:ethereum-contracts`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/ethereum_contracts/thortx.c:178`.

thor_confirm_deposit_tx() is only reachable via thor_confirmThorTx/thor_confirmMayaTx after thor_isThorchainTx()/thor_isMayachainTx() has already required msg->to to equal thor_router_for_chain() or MAYA_ROUTER (lines 61, 95). The re-derivation at lines 172-180 therefore always takes one of the first two branches; the `else { conf = confStr; }` arm (line 178-180) that would print a raw address for an unknown router is unreachable, and the `router_label` parameter is only consumed on the Maya arm while the Thor arm hardcodes its own string. Separately, ETH_NATIVE (thortx.h:29) has no references anywhere in the tree after the address(0)-only native rule was adopted. Neither is exploitable, but unreachable disclosure code is exactly the kind of thing a future edit 'relies on' without it ever running.

Host trace / impact: None (invariant 5, dead surface only).

Proposed remediation: Collapse lines 172-180 to `conf = router_label;` (pass "Thorchain router"/"Maya router" from the two wrappers) and drop the recomputation; delete the ETH_NATIVE define from thortx.h.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Code facts confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436: thor_confirm_deposit_tx (thortx.c:98) is static and only called from thor_confirmThorTx/thor_confirmMayaTx (thortx.c:284/289), which ethereum_contracts.c:115-116 only invokes after thor_isMayachainTx/thor_isThorchainTx already required msg->to to equal MAYA_ROUTER (chain 1) or thor_router_for_chain(msg). So the re-derivation at :172-180 always hits the Thor or Maya branch and the `else { conf = confStr; }` arm is unreachable; ETH_NATIVE (thortx.h:29) has zero references in lib/, include/, or unittests/. However this is purely dead/redundant code: no host input can reach the unreachable arm, it produces no incorrect display, no signing bypass, no crash, and it compiles fine. The claim itself states "Exploit: None, dead surface only." Not a defect reachable from host input -> real=false (cleanup-only, P3 hygiene).

### C3-010 — CONFIRMED — P1: Struct definition is re-fetched for every member and never compared with the one hashed into typeHash, so nested structs/arrays can be collapsed into an opaque 32-byte leaf and member names/types relabeled on screen

Unit: `audit:eip712`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/eip712_stream.c:891`.

The walk fetches a struct's definition from the host on separate round trips for separate purposes and nothing binds them together. begin_type_hash() streams the encodeType closure (PH_DISCOVER -> PH_STREAM, lines 843-889) and folds it into stack[depth-1].type_hash; then for EVERY member it sends a fresh EthereumTypedDataStructRequest for the same name (advance_after_slot() line 770 / PH_STREAM tail line 887) and, in PH_MEMBER (lines 891-958), takes member_count, the member's name, data_type, size and struct_name straight from that new ack: `f->member_count = ack->members_count; m = &ack->members[f->member_index]; pending_data_type = m->type.data_type; pending_name = m->name`. No digest or fingerprint of the definition used for typeHash is stored, and no check compares the PH_MEMBER ack against it. The only cross-check on a value is eip712_validate_leaf() against the PH_MEMBER-supplied type. Consequently hashStruct = keccak(typeHash_canonical || enc(m1)..enc(mn)) is computed with the CANONICAL typeHash while the per-member type/name that drive validation and the review screen are whatever the host supplies on the later request. Since the review renders every non-string leaf as `<type>: 0x<hex>`, and a bytes32/uint256/address/uint160 leaf all encode to the same 32-byte word as their canonical counterpart, the host can (a) declare a member whose canonical type is a struct or an array (Seaport `offer`/`consideration`, Permit2 `PermitBatch.details[]`, Blur/Uniswap-X order components) as `bytes32` in the PH_MEMBER ack and send the offline-computed hashStruct/array digest as the value -- the walker stores those 32 bytes in the pool exactly where fold_frame() expects the container digest, so the resulting message hash and signature are the canonical, on-chain-valid ones, but the user only ever saw one opaque `bytes32: 0x...` screen instead of the items, amounts and recipients inside; (b) rename members on screen ('spender' -> 'refundAddress', 'deadline' -> 'nonce') and retype them (uint256 -> bytes32, address -> uint160) without changing a single signed byte. The header's stated invariant ('the field NAME comes from the struct definition, which is the same text hashed into encodeType -- so the label and the commitment cannot disagree', lines 528-531) does not hold, because that text is fetched twice and only the first copy is hashed. This is the streaming sibling of the display-vs-signed class the prior audit reported against the JSON parser; the stream path was assumed immune.

Host trace / impact: Host builds a Seaport 1.5 OrderComponents document. Round 1 (typeHash): it answers every StructRequest honestly, so the device computes the canonical typeHash of OrderComponents/OfferItem/ConsiderationItem. Round 2 (PH_MEMBER): for the 'offer' and 'consideration' members it answers the StructRequest for 'OrderComponents' with a definition where those two members are `bytes32` (no array_levels, no struct_name); the device asks for their values and the host returns keccak256(concat(hashStruct(item_i))) computed offline for an offer that gives away the victim's NFT for 0 and a consideration paying the attacker. The device shows 'offer  bytes32: 0x9a1c...' and 'consideration  bytes32: 0x77e0...' and the remaining honest scalar fields; the user approves; the device signs keccak(0x1901 || domainSeparator || keccak(canonicalTypeHash || enc(offerer) || ... || 0x9a1c.. || 0x77e0.. || ...)), which is precisely the canonical Seaport order hash. The signature validates on-chain and the order is fillable. No warning screen, no failure code; the transcript is indistinguishable from a compliant host to the user.

Proposed remediation: Bind the value walk to the definition that was hashed. Cheapest: during PH_STREAM, when the segment for stack[depth-1].name is hashed, also compute keccak of the raw member table (or simply the segment string) and store it in the frame (32 bytes; there are only EIP712_MAX_DEPTH frames); in PH_MEMBER recompute the same digest over the incoming ack and fail('EIP-712 struct definition changed') on mismatch. Equivalent alternative: cache the top frame's member list (name+type, compact form) once per frame instead of re-requesting it per member, so a single ack serves both encodeType and the walk; this also removes one round trip per member. In either form, add a unit test that feeds a different definition for the same name on the second request and asserts the walk fails.

Verification: 2/2 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436 in lib/firmware/eip712_stream.c. begin_type_hash() (PH_DISCOVER/PH_STREAM) hashes host-supplied StructAcks into stack[depth-1].type_hash and stores nothing else about them (no digest/fingerprint; grep for memcmp/fingerprint finds no cross-check). advance_after_slot() and the PH_STREAM tail then re-request the same struct name with phase=PH_MEMBER, and the PH_MEMBER case (~line 891) reads member_count, name, data_type, size, array_levels and struct_name directly from that NEW ack, with no comparison against the definition already folded into type_hash. eip712_stream_on_struct's only checks are identifier validity, spellable type name, duplicate names and slot counts. So a host can answer the PH_MEMBER request with a definition where a canonical struct/array member is declared bytes32 (or any 32-byte-encoding scalar): eip712_validate_leaf accepts bytes32 (size 1..32, exact length), eip712_encode_leaf's BYTES case write_rightpad32 stores the 32 bytes verbatim into pool[slot_base+member_index] -- the same slot fold_frame() would use for a child's hashStruct/array digest -- and fold_frame keccaks canonical type_hash || slots, yielding the canonical message hash and an on-chain-valid signature while the screen showed 'name  bytes32: 0x...'. Renaming/retyping among 32-byte-encoded scalars (uint256<->bytes32, address<->uint160) likewise changes only the displayed label/type, not signed bytes. The header invariant (lines 528-531) is not enforced by code. Reachable purely from host input via the normal EthereumTypedDataStructAck flow; no failure path triggers.

Verifier 2: REAL. Verified at 71ba84436 in lib/firmware/eip712_stream.c. begin_type_hash() (line ~621) drives PH_DISCOVER/PH_STREAM (lines 843-889): each StructAck is folded into e712.hash via hash_segment_from_ack and finalized into stack[depth-1].type_hash; nothing else about those acks is retained (Eip712Frame stores only name/slot_base/member_count/member_index/type_hash; no per-member digest or copy). Then, at the PH_STREAM tail (line 887) and again from advance_after_slot() (lines 769-770) for EVERY subsequent member, request_struct(f->name) is re-sent and the PH_MEMBER branch (lines 891-958) takes f->member_count = ack->members_count, and pending_data_type/has_size/size/pending_name straight from ack->members[f->member_index] with no comparison against the definition already hashed. The only per-ack checks in eip712_stream_on_struct are member-count bound, identifier syntax, type spellability and duplicate names -- all satisfied by a relabeled/retyped definition. eip712_stream_on_value validates only against the rebuilt PH_MEMBER type (validate_leaf: BYTES with size 32 accepts exactly 32 bytes) and eip712_encode_leaf writes into pool[slot_base+member_index], the same slot complete_frame() would fill with a child's fold digest; fold_frame then computes keccak(canonical type_hash || slots). So a host can answer typeHash-round StructRequests honestly and PH_MEMBER-round requests with `offer`/`consideration` declared as bytes32 (no array_levels/struct_name), supply offline hashStruct/array digests as values, and the device shows only `bytes32: 0x..` yet produces the canonical, on-chain-valid hash. Renaming/retyping members (uint256->bytes32, address->uint160 is not possible since ADDRESS requires 20 bytes and UINT encodes left-padded, but uint256<->bytes32 relabeling and name changes are) likewise changes the screen without changing the signed bytes. Reachable purely from host input; no failure path triggers.

### C3-011 — CONFIRMED — P2: Dynamic bytes/string leaves longer than the 352-byte confirm body are hashed in full but only partially displayed (fail-open via 'Cut Off … continue anyway')

Unit: `audit:eip712`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/eip712_stream.c:565`.

eip712_confirm_leaf() formats a whole leaf into one confirm() call: strings up to EIP712_MAX_LEAF (1024) bytes as "%s", everything else as "%s: %s" with a hex buffer of 2+2*1024+1 bytes. The comment at lines 557-565 asserts that 'confirm_helper paginates, so a long dynamic bytes value is disclosed across screens rather than truncated ... nothing signed is ever cut off the screen'. That is not what confirm() does: confirm() (lib/board/confirm_sm.c:645-664) vsnprintf()s the body into `static char strbuf[BODY_CHAR_MAX]` where BODY_CHAR_MAX is 352 (include/keepkey/board/layout.h:76), sets body_truncated, and confirm_helper() (confirm_sm.c:613-627) on SOURCE truncation shows a 'Cut Off' screen reading 'This text is too long to show in full. The rest cannot be displayed. Hold to continue anyway.' and then pages the ALREADY-TRUNCATED body. The user can hold through it, confirm() returns true, and on_value() then absorbs the full ack->value.bytes into the hash (line 1036). So for a `bytes` leaf of >= 172 bytes (9 + 2*len >= 352) or a `string` of >= 352 bytes, the tail of the signed value is never rendered and the operation is not refused -- violating invariant (2) and the unit's own stated exact-byte rule. Note the pagination that DOES exist (RENDER overflow) only helps bodies that fit strbuf.

Host trace / impact: Host declares a message member `data` of dynamic type `bytes` (or `string`) and sends a 400-byte value whose first ~170 bytes are the benign calldata/text the user expects and whose tail carries the payload (e.g. a Gnosis Safe SafeTx.data or a permissioned-relay `string` order note). The device shows 'Cut Off … Hold to continue anyway', then 'data  bytes: 0x<first ~340 hex chars>'. A user who holds through both signs keccak over all 400 bytes. The value's semantics (what the verifying contract will execute) live in the bytes that were never on screen.

Proposed remediation: Fail closed in the stream, not in the generic confirm path: in eip712_confirm_leaf() compute the body length before calling confirm() and refuse (fail('EIP-712 value too long to review') rather than a cancel) any leaf whose formatted body would exceed BODY_CHAR_MAX-1, or render long leaves yourself in chunks of <= (BODY_CHAR_MAX-1) characters with explicit 'part i/n' titles so every hashed byte is drawn before it is absorbed. Then lower EIP712_MAX_LEAF (or the BYTES/STRING validate bound) to the reviewable size so the limit is honest, and fix the comment. Add a unit/emulator test with a 400-byte `bytes` leaf asserting either refusal or that every byte appears on a page.

Verification: 2/2 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Reproduced at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/eip712_stream.c:278 accepts dynamic bytes up to EIP712_MAX_LEAF (1024; include/keepkey/firmware/eip712_stream.h:58) and strings of any printable length up to 1024. eip712_confirm_leaf() (lines 543-577) formats the whole leaf into a single confirm() call ("%s" for string, "%s: %s" with a 2+2*1024+1 hex buffer otherwise). confirm() (lib/board/confirm_sm.c:645-664) calls format_body(), which vsnprintf()s into `static char strbuf[BODY_CHAR_MAX]` with BODY_CHAR_MAX=352 (include/keepkey/board/layout.h:76) and sets body_truncated when needed >= 352 (confirm_sm.c:67-69). confirm_helper() (confirm_sm.c:559-627) on SOURCE truncation shows the 'Cut Off ... Hold to continue anyway' screen and then page_body_confirm()s the already-clipped strbuf; holding returns true. on_value() (eip712_stream.c:1029-1040) then passes the full `bytes`/`len` to eip712_encode_leaf, so the entire value is hashed. No guard rejects a leaf whose formatted body exceeds 352 chars, so a host-supplied dynamic bytes leaf >= ~172 bytes or a string >= 352 bytes is signed with its tail never displayed. The in-file comment claiming confirm_helper paginates rather than truncates is wrong for this path; only RENDER overflow (bodies that fit strbuf) is paginated.

Verifier 2: REAL. Reproduced at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/eip712_stream.c: eip712_validate_leaf accepts dynamic BYTES up to EIP712_MAX_LEAF=1024 (line 278) and STRING of any printable length; protobuf options cap EthereumTypedDataValueAck.value at 1024. eip712_confirm_leaf (545-577) formats the entire leaf into ONE confirm() call ("%s" for string, "%s: %s" with 2+2*len hex for bytes). lib/board/confirm_sm.c: confirm() -> format_body() vsnprintf()s into static strbuf[BODY_CHAR_MAX=352] (layout.h:76) and sets body_truncated when needed >= 352 (lines 67-69). confirm_helper (613-627) on SOURCE truncation shows the "Cut Off ... Hold to continue anyway" screen and then page_body_confirm()s the already-clipped strbuf; it returns true when the user holds -- there is no refusal path. Back in eip712_stream_on_value (1028-1037), a true return leads to eip712_encode_leaf(field, bytes, len, ...) absorbing the full host-supplied value into the hash. So a host-supplied `bytes` leaf >= 172 bytes (9 + 2*len >= 352 after "bytes: 0x" prefix) or a `string` leaf >= 352 bytes is signed in full while only the first ~340 hex chars / ~351 chars are ever rendered. The comments at 557-565 claiming confirm_helper paginates and 'nothing signed is ever cut off the screen' are false: RENDER-overflow pagination only applies to bodies that fit strbuf. Fully reachable from host input via a normal EIP-712 typed-data flow with a dynamic bytes/string member.

### C3-012 — CONFIRMED — P2: eip712_stream_abort() has no callers: a half-walked signing session survives Initialize, ClearSession, Cancel and auto-lock, and the resumed walk signs with no PIN check

Unit: `audit:eip712`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/eip712_stream.c:524`.

The header contract (eip712_stream.h, 'Drop all session state. Called on completion, failure, Initialize and ClearSession -- a half-walked document must never survive into the next one') is not wired. `grep -rn eip712_stream lib include` shows the only callers are the three FSM handlers in fsm_msg_ethereum.h; fsm.c includes the header but never calls eip712_stream_abort(). session_clear() (storage.c:1849) explicitly tears down zcash_signing_abort() and signer slots but not the EIP-712 walk; fsm_msgCancel (fsm_msg_common.h:620) aborts every other signing state machine (ethereum_signing_abort, tendermint_signAbort, eos_signingAbort, zcash_signing_abort) but not this one; fsm_msgInitialize/ClearSession and the screensaver auto-lock (home_sm.c:123 session_clear(true)) leave e712.active and e712.waiting intact. Compounding it, fsm_msgEthereumTypedDataStructAck/ValueAck carry CHECK_INITIALIZED only (fsm_msg_ethereum.h:651-660, no CHECK_PIN), and the REQ_DONE path derives the key via fsm_getDerivedNode() -> storage_getRootNode(), which prompts for passphrase but never re-checks the PIN. So consent gathered for leaves before a teardown is carried across it, and the signature can be produced after the device has locked.

Host trace / impact: User begins signing a typed-data document; the host delivers the domain and the first message members, the user approves them. The device auto-locks (or the host sends Initialize/ClearSession/Cancel, e.g. a wallet reconnect); the user believes the flow ended. Later -- minutes or hours, as long as the device stays powered -- the host sends the remaining EthereumTypedDataValueAck messages. e712.active is still true and waiting == EIP712_WANT_VALUE, so on_value() resumes; the remaining leaves are shown on a device that is nominally locked (no PIN prompt is issued because the ack handlers have no CHECK_PIN and fsm_getDerivedNode does not enforce one), and EIP712_REQ_DONE signs. If the remaining members are ones the user would approve without context (e.g. a single `nonce`), the earlier-approved fields bind to a document finished after the session the user thought was over.

Proposed remediation: Call eip712_stream_abort() from session_clear() (next to zcash_signing_abort(), so auto-lock, PIN failure, Initialize and ClearSession are all covered) and from fsm_msgCancel(). Add CHECK_PIN to fsm_msgEthereumTypedDataStructAck and fsm_msgEthereumTypedDataValueAck so a resumed walk on a locked device re-authenticates before any further screen or the signature. Add an emulator test: begin, one ValueAck, Initialize, then ValueAck -> expect Failure 'Unexpected EIP-712 value'.

Verification: 2/2 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Reproduced at HEAD 71ba84436. `eip712_stream_abort()` (lib/firmware/eip712_stream.c:524) has no callers outside eip712_stream.c's own fail(); grep over lib/ and include/ shows only eip712_stream_begin/on_struct/on_value/next used from fsm_msg_ethereum.h. Teardown paths verified: fsm_msgCancel (fsm_msg_common.h) aborts signing/ethereum/tendermint/eos/zcash but not the EIP-712 walk; fsm_msgInitialize aborts the same set plus session_clear(false); fsm_msgClearSession (fsm.c:354) and the screensaver lock (lib/firmware/home_sm.c:123) call session_clear(true), and session_clear (storage.c:1849) tears down signer slots and zcash_signing_abort() only. None touch `e712`, so e712.active/waiting persist. fsm_msgEthereumTypedDataStructAck/ValueAck (fsm_msg_ethereum.h:651-660) carry only CHECK_INITIALIZED, and eip712_stream_on_value only checks `e712.active && waiting == EIP712_WANT_VALUE`, so a host that sends Cancel/Initialize (or waits for auto-lock) and then continues sending ValueAcks resumes the walk; remaining leaves are confirmed and REQ_DONE derives via fsm_getDerivedNode -> storage_getRootNode (fsm.c:259, storage.c:2491), which prompts passphrase but never PIN. One caveat narrows (not negates) the claim: after a real lock with a PIN set, session_clear_impl falls to `clear:` and sets storage->has_sec=false, so storage_getRootNode returns false and the final signature cannot be produced until the device is unlocked -- but the remaining leaves are still displayed and consent gathered on the locked device with no PIN prompt. After Cancel or Initialize (session_clear(false) keeps the PIN cached) the signature over a document the user believed cancelled completes fully. Reachable purely from host input; header contract in eip712_stream.h:181 is not wired.

Verifier 2: REAL. Core defect confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436, with one part of the claim overstated.

CONFIRMED: eip712_stream_abort() (lib/firmware/eip712_stream.c:524) has zero callers outside eip712_stream.c itself. grep over lib/ and include/ shows the only external entry points are eip712_stream_begin/on_struct/on_value/next in fsm_msg_ethereum.h:550-659. fsm_msgInitialize (fsm_msg_common.h:1-12) aborts signing/ethereum/tendermint/eos + session_clear(false); fsm_msgCancel (fsm_msg_common.h:620-631) aborts setup/signing/authenticator/ethereum/tendermint/eos/zcash; session_clear (storage.c:1849-1868) tears down signer slots + zcash_signing_abort; none touch e712. The header contract (eip712_stream.h:181, 'Called on completion, failure, Initialize and ClearSession') is not wired.

Reachable reproduction (host input only): EthereumSignTypedData (CHECK_PIN passes, e712.active=true) -> host feeds StructAck/ValueAck, user approves N leaves -> host sends Cancel (device replies 'Aborted') or Initialize -> e712.active still true, waiting still EIP712_WANT_VALUE -> host sends the remaining EthereumTypedDataValueAck; fsm_msgEthereumTypedDataValueAck (fsm_msg_ethereum.h:657) has only CHECK_INITIALIZED; eip712_stream_on_value accepts because active && waiting==WANT_VALUE (eip712_stream.c:967); EIP712_REQ_DONE (fsm_msg_ethereum.h:567-611) derives via fsm_getDerivedNode -> storage_getRootNode (storage.c:2491) which checks passphrase only, and signs. Since Cancel/Initialize use clear_pin=false, pinCached and storage.sec survive, so the signature IS produced. Consent gathered before the Cancel/Initialize is bound to a document finished afterwards.

OVERSTATED: the auto-lock / ClearSession branch. Both call session_clear(clear_pin=true) (home_sm.c:123, fsm.c:356), and session_clear_impl on a PIN-protected wallet falls to the 'clear:' label (storage.c:1938-1942): has_sec=false, sec memzero'd. storage_getRootNode then returns false at line 2495 and REQ_DONE fails with 'Device not initialized or passphrase request cancelled' -- no signature after a lock. The half-walked state DOES survive the lock (remaining leaves can still be rendered/confirmed on the lock screen, and the stale walk lingers in RAM), but 'the signature can be produced after the device has locked' is false for a PIN-protected device. The Cancel/Initialize resume path is the real, exploitable part; severity is closer to P3 than P2.

### C3-013 — REFUTED — P3: Withdrawn JSON EIP-712 parser (eip712.c, ~1100 lines) is still compiled and linked into the device image behind a hardcoded false gate

Unit: `audit:eip712`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/eip712.c:1014`.

ethereum_structured_eip712_enabled() returns a constant false (ethereum.c:66) and fsm_msgEthereum712TypesValues (fsm_msg_ethereum.h:509) refuses before any parse, so encode()/parseType()/parseVals()/dsConfirm()/marshallDsVals() and the tiny-json object pool it depends on are unreachable from the wire, yet lib/firmware/CMakeLists.txt:28 still compiles eip712.c into the firmware, ethereum.c still carries e712_types_values() and the failMsgReturn[] table, and eip712.h still exports the encoder API with file-static state (udefList[10] pointers, ds* pointers, nameForValue). The prior audit found two real defects in this file (dsConfirm 40/10-char truncation vs full-string hashing at line 576; unsorted encodeType at line 394) and dismissed both only because the gate is closed; neither was fixed. Under invariant (5) this is dead surface that (a) consumes flash and .bss on a device whose comments say the linker gap is measured in hundreds of bytes, (b) keeps known display-vs-signed bugs one flipped boolean away from production, and (c) doubles the audit surface for every future EIP-712 change.

Host trace / impact: Not directly exploitable at HEAD. Risk is regression: any commit that flips ethereum_structured_eip712_enabled() (or a merge that resolves the develop/alpha conflict the wrong way -- the git history shows exactly such wholesale restores, e.g. 384ff3a47) re-exposes the truncated-domain-name and unsorted-typeHash defects without any code in eip712.c changing.

Proposed remediation: Delete lib/firmware/eip712.c, include/keepkey/firmware/eip712.h, unittests/firmware/eip712.cpp, e712_types_values()/failMsgReturn[] in ethereum.c, and the fsm_msgEthereum712TypesValues body (keep a two-line handler that returns Failure_Other so the wire symbol still answers), and drop eip712.c from lib/firmware/CMakeLists.txt. If the tiny-json pool has no other user, remove it too. Record the flash/.bss delta in the release evidence.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436: ethereum_structured_eip712_enabled() returns constant false (lib/firmware/ethereum.c:66) and fsm_msgEthereum712TypesValues (lib/firmware/fsm_msg_ethereum.h:509) fails closed before any parse, so encode()/parseType()/parseVals()/dsConfirm() in eip712.c are unreachable from host input. The claim itself concedes "Not directly exploitable at HEAD" -- it is a dead-code/hygiene/regression-risk observation, not a defect reachable from the wire and not a build-portability failure. Does not meet the bar for real=true.

### C3-014 — CONFIRMED — P2: Certified ClearSign path never runs the priority-fee binding it claims to require

Unit: `audit:solana`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_solana.h:949`.

solana_confirmPriorityFee() (fsm_msg_solana.h:58-90) is gated on `tx_review == SOL_TX_REVIEW_VERIFIED` (line 949), but a certified transaction can never be VERIFIED: solana_schemaApplies() (solana.c:950) only matches an instruction whose type is SOL_INSTR_UNKNOWN, and the parser sets *has_unknown for every UNKNOWN instruction (solana.c:285-586), so solana_parseLegacyTx/solana_parseVersionedTx return OPAQUE (solana.c:650, 771). The comment directly above the gate says the check 'is required for every fully verified path, including certified schemas' (introduced in dee5d1014), so this is a regression-by-construction: the certified branch (line 822-831) shows only the raw ComputeBudget screens from solana_confirmInstruction ('Set compute unit price to N?' in micro-lamports, 'Set compute unit limit to N?') and never the fee payer or the bounded 'Max priority fee X SOL' figure. The prior audit's P1 fix (blind_only) did not touch this sibling gap: certified/runtime-schema flows get a weaker fee disclosure than an ordinary verified transfer, with no blind-sign warning to compensate.

Host trace / impact: Host holds any legitimate certified schema payload+signature+certificate (they are reusable by design). It builds a message containing the schema-described instruction plus ComputeBudget SetComputeUnitPrice = 1,000,000,000,000 micro-lamports/CU and SetComputeUnitLimit = 1,400,000 (or omits the limit). The device shows the schema screens, then 'Set compute unit price to 1000000000000?' and a plain 'Sign this Solana transaction?'. The user is never shown 'Fee payer <victim>' or 'Max priority fee 1400000.000000000 SOL'. Approving drains the fee payer's SOL (up to its entire balance) as a priority fee, charged even if the schema instruction fails. The same tx sent WITHOUT the certified material would show the bounded fee screens.

Proposed remediation: Run solana_confirmPriorityFee(&parsed) on every path that ends in a plain 'Sign this Solana transaction?' with no blind-sign warning, i.e. `if ((tx_review == SOL_TX_REVIEW_VERIFIED || certified || runtime_schema) && !solana_confirmPriorityFee(&parsed))`. Add a unit/FSM test that a certified schema tx carrying SetComputeUnitPrice produces the 'Fee payer' and 'Max priority fee' screens.

Verification: 2/2 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. Certified path (fsm_msg_solana.h:754-766) requires solana_schemaApplies(), which only matches an instruction with type SOL_INSTR_UNKNOWN (solana.c:950); every UNKNOWN instruction sets *has_unknown (solana.c:285-586), and both solana_parseLegacyTx (solana.c:650) and solana_parseVersionedTx (solana.c:771) return SOL_TX_REVIEW_OPAQUE when has_unknown is set. Therefore tx_review is always OPAQUE whenever certified==true, and the priority-fee gate at fsm_msg_solana.h:949 (`tx_review == SOL_TX_REVIEW_VERIFIED && !solana_confirmPriorityFee(&parsed)`) is dead for certified transactions despite its comment claiming it covers certified schemas. The certified branch (line 822-831) only runs solana_confirmSchemaTransaction, which for ComputeBudget instructions shows the raw 'Set compute unit price to %llu?' / 'Set compute unit limit to %llu?' screens (lines 424-432), then goes directly to the final 'Sign this Solana transaction?' with no fee-payer screen, no bounded 'Max priority fee' SOL figure, and no blind-sign warning. Host-reachable: reuse any valid certified schema payload/signature/certificate and append attacker-chosen SetComputeUnitPrice/Limit instructions; the same tx without certified material would be VERIFIED and show the bounded fee screens. Same gap applies to the runtime_schema branch.

Verifier 2: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. solana_schemaApplies() (lib/firmware/solana.c:950) requires the matched instruction to be non-external SOL_INSTR_UNKNOWN; every such assignment in the instruction parser (solana.c:285-586) sets *has_unknown=true, and solana_parseLegacyTx/solana_parseVersionedTx then return SOL_TX_REVIEW_OPAQUE (solana.c:650 and the versioned equivalent). The certified branch in fsm_msg_solana.h (~line 741) rejects only MALFORMED, so every certified ClearSign tx reaches the confirm section with tx_review==OPAQUE, takes `if (certified)` (line ~822) which runs solana_confirmSchemaTransaction -> solana_confirmInstruction, showing only raw "Set compute unit price to %llu?" / "Set compute unit limit to %llu?" (lines 424-432) and no blind-sign warning. The gate at line 949 (`tx_review == SOL_TX_REVIEW_VERIFIED && !solana_confirmPriorityFee(&parsed)`) is therefore unreachable for certified txs, so the fee payer + "Max priority fee" screens (lines 58-90) never appear, contradicting the comment at 947-948. Host-reachable: a reusable certified schema payload/signature/certificate plus arbitrary ComputeBudget SetComputeUnitPrice/Limit instructions in raw_tx yields signing with only micro-lamport raw values displayed; the same tx without certified material would be VERIFIED and show the bounded fee.

### C3-015 — REFUTED — P3: Unreachable token/ATA/recipient-owner helpers and inadequate blind_only confirm screens retained in firmware

Unit: `audit:solana`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/solana.c:1121`.

After the prior audit's blind_only fix, several pieces of the unit are dead in firmware but still compiled into it: (a) solana_findKnownToken, SOL_KNOWN_TOKENS, solana_findTokenInfo, solana_token_info_trusted, solana_findTokenRecipientOwner, solana_deriveAssociatedTokenAddress (with a 256-iteration SHA256+ge25519_unpack loop), SOL_PDA_MARKER and solana_parseTx (solana.c:80-90, 1010-1012, 1121-1227) are referenced only from unittests; no fsm path consults token_info or token_recipient_owner (the display code at fsm_msg_solana.h:190-206 explicitly refuses to). (b) solana_confirmInstruction cases SOL_INSTR_SYSTEM_CREATE_ACCOUNT (fsm_msg_solana.h:115-120), SOL_INSTR_TOKEN_APPROVE (216-222) and SOL_INSTR_TOKEN_SET_AUTHORITY (229-234) are unreachable: those types always set blind_only+force_opaque (solana.c:249, 314, 358, 378), so they never appear in a VERIFIED tx and solana_schemaApplies rejects them (solana.c:993). The retained screens are the inadequate ones the audit flagged (no destination/owner for CreateAccount, no mint for Approve, 'None' indistinguishable from all-zero for SetAuthority). (c) The `case SOL_INSTR_UNKNOWN: default:` arm (445-453) is likewise unreachable since no confirm path ever receives an UNKNOWN or external instruction.

Host trace / impact: No direct exploit at HEAD. Risk is latent: a future change that clears blind_only for one of these types (or adds a schema match against a non-UNKNOWN type) would immediately re-expose the under-disclosing screens the previous audit rated P1, and the unused token_info/recipient-owner code path invites someone to wire host-asserted symbols back into the display. The dead ATA-derivation loop also costs flash on a ROM-tight target.

Proposed remediation: Delete the unreferenced helpers and their declarations (or move them behind the unittest build only), delete the three unreachable confirm cases (replace with an explicit `return false` in default so any future reach fails closed), and remove the now-unused token_info/token_recipient_owner proto fields from the .options caps if they are not going to be consumed.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Verified at HEAD 71ba84436 that the audit's facts are accurate but the finding is not a reachable defect. In lib/firmware/solana.c the CreateAccount (l.249-250), unchecked SPL transfer (314-315), Approve (358-359) and SetAuthority (378-379) branches set pi->blind_only=true and *force_opaque=true; both solana_parseLegacyTx (l.650) and solana_parseVersionedTx (l.771) then return SOL_TX_REVIEW_OPAQUE, so fsm_msg_solana.h only calls solana_confirmInstruction on VERIFIED txs (l.835) or via solana_confirmSchemaTransaction (l.543), and solana_schemaApplies (solana.c l.993-994) refuses any co-instruction that is external, blind_only or SOL_INSTR_UNKNOWN. Hence the SOL_INSTR_SYSTEM_CREATE_ACCOUNT / TOKEN_APPROVE / TOKEN_SET_AUTHORITY / UNKNOWN-default arms in solana_confirmInstruction cannot be reached from any host-supplied message, and solana_findKnownToken / solana_deriveAssociatedTokenAddress / solana_findTokenRecipientOwner / solana_token_info_trusted / solana_parseTx have no callers outside unittests. The claim is dead-code/latent-risk hygiene (the audit itself states "No direct exploit at HEAD"); no under-disclosing screen or token-info path is host-reachable, and no build-flag portability failure is asserted. Per the rubric (defect must be reachable from host input at HEAD), real=false.

### C3-016 — CONFIRMED — P2: fee_amount and gas are committed to the THORChain sign-doc but never displayed; final screen only says 'Additional network fees apply'

Unit: `audit:thorchain`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_thorchain.h:329`.

thorchain_signTxInit() (lib/firmware/thorchain.c:89-97) hashes msg.fee_amount and msg.gas into the amino sign-doc ("fee":{"amount":[{"amount":"<fee_amount>","denom":"rune"}],"gas":"<gas>"}). The only screen that could disclose them is the final confirm at fsm_msg_thorchain.h:329-333, which prints chain_id, account_number and sequence and the fixed phrase 'Additional network fees apply' -- fee_amount/gas are not rendered anywhere. The HEAD fix (71ba84436, 'account/sequence on 3 more sign screens') closed the prior audit's account_number/sequence sibling (2026-09-01 P3 cosmos-tendermint) on this screen but left the fee, which -- unlike account/sequence -- is a fund-moving field. The Cosmos sibling handler already discloses it (fsm_msg_cosmos.h:509 'It includes a fee of %u uATOM and %u gas'). fee_amount is uint32 in base units, so up to 4,294,967,295 (~42.9 RUNE) can be attached per transaction unseen; THORChain's ante handler deducts the declared fee from the signer. fsm_msg_mayachain.h:311-316 has the identical gap.

Host trace / impact: Compromised host sends ThorchainSignTx with fee_amount=4294967295, gas=200000 and an ordinary MsgSend of 1 RUNE to the user's own address. Device shows 'Send 1 RUNE to thor1...', 'Asset rune', then 'Sign this RUNE transaction on thorchain-1? Additional network fees apply. Account N, sequence M.' User approves; the signature commits to the 42.9 RUNE fee, which is deducted from the account on broadcast. Repeated per sequence number until the balance is drained.

Proposed remediation: Render fee_amount (formatted with 8 decimals as RUNE) and gas on the final SignTx confirm, exactly as fsm_msg_cosmos.h:509 does for uATOM, e.g. 'It includes a fee of %s RUNE and %u gas.' Apply the same change to fsm_msg_mayachain.h (CACAO, 10 decimals). Optionally refuse fee_amount above a sane ceiling.

Verification: 2/2 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Verified at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/thorchain.c:89-97 commits msg.fee_amount and msg.gas into the amino sign-doc ("fee":{"amount":[{"amount":"%u","denom":"rune"}],"gas":"%u"}). lib/firmware/fsm_msg_thorchain.h:85-86 only checks has_fee_amount/has_gas; grep shows no other reference to fee_amount/gas in the handler, and the final confirm at :329-333 renders only chain_id, account_number, sequence and the fixed text "Additional network fees apply". So a host-supplied fee_amount up to UINT32_MAX (~42.9 RUNE) is signed with no disclosure. The Cosmos sibling (fsm_msg_cosmos.h:509) does display fee/gas, confirming this is an omission, and fsm_msg_mayachain.h:311-316 has the identical gap. Reachable from ordinary host ThorchainSignTx input.

Verifier 2: REAL. Verified at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/thorchain.c:89-97 (thorchain_signTxInit) hashes msg.fee_amount as "fee":{"amount":[{"amount":"%u","denom":"rune"}]} and msg.gas into the amino sign-doc, so the signature commits to host-supplied fee. In lib/firmware/fsm_msg_thorchain.h the only references to fee_amount/gas are the has_ presence checks at lines 85-86; the final confirm at 329-333 prints chain_id, account_number, sequence and the fixed string "Additional network fees apply" — fee_amount/gas are never rendered on any screen. fsm_msg_mayachain.h:311-316 has the identical gap. Contrast fsm_msg_cosmos.h:508-512, which displays "It includes a fee of %u uATOM and %u gas". Reachable from host input: any ThorchainSignTx with fee_amount up to UINT32_MAX (~42.9 RUNE) is signed with the user only seeing the MsgSend amount.

### C3-017 — CONFIRMED — P3: ThorchainGetAddress: bip32 path-string failure sends Failure but does not return; flow continues with a blanked path and emits a second (Address) response

Unit: `audit:thorchain`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_thorchain.h:40`.

In fsm_msgThorchainGetAddress the show_display branch calls bip32_node_to_string()/bip32_path_to_string(); on failure it memsets node_str, memzeros node, calls fsm_sendFailure(...'Can't create Bip32 Path String') and layoutHome() -- but has no `return` (lines 45-49). Execution falls through to tendermint_pathMismatched(), confirm_ethereum_address(node_str="", resp->address) and finally msg_write(ThorchainAddress). This violates fail-closed (the formatting failure is blanked and the screen proceeds with an empty path title) and produces two replies to one request (Failure followed by ThorchainAddress), desynchronising the host protocol. Reachable: NODE_STRING_LENGTH is 50 (include/keepkey/firmware/coins.h:58) and bip32_path_to_string needs up to 12 bytes per hardened component, so any non-standard path of ~5+ hardened components (address_n max_count 10) fails both formatters. Identical fall-through exists in fsm_msg_mayachain.h:48, fsm_msg_cosmos.h:39, fsm_msg_osmosis.h:61 (outside this unit, same pattern).

Host trace / impact: Host sends ThorchainGetAddress{address_n=[2147483647',2147483647',2147483647',2147483647',2147483647',0], show_display=true}. Device sends Failure, then still shows the address screen with an empty derivation-path title; if the user approves, the device also sends ThorchainAddress. A host expecting one reply mis-parses the stream; the user is shown an address with no path context.

Proposed remediation: Add `return;` after layoutHome() in the bip32 failure branch (fsm_msg_thorchain.h:49), matching every other failure branch in the function; apply the same one-line fix to the cosmos/mayachain/osmosis siblings. Consider also refusing (rather than memset-blanking) the path in the SignTx final confirm at lines 320-327 for consistency with the fail-closed invariant.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Verified at HEAD 71ba84436 in lib/firmware/fsm_msg_thorchain.h:40-50: the show_display branch calls fsm_sendFailure('Can't create Bip32 Path String') + layoutHome() with no `return`, so execution continues to tendermint_pathMismatched(), confirm_ethereum_address(node_str="" (memset), resp->address) and msg_write(ThorchainAddress) -> two replies to one request and an address screen with a blank path title. Reachable from host input: bip32_node_to_string (lib/firmware/coins.c:486) returns false for any address_n_count != 5 or path-mismatched path; bip32_path_to_string (coins.c:250) fails when the formatted path exceeds NODE_STRING_LENGTH=50 (coins.h:58) -- each 2147483647' component is 12 chars, so e.g. address_n=[0x7fffffff'x5, 0] (6 entries, within max_count:10 per messages-thorchain.options) overflows and fails both formatters. No other guard prevents the fall-through.

### C3-018 — CONFIRMED — P3: Lowercase 's'/'a' memo-type prefix match mislabels non-swap THORChain memo types (e.g. 'secure-') as 'Thorchain swap'

Unit: `audit:thorchain`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/thorchain.c:356`.

thorchain_parseConfirmMemo() treats any memo whose first field begins with 's' (or 'a') as a SWAP (ADD). THORNode's StringToTxType lower-cases the type field before lookup, so lowercase memo types such as 'secure-:ETH.ETH:<dest>' (secured-asset withdrawal to an L1 address) are valid on-chain but are matched by `*fields[0] == 's'` and confirmed on-device under the title 'Thorchain swap' with 'Confirm swap asset ETH on chain ETH / Confirm to <dest> / Confirm limit none', and the parser returns CONFIRMED so the raw memo is never paged. The tail of fields[0] after the matched prefix (e.g. 'ecure-') is never displayed. Asset, destination and amount are still disclosed, so the money goes where the screen says, but the operation verb -- and the fact that a different (secured-asset) balance is being withdrawn rather than the deposited coin swapped -- is misrepresented. The MAYA sibling avoids this by paging the full raw memo after any summary; the THORChain handler at fsm_msg_thorchain.h:251-276 does not, despite the thorchain.h comment stating native handlers 'page this as the authoritative disclosure after their best-effort structured summary'.

Host trace / impact: Host builds MsgDeposit{amount=1e8, asset='ETH-ETH', memo='secure-:ETH.ETH:0xattacker'}. Device shows 'Send 1 ETH-ETH to <signer>', 'Asset ETH-ETH', then 'Thorchain swap: Confirm swap asset ETH on chain ETH', 'Confirm to 0xattacker', 'Confirm limit none'. A user believing this is a swap whose output they will see priced/limited is actually authorising an unconditional withdrawal of their secured ETH to that address; the 'secure-' verb and full memo bytes are never shown.

Proposed remediation: Match memo types exactly and case-insensitively against the full first field (strcasecmp against 'swap'/'s'/'=' and 'add'/'a'/'+', 'withdraw'/'wd'/'-') instead of single-character/4-char prefix tests, returning THORCHAIN_MEMO_UNPARSED for anything else so it is paged raw. Additionally (or alternatively), have the THORChain deposit/tx handlers call thorchain_confirm_full_memo() after a CONFIRMED structured summary, as the MAYA handler does and as the header comment already claims.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/thorchain.c:356 matches `*fields[0] == 's'` (and :446 `*fields[0] == 'a'`) with no check that the type field is exactly "s"/"swap". Host-supplied MsgDeposit memo "secure-:ETH.ETH:0xattacker" passes the NUL check, splits into 3 fields, fields[1] contains '.', enters the SWAP branch (nfields 3 <= 9, no '|' so the full-raw-memo page is skipped), shows only "Confirm swap asset ETH on chain ETH" / "Confirm to 0xattacker" / "Confirm limit none", and returns THORCHAIN_MEMO_CONFIRMED at :442. fsm_msg_thorchain.h:249-275 pages raw memo bytes only on UNPARSED, so the "ecure-" tail and the actual operation verb are never displayed. Asset/dest are disclosed, so impact is verb/operation misrepresentation (P3 as claimed), but the defect exists and is reachable from host input.

### C3-019 — CONFIRMED — P2: MAYAChain signing session (private-key copy + initialized flag) survives Cancel/Initialize/ClearSession/auto-lock; MsgAck has no PIN gate

Unit: `audit:mayachain`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_mayachain.h:118`.

mayachain_signTxInit() (lib/firmware/mayachain.c:66-68) copies the derived HDNode, including the private key, into the static CONFIDENTIAL `node` and sets `initialized=true`. The only scrub is mayachain_signAbort(), and it is called ONLY from inside the mayachain handlers themselves. The always-on teardown paths never call it: fsm_msgInitialize (lib/firmware/fsm_msg_common.h:1-8) and fsm_msgCancel (:620-629) call signing_abort/ethereum_signing_abort/tendermint_signAbort/eos_signingAbort/zcash_signing_abort but not mayachain_signAbort; session_clear() (lib/firmware/storage.c:1849-1863, used by ClearSession, the screensaver auto-lock in home_sm.c:123 and the PIN-failure path in pin_sm.c:262) calls fsm_clearDerivedNode() and zcash_signing_abort() but not mayachain_signAbort. tendermint_signAbort() (signtx_tendermint.c:603) only scrubs its own statics. fsm_msgMayachainMsgAck (:118-121) gates only on CHECK_PARAM(mayachain_signingIsInited()) -- no CHECK_PIN, no CHECK_INITIALIZED -- so the flow stays resumable after every one of those teardown events.
