This is the exact sibling of the Orchard-key fix the prior audit landed in session_clear() ('paths nobody enumerated ... both left the key live'); that fix covered zcash only. Two invariants break: (4) secret hygiene -- the secp256k1 private key of the signing path stays in SRAM indefinitely after the user cancels or the device locks; and the PIN boundary -- a locked device will still page the ConfirmOutput/SignTx screens and emit a valid MayachainSignedTx.

Host trace / impact: 1. Victim's host app starts a MAYAChain send (MayachainSignTx passes CHECK_PIN, device replies MayachainMsgRequest), then the host aborts (sends Cancel, or reconnects and sends Initialize), or the user simply walks away and the screensaver locks the device (session_clear(true)). The device now looks locked/idle, but mayachain.c still holds `node` with the private key and `initialized==true`. 2. Anyone with USB + button access to the locked device sends MayachainMsgAck{send:{to_address:<attacker maya addr>, amount:<all>, denom:'cacao'}}. The handler does not ask for the PIN; it walks the confirm screens (attacker presses the button) and returns a valid signature over the attacker's transfer, using account_number/sequence/chain_id from the victim's earlier SignTx. The PIN, which is the sole protection against a physically present attacker, is bypassed for the lifetime of the stale session (until power-cycle). Same shape exists in thorchain.c/fsm_msg_thorchain.h and osmosis (out of this unit's scope but should be fixed together).

Proposed remediation: Add mayachain_signAbort() (and the thorchain/osmosis siblings) to the always-on teardown: call it from fsm_msgInitialize and fsm_msgCancel in fsm_msg_common.h, and -- so the screensaver auto-lock and PIN-failure paths are covered too -- from session_clear() in storage.c next to zcash_signing_abort(), with no-op stubs under the BITCOIN_ONLY #else in fsm.c (fsm.c:388-391 pattern). Additionally add CHECK_PIN to fsm_msgMayachainMsgAck so that a cached-PIN loss always re-prompts before a message is committed to the sighash.

Verification: 2/2 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. (1) lib/firmware/mayachain.c:66-68 `mayachain_signTxInit` sets `initialized=true` and memcpy's the full HDNode (private key) into static CONFIDENTIAL `node`; `mayachain_signTxFinalize` (mayachain.c:236) signs with `node.private_key`. The only scrub is `mayachain_signAbort()` (mayachain.c:246-252), and a repo-wide grep shows it is called exclusively from lib/firmware/fsm_msg_mayachain.h -- zero callers in fsm.c, fsm_msg_common.h, storage.c, home_sm.c, or pin_sm.c. (2) Teardown paths do not reach it: fsm_msgInitialize (fsm_msg_common.h:1-12) and fsm_msgCancel (:620-629) call signing_abort/ethereum_signing_abort/tendermint_signAbort/eos_signingAbort/zcash_signing_abort only; fsm_msgClearSession (fsm.c:354-358) -> session_clear(true) (storage.c:1849-1865) calls fsm_clearDerivedNode (which memzeros only fsm_derived_node, fsm.c:111-113), signed_metadata_clear_signers, and zcash_signing_abort -- not mayachain. session_clear is also the screensaver auto-lock path (home_sm.c:123) and PIN-failure path (pin_sm.c:262). (3) fsm_msgMayachainMsgAck (fsm_msg_mayachain.h:118-121) gates only on CHECK_PARAM(mayachain_signingIsInited()) with no CHECK_PIN/CHECK_INITIALIZED; CHECK_PIN is only in GetAddress/SignTx. The board dispatcher (lib/board/messages.c:182-199 `dispatch`) has no lock/PIN gate -- it parses and calls process_func unconditionally, and MayachainMsgAck is in messagemap.def:74 for the non-BITCOIN_ONLY build (fsm.c:376, CMakeLists.txt:45). So after SignTx (PIN-gated) followed by Cancel/Initialize/ClearSession/auto-lock, a host can send MayachainMsgAck with attacker-chosen to_address/amount and, with button presses, obtain a valid MayachainSignedTx from the locked device with no PIN prompt, and the private key remains in SRAM until power-cycle. Reachable from host input.

Verifier 2: REAL. Reproduced at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/mayachain.c:66-75 mayachain_signTxInit() sets initialized=true and memcpy()s the full HDNode (private key) into static CONFIDENTIAL `node`; the only scrub is mayachain_signAbort() (mayachain.c:246-252), referenced solely from fsm_msg_mayachain.h (grep over lib/ and include/ shows no other caller). fsm_msgInitialize (fsm_msg_common.h:1-12) and fsm_msgCancel (:620-629) call signing_abort/ethereum_signing_abort/tendermint_signAbort/eos_signingAbort/zcash_signing_abort only; session_clear() (storage.c:1849-1863, used by ClearSession, home_sm.c:123 auto-lock with clear_pin=true, and pin_sm.c) calls fsm_clearDerivedNode/signed_metadata_clear_signers/zcash_signing_abort only. tendermint_signAbort (signtx_tendermint.c:603-610) scrubs only its own statics. fsm_msgMayachainMsgAck (fsm_msg_mayachain.h:118-121) is reachable from host input via messagemap.def:74 and is gated only by CHECK_PARAM(mayachain_signingIsInited()) -- the only CHECK_PIN/CHECK_INITIALIZED in the file are at lines 5-7 (GetAddress) and 82-83 (SignTx); MsgAck proceeds through confirm screens to mayachain_signTxFinalize and msg_write(MayachainSignedTx) at :323-340 with no PIN re-check, and there is no dispatcher-level lock gate in fsm.c. So after Cancel/Initialize/ClearSession/auto-lock the key stays in SRAM and a physically present attacker can obtain a valid signature over their own send without the PIN.

### C3-020 — CONFIRMED — P3: GetAddress keeps going after sending a Failure: blank path label shown, address confirmed, second response written

Unit: `audit:mayachain`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_mayachain.h:45`.

In fsm_msgMayachainGetAddress the show_display branch (:39-50) handles a bip32_node_to_string()/bip32_path_to_string() failure by memset(node_str,0), memzero(node), fsm_sendFailure(FirmwareError) and layoutHome() -- but there is no `return`. Execution falls through to the path-mismatch warning, then confirm_ethereum_address(node_str /* now "" */, resp->address), then `resp->has_address = true; msg_write(MayachainAddress)`. So on a formatting failure the device (a) emits Failure, (b) still shows the address screen with an empty derivation-path label, and (c) emits a second, successful MayachainAddress reply for the same request. This violates invariant (2): a formatting failure must abort, never blank. bip32_path_to_string() writes into NODE_STRING_LENGTH=50 bytes (coins.c:250-274); ten hardened components of 0x7fffffff need ~121 bytes, so the host controls the trigger. Identical code exists in fsm_msgThorchainGetAddress (fsm_msg_thorchain.h:39-50).

Host trace / impact: Host sends MayachainGetAddress{address_n=[2147483647',...x10], show_display:true}. bip32_node_to_string fails (non-standard path), bip32_path_to_string overflows 50 bytes and returns false. Device sends Failure, then displays 'WARNING wrong path' and an address-confirmation screen whose path label is empty, and if the user (expecting an address check) confirms, writes MayachainAddress. A wallet that pairs a displayed path with the address (the whole point of show_display for a non-standard path) shows the user an address with no path, and the host receives two replies for one request, which can desynchronise the transport state machine.

Proposed remediation: Add `return;` after layoutHome() inside the failure block (both mayachain and thorchain GetAddress), matching every other failure branch in the handler.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436 in lib/firmware/fsm_msg_mayachain.h:39-50. Inside the show_display branch, the combined failure of bip32_node_to_string() and bip32_path_to_string() does memset(node_str)/memzero(node)/fsm_sendFailure(FirmwareError)/layoutHome() with no `return`; execution continues to tendermint_pathMismatched, confirm_ethereum_address(node_str="" , resp->address), then resp->has_address=true and msg_write(MayachainAddress). Trigger is host-controlled: messages-mayachain.options allows MayachainGetAddress.address_n max_count:10; bip32_node_to_string (coins.c:486) returns false for any count other than 5 (whole_account=false), and bip32_path_to_string (coins.c:250) returns false when the formatted path exceeds NODE_STRING_LENGTH=50 (coins.h:58) -- ten hardened 2147483647 components is ~121 bytes. Result: Failure emitted, address confirm screen shown with empty path label, and a second MayachainAddress reply if the user confirms. fsm_getDerivedNode has no prior gate on the count/path. Same pattern in fsm_msg_thorchain.h. Severity P3 reasonable (requires user confirmation; address itself is correct, but path label is blank and two responses per request).

### C3-021 — REFUTED — P3: Dead mayachain_parseConfirmMemo() retained; ADD branch signs affiliate/fee unseen and WITHDRAW bps accepts non-digit fields -- siblings thorchain.c fixed

Unit: `audit:mayachain`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/mayachain.c:254`.

The prior audit (P3 mayachain) asked for this function to be deleted or fully ported to the THORChain design. HEAD did neither: it added `nfields > 6` to the SWAP branch and `nfields > 5` to ADD, but the function still (a) has no caller in any signing path (only include/keepkey/firmware/mayachain.h:38, unittests/firmware/mayachain.cpp:225 and two comments in fsm_msg_mayachain.h reference it) -- invariant (5); (b) in the ADD branch (:377-397) accepts up to 5 fields, displays only asset/chain and pool (fields[2]) and returns true while fields[3] (affiliate) and fields[4] (fee bps) are never shown -- thorchain.c:445-480 shows both; (c) in the WITHDRAW branch (:413) runs atoi() on fields[2] without the all-digits guard thorchain.c:499-509 added, so '1HELLO' or '50abc' renders as '0.01%'/'0.50%' while the unmutated field is what is signed; (d) still returns a bare bool that conflates 'unrecognised' with 'user refused', which is the reason fsm_msg_mayachain.h:252-263 refuses to call it. The live path is safe because it pages the raw memo through thorchain_confirm_full_memo(); the risk is that a future caller wires this exported, header-declared helper in (as the FSM comment invites) and inherits the under-disclosure.

Host trace / impact: No live exploit at HEAD (no caller). If wired in as the FSM comment contemplates: memo 'ADD:BTC.BTC:bc1qpool:evilaff:1000' confirms pool only and returns true, signing a 10% affiliate skim unseen; memo 'wd:BTC.BTC:1HELLO' confirms 'withdraw 0.01%' while the signed memo carries '1HELLO'.

Proposed remediation: Delete mayachain_parseConfirmMemo(), its header declaration and the unittests/firmware/mayachain.cpp cases (the live path already discloses the full memo). If it must stay, port thorchain_parseConfirmMemo() wholesale: tri-state result, 10-slot split, ADD affiliate/fee screen, all-digits check before atoi in WITHDRAW.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. At HEAD 71ba84436, mayachain_parseConfirmMemo() (lib/firmware/mayachain.c:254) has no caller outside unittests; the only references are the header declaration, the unit test, and two comments in lib/firmware/fsm_msg_mayachain.h. Both MayaChain signing paths (fsm_msg_mayachain.h:264 deposit, :293 send) page the full raw memo via thorchain_confirm_full_memo(), so affiliate/fee bps and any non-digit withdraw field are shown to the user byte-for-byte before signing. The described under-disclosure (ADD hides fields[3]/[4]; WITHDRAW atoi without digit guard) is real inside the dead function but is not reachable from any host input; the claim itself states "No live exploit at HEAD (no caller)". This is a dead-code hygiene finding, not a reachable defect.

### C3-022 — CONFIRMED — P3: IBC source_channel/source_port/revision_height/revision_number still hashed unescaped via %s (sibling of audit P3 #147, fixed only in signtx_tendermint.c)

Unit: `audit:osmosis`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/osmosis.c:678`.

Audit finding #147 (cosmos-tendermint IBC fields streamed into the amino-JSON sighash without escaping) was closed in commit f61503a6f for lib/firmware/signtx_tendermint.c (lines 525-547 now route sender/source_channel/source_port/revision_height/revision_number through tendermint_sha256UpdateEscaped). The Osmosis twin osmosis_signTxUpdateMsgIBCTransfer was only partially fixed: `sender` is now bound to the derived address (osmosis.c:647), but source_channel (line 678-679), source_port (682-683), revision_height (686-688) and revision_number (691-693) are still interpolated with plain tendermint_snprintf("%s"), which is vsnprintf+sha256_Update with no escaping (tendermint.c:127-138). None of the four is format-validated in fsm_msgOsmosisMsgAck (fsm_msg_osmosis.h:640-652 only checks has_*). Trace: host OsmosisMsgAck.ibc_transfer.source_port = `transfer","x":"y` (max_size 32 -> 31 chars) -> displayed raw via confirm("Confirm Source Port","%s") -> hashed verbatim into the StdSignDoc -> signed. The same file escapes chain_id, memo, MsgSend amount/denom and every validator address with comments stating host-supplied fields must be escaped, so this is the one remaining unescaped host string family in the unit. Impact is capped because the raw string is shown on-screen and the chain rejects a non-canonical sign doc, matching the P3 rating the audit gave the cosmos twin.

Host trace / impact: Malicious host sends OsmosisSignTx then an OsmosisMsgAck with ibc_transfer.source_channel = `channel-0","memo":"a`. An inattentive user sees `channel-0","memo":"a` on a 'Confirm Source Channel' screen and approves; the device emits a valid secp256k1 signature over a structurally-injected, non-canonical amino JSON document containing attacker-chosen key/value pairs the firmware never modelled.

Proposed remediation: Mirror the signtx_tendermint.c fix: emit the four fields with tendermint_sha256UpdateEscaped (prefix/suffix literals via sha256_Update), and additionally whitelist them before display: revision_height/revision_number must pass osmosis_isCanonicalUint64 (they are numeric uint64 strings on-chain), source_channel/source_port must pass an identifier charset check (reuse osmosis_isValidDenom's [A-Za-z0-9/:._-] set). Refuse with FailureType_Failure_SyntaxError on mismatch.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Reproduced at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/osmosis.c osmosis_signTxUpdateMsgIBCTransfer: sender is bound to the derived address (strcmp at ~647), but source_channel (678-679), source_port (682-683), revision_height (686-688) and revision_number (691-693) are hashed via tendermint_snprintf("%s") which is vsnprintf + sha256_Update with no escaping (tendermint.c:127-138). fsm_msg_osmosis.h:640-646 only checks has_* flags, then confirm()s each raw string ("Confirm Source Channel"/"Source Port"/"Revision Height"/"Revision Number" with "%s") and passes them straight into the hash at 712-717. No format validation exists anywhere on the path, so a host-supplied value like `channel-0","memo":"a` is displayed raw and, on approval, signed verbatim as part of the amino StdSignDoc. The cosmos twin in signtx_tendermint.c was fixed with tendermint_sha256UpdateEscaped; osmosis.c was not. Reachable from host input; severity capped (on-screen display, chain rejects non-canonical doc), consistent with the P3 rating.

### C3-023 — CONFIRMED — P3: fsm_msgOsmosisGetAddress: path-string failure sends Failure but does not return; handler continues, prompts with a blank path, and sends a second (OsmosisAddress) response

Unit: `audit:osmosis`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_osmosis.h:58`.

At fsm_msg_osmosis.h:53-63, when both bip32_node_to_string and bip32_path_to_string fail, the handler memzeros node_str and node, calls fsm_sendFailure("Can't create Bip32 Path String") and layoutHome(), but there is no `return;`. Execution falls through to tendermint_pathMismatched (line 65), the WARNING confirm, confirm_ethereum_address(node_str="", resp->address) (line 78), and finally msg_write(OsmosisAddress) at line 90. The failure is reachable from host input: NODE_STRING_LENGTH is 50 (coins.h:58), bip32_path_to_string fails closed when the rendered path exceeds it (coins.c:255-267), and OsmosisGetAddress.address_n allows max_count:10 with arbitrary uint32 values, so a 10-element hardened path renders to ~120 chars and fails both formatters. Outcome: (1) host receives a Failure and then an OsmosisAddress for one request, desynchronising the protocol; (2) on show_display the user is asked to confirm an address whose derivation-path label is empty, so the show-address disclosure loses the path binding for exactly the non-standard paths the WARNING screen is meant to flag. The identical missing return exists in fsm_msg_cosmos.h:31-41, fsm_msg_thorchain.h:40-50 and fsm_msg_mayachain.h:40-50 (outside this unit, noted for the sibling sweep).

Host trace / impact: Host sends OsmosisGetAddress{address_n=[0x8000002C,0x80000076,0xFFFFFFFF,0xFFFFFFFF,0xFFFFFFFF,0xFFFFFFFF,0xFFFFFFFF,0xFFFFFFFF,0xFFFFFFFF,0xFFFFFFFF], show_display=true}. Device emits Failure, then still shows 'WARNING wrong path' and an address confirm screen with no path text, then emits OsmosisAddress for the same request. No key material leaks (node is zeroed first and the address was already derived), but the device answers twice and the on-screen path disclosure is blank.

Proposed remediation: Add `return;` after layoutHome() inside the `Can't create Bip32 Path String` branch (and in the three sibling handlers).

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Verified at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/fsm_msg_osmosis.h lines 51-63: on failure of both bip32_node_to_string and bip32_path_to_string the handler memsets node_str, memzeros node, calls fsm_sendFailure(...) and layoutHome() but has no `return;` — unlike every other failure branch in the function. Execution continues to tendermint_pathMismatched, the WARNING confirm, confirm_ethereum_address(node_str="" , resp->address), and msg_write(MessageType_MessageType_OsmosisAddress). Reachable from host: include/keepkey/transport/messages-osmosis.options sets OsmosisGetAddress.address_n max_count:10; bip32_node_to_string (coins.c:489) returns false for any address_n_count not 3 or 5 (and for whole_account=false, anything != 5); bip32_path_to_string (coins.c:250-267) fails closed via snprintf length checks against NODE_STRING_LENGTH=50 (coins.h:58) — a 10-element hardened path with 0xFFFFFFFF entries renders to ~121 chars. Address was already derived before the check, so the device sends Failure then OsmosisAddress for one request and shows the address confirm with a blank path label. No key leak, protocol desync / weakened path disclosure only (P3 is appropriate).

### C3-024 — REFUTED — P3: Dead surface: from_address derived and discarded in Delegate/Undelegate/Redelegate/Rewards; undefined debug_intermediate_hash() prototype; stale testnet TODO and unused <time.h>

Unit: `audit:osmosis`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/osmosis.c:234`.

osmosis_signTxUpdateMsgDelegate (lines 227-236), ...Undelegate (297-305), ...Redelegate (368-376) and ...Rewards (573-577) each call tendermint_getAddress(&node, pfix, from_address) and then never read from_address: the signed delegator_address is the host string. The audit's fix for the sender finding bound sender to this derived address in LP-Add/Remove, Swap and IBC (lines 439-441, 502-504, 647-649, 721-723) but left the four staking helpers computing a value that is neither signed, displayed nor compared. delegator_address IS displayed via confirm_osmosis_address in every branch and is bech32-validated, so this is not a display-vs-signed gap, but the derivation is dead code that spends a pubkey hash + bech32 encode per message and misleads readers into assuming the binding exists. Related dead surface in the same unit: include/keepkey/firmware/osmosis.h:16 declares `void debug_intermediate_hash(void);` with no definition anywhere in the tree; osmosis.c:715 still says `// TODO: add testnet support` although line 721 already selects tosmo; osmosis.c:32 includes <time.h> which nothing in the file uses.

Host trace / impact: Not directly exploitable. A host can supply a delegator_address for a different account than the signing key; the user sees it on-screen and the chain rejects the signature-vs-signer mismatch, so impact is limited to wasted cycles and reviewer confusion.

Proposed remediation: Either bind delegator_address to the device (strcmp(delegator_address, from_address) != 0 -> return false, as the LP/Swap/IBC helpers now do) or delete the four dead tendermint_getAddress calls and their from_address buffers. Remove the debug_intermediate_hash prototype, the stale TODO comment, and the <time.h> include.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Facts confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436 (lib/firmware/osmosis.c): Delegate (227-236), Undelegate (298-306), Redelegate (369-376), Rewards (574-577) each compute from_address via tendermint_getAddress and never read it; only LP-Add/Remove/Swap/IBC (439-441, 502-504, 647, 721-723) strcmp it against sender. include/keepkey/firmware/osmosis.h:16 declares debug_intermediate_hash with no definition in lib/ or include/; <time.h> (line 32) is unused; the '// TODO: add testnet support' at 715 precedes code that already selects tosmo at 721. However, this is dead code / hygiene, not a defect reachable from host input: delegator_address is bech32-decoded, shown to the user via confirm, and a host-supplied mismatch is rejected on-chain (signature vs signer). No incorrect signing, display gap, memory-safety issue, or build failure exists. Per the verifier criteria (defect reachable from host input, or would fail device compile), this is not real.

### C3-025 — CONFIRMED — P3: confirm_cosmos_address silently clips 52-char validator addresses (draw_string, not draw_string_fits) while the full address is signed

Unit: `audit:cosmos-tendermint`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/app_layout.c:466`.

layout_cosmos_address_notification draws the address with draw_string() at x=LEFT_MARGIN+65, width 140, starting at y=13+7+7=27 with body font (height 10, line height 14). Only two text rows fit (y=27, y=41; a third row at y=55 is rejected by draw_char_impl because 55+10 > 64), and draw_string() discards draw_string_walk's completeness result, so any character that does not fit is dropped with no ellipsis and no refusal. Simulating with the real body-font glyph widths (font.c): a typical 52-char cosmosvaloper address, e.g. cosmosvaloper1sjllsnramtg3ewxqwwrwjxfgc4n4ef9u2lcnj0, renders 49 of 52 characters; a wide-glyph address (m/w = 8px) renders only 38 of 52. Callers in the unit that pass 52-char host-supplied validator addresses through this screen: lib/firmware/fsm_msg_cosmos.h:180 (delegate), :230 (undelegate), :291/:299 (redelegate src/dst), :360 (rewards). Meanwhile lib/firmware/signtx_tendermint.c:249/318/386/393/465 hash the full validator_address bytes into the signed StdSignDoc. This is the same defect class the prior audit fixed in page_body_confirm() (P2 confirm-display, now using draw_string_fits at confirm_sm.c:364/405); the sibling custom address layouts (cosmos, osmosis, ethereum) were not converted. 45-char delegator/account addresses do fit, so the bug only shows on the longer valoper form and passes casual testing.

Host trace / impact: Host sends CosmosMsgAck{delegate} with a validator_address whose last 3-14 characters differ from what the user expects; the screen shows only the leading 38-49 characters (plus a QR the user is unlikely to scan for a validator), the user approves, and the device signs the full undisclosed address. Practical redirection requires an attacker validator whose operator address shares the displayed prefix (~175+ bits for the 49-char case), so this is a see-what-you-sign invariant violation rather than a cheap theft path.

Proposed remediation: In layout_cosmos_address_notification (and the osmosis/ethereum siblings) use draw_string_fits() for the address body and propagate a failure that makes confirm_with_custom_layout refuse (return false) when the address does not fully fit; or route validator addresses through confirm_bytes()/page_body_confirm which already fail closed. Add a unit test that renders a 52-char cosmosvaloper address with the widest body-font glyphs and asserts full placement.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. (1) lib/firmware/app_layout.c:466 layout_cosmos_address_notification draws the address with draw_string(canvas, body_font, address, &sp, 140, 14) at y = 13+7+7 = 27; draw_string (lib/board/draw.c:233) explicitly discards draw_string_walk's completeness bool, and draw_char_impl rejects any glyph with y+10 > 64, so only rows y=27 and y=41 render; row y=55 is dropped silently, no ellipsis. (2) confirm_helper's render-incompleteness gate (lib/board/confirm_sm.c:608-610) is `(layout_notification_func == &layout_standard_notification) && !confirm_body_fits(...)`, so custom layouts like the cosmos one get NO fit check; confirm_with_custom_layout (confirm_sm.c:841) adds nothing either. (3) Reachable from host input: fsm_msg_cosmos.h:180 (delegate), :230, :291/:299, :360 pass msg->*.validator_address straight to confirm_cosmos_address with only a has_ check; proto max_size:53 allows the full 52-char valoper form. (4) signtx_tendermint.c:249 hashes strlen(validator_address) bytes into the signed doc. (5) Simulated with the real body_font glyph widths parsed from lib/board/font.c (a-z mostly 6px, i/l 2, m/w 8, 1 3px): cosmosvaloper1sjllsnramtg3ewxqwwrwjxfgc4n4ef9u2lcnj0 renders 49/52; cosmosvaloper1 + 38x'm' renders 38/52 — exactly the audit's numbers. Minor correction: a wide-glyph 45-char cosmos1 address (36/45) also clips, so the defect is not strictly valoper-only, which only widens the exposure. Severity as stated (see-what-you-sign violation, impractical redirection due to prefix-collision cost) is fair.

### C3-026 — CONFIRMED — P3: Generic Tendermint path hashes host-supplied denom and message_type_prefix into the sign doc unescaped and unvalidated (sibling of the fixed IBC escaping issue)

Unit: `audit:cosmos-tendermint`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/signtx_tendermint.c:96`.

fsm_msgTendermintSignTx (lib/firmware/fsm_msg_tendermint.h:76-84, :96) only checks has_denom/has_message_type_prefix and then passes msg->denom into tendermint_signTxInit, which interpolates it with plain %s via tendermint_snprintf at signtx_tendermint.c:94-97 (fee denom). fsm_msgTendermintMsgAck then passes msg->denom and msg->message_type_prefix (matched by strcmp to the SignTx values, but never charset-checked) into tendermint_signTxUpdateMsgSend, which interpolates them with %s at :158-160 ("type":"%s/MsgSend") and :163-166 ("denom":"%s"). The only guards are strnlen caps (9 / 24). tendermint_isValidDenom() exists in tendermint.c:71 precisely for this purpose but is called only from thorchain.c/mayachain.c, never on the generic Tendermint path. The prior audit's P3 for IBC/sender fields was fixed by routing those through tendermint_sha256UpdateEscaped; denom and message_type_prefix were left on the unescaped %s path. Both values are shown to the user via confirm_bytes (fsm_msg_tendermint.h:204-208) so this is disclosure-complete, but a '"' or '\\' inside either field produces a structurally-injected, non-canonical StdSignDoc that the device happily signs.

Host trace / impact: Host sends TendermintSignTx with denom = 'ua"x' (fits the 9-char cap) and a matching TendermintMsgAck; the device shows the raw bytes on the Denomination screen, and if approved hashes {"amount":"..","denom":"ua"x"} verbatim into the signature. No fund loss (the chain will reject a non-canonical sign doc), but the firmware violates its own stated escaping policy and accepts a JSON-injection surface the sibling fields were hardened against.

Proposed remediation: In fsm_msgTendermintSignTx reject the message unless tendermint_isValidDenom(msg->denom) holds, and apply an equivalent whitelist (lowercase alnum, '-', '.', '/') to message_type_prefix; alternatively route both through tendermint_sha256UpdateEscaped in tendermint_signTxInit and tendermint_signTxUpdateMsgSend exactly as chain_id/memo/validator_* are.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/fsm_msg_tendermint.h:76-84 only checks has_denom/has_message_type_prefix; :97 passes msg->denom to tendermint_signTxInit, which (signtx_tendermint.c:66-68) only enforces strnlen<=9 and then at :94-97 interpolates it with plain %s into "\"denom\":\"%s\"" via tendermint_snprintf. fsm_msgTendermintMsgAck (:117-120) checks only strcmp-equality against SignTx values, then :173-175 passes denom and message_type_prefix to tendermint_signTxUpdateMsgSend, which caps length (<=24 / <=9) at :140-143 and interpolates both with %s at :158-160 ("{\"type\":\"%s/MsgSend\"") and :163-166 ("\"denom\":\"%s\""). In the same functions, chain_id/memo (:91, :107) and the IBC sender/channel/port fields (:528-536) go through tendermint_sha256UpdateEscaped. tendermint_isValidDenom (tendermint.c:71, explicitly documented as rejecting quote/backslash) is only invoked from mayachain.c/thorchain.c (osmosis.c has its own validator); grep shows no call on the generic Tendermint path. So a host-supplied denom like 'ua"x' or a message_type_prefix containing '"' or '\\' is hashed verbatim into the StdSignDoc and signed after the user approves the raw bytes on the confirm_bytes screens (:204-208). Reachable from host input; consequence is a structurally non-canonical sign doc (no fund loss), matching the P3 severity as claimed.

### C3-027 — CONFIRMED — P3: GetAddress: missing return after 'Can't create Bip32 Path String' failure sends a Failure and then continues to confirm and emit the address (double response, blank path on screen)

Unit: `audit:cosmos-tendermint`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_cosmos.h:31`.

In fsm_msgCosmosGetAddress (lib/firmware/fsm_msg_cosmos.h:31-41) and identically in fsm_msgTendermintGetAddress (lib/firmware/fsm_msg_tendermint.h:31-41), when both bip32_node_to_string and bip32_path_to_string fail the handler zeroes node_str, memzeros the node, calls fsm_sendFailure(...) and layoutHome() but does NOT return. Execution falls through to the path-mismatch warning, confirm_ethereum_address(node_str="", resp->address) and finally msg_write(CosmosAddress/TendermintAddress). Reachable from the host: show_display=true with a 5-element path that fails path_mismatched (so bip32_node_to_string returns false at coins.c:503) and whose indices are large/hardened — 'm' + 5 x "/2147483647'" = 61 chars exceeds NODE_STRING_LENGTH (50), so bip32_path_to_string also returns false; any count of 6..10 with such indices also triggers it. Result: the host receives a Failure followed by an unsolicited Address message (protocol desync), and the address-confirm screen is rendered with an empty derivation-path label, violating the fail-closed invariant. The same missing-return exists in the sibling handlers fsm_msg_thorchain.h:49-50, fsm_msg_osmosis.h:62-63 and fsm_msg_mayachain.h:49-50 (out of unit, same pattern).

Host trace / impact: Host sends CosmosGetAddress{address_n=[0x8000002c,0x80000076,0x7fffffff,0x7fffffff,0x7fffffff], show_display=true}. Device emits Failure(FirmwareError), then shows the 'Wrong address path' warning and an address screen with no path text; if the user clicks through, a CosmosAddress message is written after the Failure. Impact is limited to protocol desync and an under-disclosed address screen (the address itself is derived correctly for the requested path); no key material leaks because node was memzero'd before the fall-through.

Proposed remediation: Add `return;` after layoutHome() inside the bip32-string failure branch in both fsm_msgCosmosGetAddress and fsm_msgTendermintGetAddress (and the thorchain/osmosis/mayachain siblings), matching the pattern used by every other failure branch in these handlers.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Verified at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/fsm_msg_cosmos.h:31-41 and fsm_msg_tendermint.h:31-41: when both bip32_node_to_string and bip32_path_to_string fail, the block memzeros node, calls fsm_sendFailure(FirmwareError, "Can't create Bip32 Path String") and layoutHome() but has no return; control falls through to tendermint_pathMismatched, confirm_ethereum_address(node_str="", addr) and msg_write(CosmosAddress/TendermintAddress). Reachable from host: address_n=[44',118',0x7fffffff,0x7fffffff,0x7fffffff], show_display=true. bip32_node_to_string (coins.c:503) returns false via path_mismatched (index 2 not hardened/others nonzero); bip32_path_to_string (coins.c:250-274) writes "m" + 5x"/2147483647'" = 61 bytes > NODE_STRING_LENGTH 50 (coins.h:58) and returns false on the len<=cx check. Result is Failure followed by an unsolicited Address message and an address confirm screen with an empty path label. Node is memzero'd before fall-through so no key leak; impact is protocol desync / under-disclosed confirm screen, consistent with the P3 rating.

### C3-028 — CONFIRMED — P2: Nano account label masks with 0x7ffffff (27 bits) instead of 0x7fffffff: transfer/address screens show the wrong account index

Unit: `audit:ripple-nano`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/nano.c:59`.

nano_bip32_to_string() renders the hardened account index as `address_n[2] & 0x7ffffff`. Every other path formatter in the tree (lib/firmware/coins.c:260,512,516) uses 0x7fffffff. Bits 27-30 of the account index are therefore dropped from the label while the full index is used for derivation. This string is the ONLY thing the user sees on the Nano 'Transfer' confirmation (nano.c:333-344, `Send %s NANO to %s?` with account_str) and on the NanoGetAddress show_display screen (fsm_msg_nano.h:30). nano_path_mismatched() only checks the hardened bit, so such paths are accepted as well-formed and the fallback to bip32_path_to_string (which would have shown the true index) is never taken. Display-vs-signed invariant (1): the link (recipient pubkey) hashed into block_hash is derived from the real 31-bit index, the screen shows a different one.

Host trace / impact: Compromised host sends NanoSignTx for a send block with link_recipient_n = [44', 165', 0x80000000|0x08000000] (account 134217728'). is_transfer=true; the device displays 'Send 100 NANO to Nano Account #0?'. The user, believing this is an internal move to their first account, approves. The block's link is the pubkey of account 134217728', an account no wallet software will ever scan or show. Funds are effectively hidden from the user (still under the seed, but only recoverable by someone who knows the odd path -- i.e. the attacker, who can then present a ransom or wait for the device to be used with their own software). Same mislabel on NanoGetAddress with show_display for such a path.

Proposed remediation: Change the mask to `address_n[2] & 0x7fffffff` (or reuse coins.c's formatter). Add a unit test in unittests/firmware/nano.cpp with an account index having bits 27-30 set. Consider also having nano_path_mismatched() (or the transfer branch) reject account indices above a sane bound so is_transfer only triggers for wallet-discoverable accounts.

Verification: 2/2 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/nano.c:58-59 `snprintf(node_str, len, "%s Account #%" PRIu32, _coin->coin_name, address_n[2] & 0x7ffffff);` masks with 27 bits (0x7ffffff), whereas coins.c:260/512/516 use 0x7fffffff. nano_path_mismatched() (nano.c:38-48) only checks count==3, [0]==44', [1]==bip44_account_path, and hardened bit on [2] — no upper bound on the account index — so link_recipient_n=[44',165',0x88000000] passes, is_transfer=true (nano.c:305-308), and the Transfer confirm (nano.c:330-344) shows "Send X NANO to Nano Account #0?" while the link is derived from the real 31-bit index (nano_currentHash, nano.c:223-225 copies recip->public_key from the HDNode derived from link_recipient_n). The bip32_path_to_string fallback is only used when nano_bip32_to_string returns false, which it does not here. Same mislabel on NanoGetAddress show_display (fsm_msg_nano.h:30) with no mismatch warning since nano_path_mismatched returns false. Host-controlled input, display/sign mismatch as claimed.

Verifier 2: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/nano.c:58-59 formats the label as `address_n[2] & 0x7ffffff` (7 f's = 27 bits), while coins.c:260/512/516 use 0x7fffffff. nano_path_mismatched() (nano.c:38-49) only requires count==3, [0]==44', [1]==bip44_account_path, and bit 31 set on [2] — so link_recipient_n = [44', 165', 0x88000000] is accepted as well-formed (is_transfer gated at nano.c:306 via !nano_path_mismatched), nano_bip32_to_string returns true, and the bip32_path_to_string fallback (nano.c:336) never runs. The Transfer confirm at nano.c:342-344 shows "Send X NANO to Nano Account #0?" while the link pubkey is derived from the full 31-bit index 134217728'. Same path in fsm_msg_nano.h:30 for NanoGetAddress show_display. Both are host-controlled inputs; the display-vs-signed mismatch is a genuine, reachable defect.

### C3-029 — CONFIRMED — P3: NanoSignTx/NanoGetAddress accept any coin_name: secp256k1 coins are used as a PIN-only ECDSA signing oracle with no confirmation

Unit: `audit:ripple-nano`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_nano.h:73`.

Both handlers resolve `coin = fsm_getCoin(true, msg->coin_name)` and never check that the coin is a Nano-type coin (curve ED25519_BLAKE2B_NANO_STRING / non-empty nanoaddr_prefix). For the 42 other coins in coins.def, nanoaddr_prefix is "" (coins.c:130) and curve is secp256k1. With coin_name="Bitcoin" the flow proceeds: nano_signingInit copies node->public_key[1..33] (the secp256k1 x-coordinate) as account_pk; nano_validate_address with prefix "" accepts any bare 60-char base32 string as representative/recipient; a receive-shaped block (balance > parent_balance, representative unchanged) sets needs_confirm=false (nano.c:302-304); hdnode_sign_digest then calls ecdsa_sign_digest with the Bitcoin/Ethereum/etc. private key over blake2b(preamble||pubkey||parent_hash||rep||balance||link) and returns the 64-byte signature to the host with no screen shown. The return value of hdnode_sign_digest is also ignored (nano.c:365), so a failing sign still returns has_signature=true with a zero/garbage signature.

Host trace / impact: Host sends NanoSignTx{coin_name:"Bitcoin", address_n:m/44'/0'/0'/0/0, no parent_block, link_hash:<32 bytes>, representative:<any 60 base32 chars>, balance:<16 bytes>}. After PIN only, the device returns an ECDSA signature by the user's BTC key over a host-influenced 32-byte blake2b digest with no user interaction. The digest is a hash of a structure that embeds the device pubkey, so a direct Bitcoin sighash forgery needs a second preimage and is not practical today; the defect is a cross-protocol key-reuse oracle plus a silently-failing signature path, both outside the Nano threat model that the confirm-less receive path was designed for.

Proposed remediation: In both handlers, after fsm_getCoin, refuse coins that are not Nano: e.g. `if (strcmp(coin->curve_name, ED25519_BLAKE2B_NANO_STRING) != 0 || coin->nanoaddr_prefix[0] == '\0') { fsm_sendFailure(FailureType_Failure_SyntaxError, _("Not a Nano coin")); layoutHome(); return; }`. In nano_signTx, check `hdnode_sign_digest(...) == 0` and on failure nano_signingAbort() + fsm_sendFailure(FirmwareError) + return false (mirror fsm_msgRippleSignTx's has_signature check).

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. Both fsm_msgNanoSignTx/GetAddress call fsm_getCoin(true, coin_name) with no restriction to Nano-type coins (fsm_msg_nano.h:73; fsm.c:243 coinByName accepts "Bitcoin"). For secp256k1 coins, nano_signingInit copies node->public_key[1..33] as account_pk (nano.c:163), and hdnode_sign_digest routes to ecdsa_sign_digest (bip32.c:875-877), producing a real ECDSA signature by the BTC/ETH key over a host-influenced blake2b digest. The sign return value is ignored while has_signature=true/size=64 are set unconditionally (nano.c:363-366) — silently-failing-signature defect confirmed. A confirm-less receive path exists (needs_confirm=false when rep unchanged and !is_send), reachable from host input. nano_validate_address with empty prefix accepts any 60-char base32 string with a valid (attacker-computable) checksum. The defect is real and host-reachable. Caveat: the writeup's exact repro ("no parent_block") is slightly wrong — with no parent_block, nano_sanityCheck never zeroes representative_address, so a "Set representative" confirm IS shown; the confirm-less oracle is actually reached via a receive block WITH a matching-representative parent_block. The vulnerability class as characterized (cross-protocol secp256k1 signing oracle + ignored sign return, outside Nano threat model) exists and is reachable, so real=true despite the imprecise exploit message.

### C3-030 — CONFIRMED — P2: EosActionUnknown: contract:action shown on the final confirm comes from the LAST chunk's `common`, but the hashed/signed contract:action is the FIRST chunk's

Unit: `audit:eos`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/eos.c:483`.

eos_compileActionUnknown() hashes `common` (account, name, authorization) into hasher_preimage only on the first chunk (unknown_remaining == 0 branch, eos.c:452-459 via eos_compileActionCommon). Every later chunk of the same action arrives in a fresh EosTxActionAck carrying its own `common`, which is only run through eos_isSupportedAction() (eos.c:429) and then ignored for hashing -- except at eos.c:483-491, where the final chunk's `common->name`/`common->account` are formatted into the confirm title (eos.c:500-502). The fingerprint on that screen covers only the data bytes (hasher_unknown), not the contract/action names. So for any data_size > 256 (the nanopb data_chunk cap, forcing >=2 chunks) the one identity the blind-sign screen shows -- which contract and which action -- is unbound from what is signed.

Host trace / impact: AdvancedMode enabled (required for unknown actions). Host sends EosSignTx, then chunk 1: common={account:'evilcontract', name:'drain', authorization:[victim@active]}, unknown={data_size:300, data_chunk:256 bytes}. Chunk 2: common={account:'eosio', name:'claimrewards'} (not in eos_isSupportedAction's list, so accepted), unknown={data_size:300, data_chunk:44 bytes}. Device screen: title 'eosio:claimrewards', body '300 bytes with fingerprint <sha256(data)>'. User approves a benign-looking reward claim; the signature commits to evilcontract::drain with the same data payload.

Proposed remediation: Snapshot `common->account`/`common->name` (or the whole EosActionCommon) into static state on the first chunk and (a) reject any later chunk whose common differs, and (b) build the confirm title from the snapshot, not from the current message. Alternatively fold account/name into hasher_unknown so the displayed fingerprint also binds them.

Verification: 2/2 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Reproduced at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/eos.c eos_compileActionUnknown(): `common` is fed to eos_compileActionCommon() (hashed into hasher_preimage) only inside the `if (unknown_remaining == 0)` first-chunk branch (eos.c:452-459). On subsequent chunks the freshly supplied `common` is only checked by eos_isSupportedAction() (eos.c:429) and never compared against the first chunk's account/name/authorization. When the last chunk drains `unknown_remaining` to 0 (eos.c:482-491), the title shown via confirm() is built from THAT chunk's `common->account`/`common->name`, while the fingerprint covers only hasher_unknown (data bytes). fsm_msgEosTxActionAck (fsm_msg_eos.h:121-128, 175-177) accepts any EosTxActionAck with has_unknown while eos_hasActionUnknownDataRemaining(), passing `&msg->common` from each message with no cross-chunk validation. So with AdvancedMode enabled a host can hash chunk 1 as evilcontract:drain and display chunk 2 as eosio:claimrewards (unsupported name, so passes the supported-action guard); user confirms a benign-looking contract:action while the signature commits to a different one. Reachable purely from host input.

Verifier 2: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. messages-eos.options caps EosActionUnknown.data_chunk at 256, so data_size>256 forces multiple EosTxActionAck messages. fsm_msg_eos.h:121-128/175-176 forwards every follow-up chunk's msg->common to eos_compileActionUnknown with no consistency check against the first chunk. eos.c:452-459 hashes common (account/name/authorization) into hasher_preimage only on the first chunk (unknown_remaining==0); later chunks' common is only checked by eos_isSupportedAction (eos.c:429) and never hashed. eos.c:482-502 builds the confirm title from the LAST chunk's common->account/name, and the displayed fingerprint (hasher_unknown) covers only data bytes. With AdvancedMode enabled, a host can send chunk1 common=evilcontract:drain and chunk2 common=eosio:claimrewards (unsupported -> accepted); the user sees 'eosio:claimrewards' while the signature commits to evilcontract::drain.

### C3-031 — CONFIRMED — P2: EosAuthorizationKey.type is signed but never displayed or constrained; standard-auth path lets a host bind the device-derived key as an R1 key, permanently locking the account

Unit: `audit:eos`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/eos-contracts/eosio.system.c:453`.

eos_hashAuthorization() varuint-encodes `auth_key->type` into the signed authorization (lines 453-454). No path ever shows or validates it: isStandardAuthorization() (501-522) checks threshold/keys_count/key.size/address_n_count/weight/waits/accounts but not `type`; confirmStandardAuthorization() (524-554) shows only the BIP32 path; confirmArbitraryAuthorization() (586-594) always encodes raw key bytes with EosPublicKeyKind_EOS (K1 prefix) regardless of `type`. On-chain, type 0 = K1 and type 1 = R1 (secp256r1). A K1 compressed pubkey re-labelled as R1 denotes a P-256 point nobody holds the discrete log for, so a permission bound to it can never be satisfied. This is a sibling of the already-fixed key.size/address_n XOR and accounts_count findings: another authorization field that reaches the hash without reaching the screen.

Host trace / impact: Host sends updateauth for account=victim, permission=owner, auth={threshold:1, keys:[{type:1, address_n:m/44'/194'/0'/0/0, weight:1}]} followed by the same for permission=active (both actions in one tx, or two txs). Device shows the standard screen 'Do you want to assign signing auth for victim@owner to m/44'/194'/0'/0/0?' -- exactly what a legitimate key rotation looks like. User approves. The chain now requires an R1 key with the device's K1 x-coordinate; no private key exists for it, the account (and all funds/RAM/stake in it) is irrecoverably locked. Same applies to newaccount owner/active: accounts created by the user are unusable. No on-screen signal distinguishes this from an honest request.

Proposed remediation: In isStandardAuthorization() require `auth->keys[0].type == 0` (the device only derives/signs K1). In confirmArbitraryAuthorization() require `auth_key->type == 0` for device-derived keys, and for raw keys either refuse type != 0 or encode with the matching prefix (eos_publicKeyToWif already returns false for R1) and show the type on the key screen so it is disclosed before it is hashed.

Verification: 2/2 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Reproduced at HEAD 71ba84436 in lib/firmware/eos-contracts/eosio.system.c. EosAuthorizationKey.type is `optional uint32` in deps/device-protocol/messages-eos.proto (no has_type / range check anywhere). eos_hashAuthorization() lines 453-454 varuint-encodes auth_key->type into the signed preimage; isStandardAuthorization() lines 501-522 checks threshold/keys_count/key.size/address_n_count/weight/waits/accounts but never `type`; confirmStandardAuthorization() lines 524-554 displays only the BIP32 path string; confirmArbitraryAuthorization() lines 586-594 formats raw key bytes with EosPublicKeyKind_EOS regardless of `type`. Reachable from host input via updateauth (line 670-676) and newaccount (lines 880-883): a host sends keys=[{type:1, address_n:<device path>, weight:1}], threshold 1, no accounts/waits -> standard path -> user sees the normal "assign signing auth for X to m/44'/194'/0'/0/0" screen -> device signs an authorization tagged as an R1 (secp256r1) key carrying the K1 pubkey bytes, which no one can satisfy. No on-screen signal distinguishes it from an honest request. Same class as the already-patched key.size/address_n XOR and accounts_count gaps: a hashed authorization field that never reaches the screen.

Verifier 2: REAL. Verified at HEAD 71ba84436 in lib/firmware/eos-contracts/eosio.system.c: eos_hashAuthorization() varuint-encodes host-supplied auth_key->type into the signed authorization (lines 453-454); the only other `type` references in the file are the unrelated linkauth/unlinkauth name fields (773-832). isStandardAuthorization() (501-522) checks threshold/keys_count/key.size/address_n_count/weight/waits/accounts but never type; confirmStandardAuthorization() (524-554) displays only the derived BIP32 path; confirmArbitraryAuthorization() (586-594) formats key bytes with EosPublicKeyKind_EOS regardless of type. The confirm-then-hash dispatch (670-676) is reached from updateauth (716) and newaccount owner/active (880/883). Thus a host sending keys=[{type:1, address_n:m/44'/194'/0'/0/0, weight:1}], threshold=1 gets the ordinary standard-auth screen and a signature over an R1-typed authorization bound to a secp256k1-derived point, locking the permission. Reachable from host input; no validation or display of type anywhere.

### C3-032 — CONFIRMED — P3: chain_id is signed but never displayed or pinned to EOS mainnet; host can obtain a signature valid on any EOSIO fork that shares the user's keys

Unit: `audit:eos`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_eos.h:80`.

fsm_msgEosSignTx only checks `msg->chain_id.size == 32` (fsm_msg_eos.h:80) and eos_signingInit() hashes the 32 host-supplied bytes as the first preimage field (eos.c:306). No screen ever shows the chain, and there is no comparison against the EOS mainnet chain id (aca376f2...). Several EOSIO chains were bootstrapped from the EOS genesis snapshot and therefore carry the same account names bound to the same K1 keys, so a signature the user believes is for EOS is directly valid there. The device presents every screen as an EOS transaction with no chain qualifier.

Host trace / impact: User intends a small transfer on EOS. Host sets chain_id to a snapshot fork where the user's identically-named account holds a larger native balance, and builds a delegatebw with transfer=true or a transfer of the fork's core token amount the user is expecting to see for EOS. All action screens read as EOS mainnet; user approves; the signed tx is broadcast on the fork and moves assets the user never intended to touch. Lower severity because the asset symbol/amounts shown must still match the fork's tokens, which gives an attentive user some signal.

Proposed remediation: Either compare chain_id against the EOS mainnet id and refuse otherwise (matching the single 'EOS' coin the firmware advertises), or show the chain id (or a short fingerprint of it) on the 'Sign Transaction' screen alongside expiry/delay so it is disclosed before it is signed.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Verified at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/fsm_msg_eos.h:80 only checks `msg->chain_id.size == 32`; line 99 passes `msg->chain_id.bytes` to eos_signingInit, which at lib/firmware/eos.c:306 does `hasher_Update(&hasher_preimage, chain_id, 32)` with no comparison. grep for the EOS mainnet id `aca376f2` across lib/ and include/ returns nothing, and no confirm()/layout call anywhere in the EOS path displays the chain id. Reachable directly from host EosSignTx input; user sees generic EOS action screens with no chain qualifier. Low severity (P3) as claimed: amounts/symbols shown must still match what the user expects, and the same non-pinning design exists in upstream Trezor EOS code.

### C3-033 — REFUTED — P3: Fail-open guard: if coinByName("EOS") returns NULL, the auth confirm screens print uninitialized stack buffers instead of refusing

Unit: `audit:eos`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/eos-contracts/eosio.system.c:529`.

confirmStandardAuthorization() builds its condition as `(coin = coinByName("EOS")) && !bip32_node_to_string(...) && !bip32_path_to_string(...)` (lines 529-535) and only errors when the whole conjunction is true. If coinByName() returns NULL the conjunction short-circuits to false, the error branch is skipped, and `node_str` (never written) is passed to confirm() at 544-546. confirmArbitraryAuthorization() repeats the pattern at 596-598 with `pubkey`. Today coins.def:18 does contain EOS so the branch is dead, but it is the opposite of fail-closed (invariant 2): a table change, bitcoin-only/feature-gated coin table, or a future rename turns a formatting failure into a garbage/blank signing-auth screen rather than an abort. -Wuninitialized cannot see it because the write is conditional.

Host trace / impact: Not host-triggerable at HEAD (EOS is present in the coin table). If EOS is ever dropped from coins.def while the EOS engine stays compiled, an updateauth/newaccount flow would show uninitialized SRAM contents (possibly including stack residue) as the 'key' the user is assigning, and sign whatever the host supplied.

Proposed remediation: Invert the guard so a missing coin is itself the failure: `coin = coinByName("EOS"); if (!coin || (!bip32_node_to_string(...) && !bip32_path_to_string(...))) { fail }`, and likewise in confirmArbitraryAuthorization. Zero-initialize node_str/pubkey as belt-and-braces.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Code pattern verified at HEAD 71ba84436 (eosio.system.c:529-546: `(coin = coinByName("EOS")) && !bip32_node_to_string(...) && !bip32_path_to_string(...)` short-circuits to false on NULL coin, skipping the error branch and passing uninitialized node_str to confirm()). However, lib/firmware/coins.def:18 defines EOS, so coinByName("EOS") always returns non-NULL in the compiled firmware; the uninitialized path is dead and not reachable from any host input. The audit itself states "Not host-triggerable at HEAD". This is a latent code-hygiene/fail-closed style issue, not a real defect per the reachability criterion.

### C3-034 — REFUTED — P3: Dead surface: eos_compileString() has no callers; eosio.token.c memo-length guard is unreachable

Unit: `audit:eos`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/eos.c:360`.

eos_compileString() (eos.c:360-366, exported in include/keepkey/firmware/eos.h:?) is referenced nowhere in lib/, unittests/ or fuzzer/. It hashes a NUL-terminated string into the signed preimage with no length cap and no disclosure, so any future caller would immediately violate the display-vs-signed invariant. Separately, eosio.token.c:53-60 rejects `256 < memo_len`, but EosActionTransfer.memo is a nanopb `string` with max_size:256, so nanopb guarantees strlen <= 255 and the branch can never execute; the meaningful bound is the .options cap, not this check.

Host trace / impact: None at HEAD (unreachable code). Risk is latent: eos_compileString invites an undisclosed-string hashing path, and the dead memo check gives a false impression of an enforced limit.

Proposed remediation: Delete eos_compileString() and its prototype. Replace the runtime memo check with a `_Static_assert(sizeof(action->memo) == 256, ...)`-style compile-time tie to the nanopb cap, or drop it.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Verified at HEAD 71ba84436: eos_compileString (lib/firmware/eos.c:360) has no callers in lib/, include/, unittests/ or fuzzer/ (only its own definition matches), and eosio.token.c:55 `256 < memo_len` is unreachable given the nanopb string cap. But that is exactly the point: neither is reachable from host input, and the audit itself states "Exploit: None at HEAD". An uncalled function and a redundant bounds check are code-hygiene notes, not a defect that exists and is reachable at HEAD. Not real under the verifier standard.

### C3-035 — CONFIRMED — P3: TronSignTx carries a dead 'reconstruct-then-sign' surface (10 proto fields + TronSignedTx.serialized_tx) that the firmware never reads, with two drifting copies of the nanopb caps

Unit: `audit:tron`. Location at audited SHA: `include/keepkey/transport/messages-tron.options:14`.

deps/device-protocol/messages-tron.proto:63-73 still advertises a second signing mode ('Structured reconstruct-then-sign: provide transfer or trigger_smart plus ref_block_bytes, ref_block_hash, expiration, timestamp') and the TronTransferContract / TronTriggerSmartContract sub-messages, and include/keepkey/transport/messages-tron.options:6-18,21 sizes them (ref_block_bytes 4, ref_block_hash 32, contract_type 64, to_address 35, data 256, TriggerSmartContract.data 512, serialized_tx 1024). At HEAD lib/firmware/fsm_msg_tron.h:98-103 consumes only has_raw_data/raw_data, and lib/firmware/tron.c:465-511 signs sha256(raw_data) only; no reader of transfer, trigger_smart, ref_block_*, expiration, contract_type, to_address, amount, fee_limit, timestamp, data or serialized_tx exists anywhere in lib/ (grep confirms). nanopb still decodes and bounds-checks all of them into the message union on every TronSignTx, so this is unreachable host-facing surface kept only by the proto/options. Separately, the two checked-in copies of the caps disagree: deps/device-protocol/messages-tron.options says raw_data 1024 / ref_block_bytes 2 / ref_block_hash 8 / contract_type 50 while the include/ copy actually fed to nanopb (lib/transport/CMakeLists.txt:36,162) says 2048 / 4 / 32 / 64. Not a memory-safety issue (the include/ copy is the one compiled and every field is capped), and behaviour is fail-closed (a host sending only structured fields gets 'Missing transaction data'), but it is exactly the dead-surface class the prior audit removed elsewhere (binance, mayachain helper), and the proto comment actively misdocuments a mode the device does not implement.

Host trace / impact: No direct exploit: a host that populates transfer/trigger_smart/to_address/amount without raw_data is refused at fsm_msg_tron.h:98, and populated side fields alongside raw_data are ignored (the old to_address/amount display side-channel is already gone). Risk is maintenance: a future change that re-reads any of these unhashed fields for display would reintroduce the display-vs-signed bug the prior audit closed, and a host SDK generated from the deps/ options copy will disagree with the device about the raw_data cap (1024 vs 2048).

Proposed remediation: Delete fields 4-14 from TronSignTx, the TronTransferContract/TronTriggerSmartContract messages, and TronSignedTx.serialized_tx from deps/device-protocol/messages-tron.proto (or mark them reserved), drop their lines from messages-tron.options, and reconcile the deps/device-protocol and include/keepkey/transport copies of messages-tron.options so a single cap set exists. Rewrite the TronSignTx doc comment to state the only mode is raw_data clear-sign-from-bytes with AdvancedMode-gated blind sign for unparsed payloads.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Reproduced at HEAD 71ba84436. (1) deps/device-protocol/messages-tron.proto:53-73 documents "Two modes ... 2. Structured reconstruct-then-sign" and defines TronTransferContract/TronTriggerSmartContract plus TronSignTx fields 4-14 and TronSignedTx.serialized_tx. (2) include/keepkey/transport/messages-tron.options (the copy fed to nanopb per lib/transport/CMakeLists.txt:36,162) sizes all of them: raw_data 2048, ref_block_bytes 4, ref_block_hash 32, contract_type 64, to_address 35, data 256, TriggerSmartContract.data 512, serialized_tx 1024. (3) deps/device-protocol/messages-tron.options disagrees: raw_data 1024, ref_block_bytes 2, ref_block_hash 8, contract_type 50 -- confirmed drift between the two checked-in copies. (4) lib/firmware/fsm_msg_tron.h:98 rejects any request without has_raw_data/raw_data.size>0 ("Missing transaction data"); the only other hits for fee_limit/to_address in lib/ are on the `parsed` struct derived from raw_data (tron.c:204,418-422, fsm_msg_tron.h:214) or in a comment. No code reads msg->transfer, msg->trigger_smart, msg->ref_block_*, msg->expiration, msg->contract_type, msg->to_address, msg->amount, msg->fee_limit, msg->timestamp, msg->data, or sets serialized_tx. So the surface is dead but still host-reachable (nanopb decodes/bounds-checks every field on each TronSignTx), and the proto comment advertises a mode the device does not implement. Severity as stated (P3, maintenance/dead-surface; not memory-unsafe, fail-closed) is accurate -- no exploit, but the finding is factually correct at HEAD.

### C3-036 — CONFIRMED — P3: HiveGetPublicKey show_display clips the STM key: ethereum-address layout can only draw two 140px rows and is exempt from the completeness pager

Unit: `audit:ton-hive`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_hive.h:71`.

fsm_msgHiveGetPublicKey renders the 53-char STM-prefixed key through confirm_ethereum_address() -> layout_ethereum_address_notification (lib/firmware/app_layout.c:548-553). That layout draws the body with draw_string(..., width=140, line_height=14) starting at y = TOP_MARGIN_FOR_TWO_LINES + 2*TOP_MARGIN = 27, so rows land at y=27, 41, 55. draw_char_impl (lib/board/draw.c:70-72) rejects any glyph with y+height > 64; body font height is 10 (font.c:2492), so the third row (55+10=65) is never placed and draw_string_walk stops silently. confirm_helper's RENDER-overflow paging (confirm_sm.c:608-611) is gated on layout_notification_func == &layout_standard_notification, so this custom layout opts out of the fix that was applied to standard bodies. Simulating 20k random secp256k1 keys against the real body-font width table: 100% of STM keys need >= 3 rows at 140px (average glyph ~6px, 53 chars ≈ 320px), so the last ~15-20 characters including the 4-byte RIPEMD checksum are never on screen. Same layout, same defect for fsm_msgTonGetAddress (fsm_msg_ton.h:79): 48-char base64url TON addresses need >= 3 rows in ~57% of cases. (ETH's 42 hex chars fit two rows, which is why the layout was never noticed.) The QR on the same screen is complete, but the text the user is told to compare is truncated with no indicator.

Host trace / impact: User requests HiveGetPublicKey/TonGetAddress with show_display=true to verify a key/address the host shows. The device draws only the first ~2/3 of the string; the user compares a prefix and approves. A hostile host cannot practically forge a prefix collision (>=150 bits), so this is a display-integrity/invariant break rather than a fund-loss path: the device silently shows less than it claims to be confirming, the exact failure mode the 'never truncate, abort or page' invariant forbids and that the standard-layout pager was added to close.

Proposed remediation: Either (a) run the completeness probe for this layout too: add a fits-probe that replays layout_ethereum_address_notification's origin/width (like confirm_body_fits_constant_power does for constant-power) and page or refuse when it fails; or (b) in the Hive/TON handlers, show long strings via confirm()/confirm_bytes() (standard layout, paged) instead of confirm_ethereum_address, or split into a 2-screen flow (text pages, then QR). Add a unit test asserting draw_string_fits() is true for a 53-char STM key and a 48-char TON address at the ethereum-address layout origin.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. Path: fsm_msg_hive.h:71 (fsm.c:380 includes it) calls confirm_ethereum_address(role_label, resp->public_key) -> app_confirm.c:304 confirm_with_custom_layout(&layout_ethereum_address_notification, ...) -> confirm_sm.c:841 -> confirm_helper with that custom layout. In confirm_helper the RENDER-overflow pager is gated `render_incomplete = (layout_notification_func == &layout_standard_notification) && !confirm_body_fits(...)` (confirm_sm.c:608-610), so the ethereum layout falls through to a single confirm_screen. layout_ethereum_address_notification (app_layout.c:548-553) draws the body at y = TOP_MARGIN_FOR_TWO_LINES(13)+2*TOP_MARGIN(7) = 27 with width 140 and line_height = font_height(10)+BODY_FONT_LINE_PADDING(4) = 14, giving rows y=27,41,55. draw_char_impl (draw.c:72-73) requires img->height + p->y <= canvas->height(64); body font height is 10 (font.c:2492), so row 3 (55+10=65) is rejected and draw_string_walk stops silently (draw.c:214-219, return value discarded by draw_string). Measured against the real body-font width table: a 53-char STM key (e.g. STM5d1hc...) is 323px -> 3 rows at 140px, so the tail (including checksum) is never drawn; a sample 48-char TON address is 292px -> 3 rows as well. No truncation indicator, QR is drawn complete. Reachable from host input via HiveGetPublicKey/TonGetAddress with show_display=true. Display-integrity defect (not fund loss), as stated.

### C3-037 — REFUTED — P3: hive_prepare_account_sign leaves the master root node resident when the owner-path derivation fails (sibling of 2216275ce)

Unit: `audit:ton-hive`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_hive.h:303`.

Commit 2216275ce scrubbed fsm_derived_node when hive_deriveRawKey fails (lines 288-298), but the very next failure exit was left unfixed: fsm_getDerivedNode(SECP256K1_NAME, address_n, ...) at line 301 loads the wallet's master root into the static fsm_derived_node via storage_getRootNode and then runs hdnode_private_ckd_cached; if the CKD fails, fsm_getDerivedNode returns NULL without clearing the buffer (fsm.c:283-288), and the handler at line 303-306 only zeros `keys` and returns. The (partially derived or root) private key stays in the static CONFIDENTIAL buffer until some later handler happens to overwrite it. The same pattern exists for `!root` at line 278 when storage_getRootNode fails after a partial write. Reachability is low (CKD failure requires an invalid child key, ~2^-128, or a passphrase-cancel race), which is why this is P3, but it is exactly the exit the earlier fix documented as unacceptable.

Host trace / impact: A CKD failure (or storage_getRootNode partial failure) on the account_create/account_update path returns a Failure to the host while the master/intermediate private key remains in fsm_derived_node RAM; a subsequent memory-disclosure bug or debug read would expose it. Not remotely triggerable in practice.

Proposed remediation: On both failure exits in hive_prepare_account_sign (root == NULL and node == NULL), memzero(&fsm_derived_node, sizeof(fsm_derived_node)) (the handler is compiled inside fsm.c, so the static is in scope), or better, make fsm_getDerivedNode itself scrub fsm_derived_node on every failure return so every coin handler's `if (!node) return;` is safe.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Code at HEAD 71ba84436 matches the description textually (lib/firmware/fsm_msg_hive.h:301-306 zeros only `keys` on `!node`; lib/firmware/fsm.c fsm_getDerivedNode returns 0 without scrubbing fsm_derived_node on CKD failure), but the failure exit is not reachable from host input. Traced: (1) storage_getRootNode (lib/firmware/storage.c:2491-2577) returns false on passphrase-cancel BEFORE any write to `node` (passphrase_protect precedes hdnode_from_xprv / hdnode_from_seed), and hdnode_from_xprv validates the key and returns 0 before writing `out` -- so there is no "partial write" on the `!root` path; the buffer is either untouched or still holds whatever a previous handler left, which is the pre-existing firmware-wide pattern, not a new leak. (2) hdnode_private_ckd (deps/crypto/trezor-firmware/crypto/bip32.c:234-290) does NOT fail on an out-of-range/zero child key -- it loops re-hashing (`while(true)` with data[0]=1 retry) until a valid key is found, per BIP32. It returns 0 only for an invalid node or depth==UINT32_MAX, neither reachable from a freshly loaded root with a host path already validated by hive_slip48_path_ok (fixed 5-element path). hdnode_private_ckd_cached only propagates those same failures. So the `!node` branch at line 303 cannot be triggered by any host message; the "~2^-128 invalid child key" premise is wrong because the library retries instead of failing. No reachable defect at HEAD; at most a defensive-consistency nit on a dead branch.

### C3-038 — CONFIRMED — P3: Dead surface: ton_formatAmount() and the TonSignTx clear-sign proto fields (to_address/amount/memo/seqno/expire_at) have no consumer

Unit: `audit:ton-hive`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/ton.c:227`.

ton_formatAmount is declared in ton.h and defined in ton.c but referenced nowhere else in lib/, include/ or unittests/ (grep). It exists only to render the TonSignTx.amount field that fsm_msgTonSignTx now deliberately refuses to display (fsm_msg_ton.h:141-144). Likewise messages-ton.options:10-11 still size TonSignTx.to_address (50) and memo (121) and the proto (deps/device-protocol/messages-ton.proto:40-62) still advertises 'Clear-signing (preferred): provide to_address + amount + seqno + expire_at' with '[verified amount and recipient]' semantics that the firmware never implements. The prior audit's remediation for the TON blind-sign finding said to remove/repurpose these fields; the fix added the digest screen but left the dead surface, so hosts are still invited by the proto comment to build UIs on fields the device ignores.

Host trace / impact: A host developer follows the proto's documented 'clear-signing preferred' path, sends to_address/amount, and presents them to the user as device-verified; the device signs raw_tx that may bear no relation to them. The AdvancedMode gate and digest screen limit damage, but the misleading contract is precisely what the invariant on dead surface targets.

Proposed remediation: Delete ton_formatAmount from ton.c/ton.h; remove to_address/amount/memo/seqno/expire_at from TonSignTx (or mark them deprecated/reserved) in messages-ton.proto and .options, and rewrite the proto comment to state that the device only blind-signs raw_tx under AdvancedMode and shows sha256(raw_tx).

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Verified at HEAD 71ba84436. (1) `ton_formatAmount` has exactly two references in the tree: its declaration at include/keepkey/firmware/ton.h:58 and its definition at lib/firmware/ton.c:227 — no caller anywhere in lib/, include/, unittests/ or deps/. (2) fsm_msgTonSignTx (lib/firmware/fsm_msg_ton.h ~110-165) never reads to_address/amount/memo/seqno/expire_at; it explicitly comments they are "unbound to the signed bytes", gates on AdvancedMode, and shows only the byte-count + sha256 digest of raw_tx before ton_signTx. (3) deps/device-protocol/messages-ton.proto:40-62 still documents "1. Clear-signing (preferred): provide to_address + amount + seqno + expire_at. Device reconstructs the v4r2 unsigned body cell ... verifies it matches raw_tx ... Shows 'TON Transfer' confirmation with verified amount and recipient" — behavior the firmware does not implement. Reachable from host input: a host populates the clear-sign fields per the proto contract, the device silently accepts them, ignores them, and returns a signature over whatever raw_tx contains with no Failure indicating the fields were unverified. So the dead helper + stale proto contract exist as claimed. Severity is correctly low (P3): the AdvancedMode gate and digest screen mean the device itself never displays unverified data; the defect is the misleading host-facing contract and dead code, not a device-side signing flaw.

### C3-039 — UNVERIFIED — P2: Orchard recipient UA can be silently clipped on the review screen (custom layout bypasses the fit probe)

Unit: `audit:zcash`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_zcash.h:322`.

zcash_verify_and_confirm_orchard_output() shows the 106-char Unified Address via confirm_with_custom_layout(&layout_zcash_address_text_notification, ...). confirm_helper() (lib/board/confirm_sm.c) only runs the draw_string_fits() completeness probe / pager when the layout is layout_standard_notification, so this custom layout gets no SOURCE/RENDER completeness check. layout_zcash_address_text_notification() (lib/firmware/app_layout.c:646) draws the body font (size 10, line height 14) from y=14 at width 225: rows at y=14/28/42 fit, a 4th row at y=56 is rejected by draw_char_impl (56+10>64) and draw_string_walk() stops silently. Bech32 glyph widths in body_font range 2..8 px ('w','m' = 8). A random UA fits (0/300k in simulation), but a UA whose 104 data chars are wide enough (total > ~654 px) loses its tail with no ellipsis, and the confirm returns true. The same layout is the first screen of confirm_zcash_address() (lib/firmware/app_confirm.c:339) used by ZcashDisplayAddress. This is the exact pattern the file's own comment at :290-313 describes for the amount line, fixed there by splitting screens but not for the address itself.

Host trace / impact: A compromised host controls the destination receiver: it iterates diversifier indices for an attacker-owned Orchard FVK (each index yields a fresh pseudo-random UA) until the UA's rendered width exceeds three 225-px rows, then submits it as the action recipient. The device shows a UA missing its last characters (up to 21 for an all-'w' string) with no indication, the user holds to approve, and the device emits a RedPallas signature over cmx bound to the full (unseen) receiver. No forged-prefix attack is possible, so this does not directly redirect funds to a look-alike address; it is a fail-closed violation: the device attests a recipient the user could not fully read.

Proposed remediation: Route custom-layout bodies through a fit probe at the layout's real origin, like confirm_body_fits_constant_power(): add confirm_body_fits_zcash_address() that replays draw_string_fits() from (LEFT_MARGIN, TOP_MARGIN_FOR_THREE_LINES+ADDRESS_XPUB_TOP_MARGIN) at TRANSACTION_WIDTH-25, select it in fits_probe_for(), and refuse (or page) when it reports incomplete. Alternatively, in zcash_verify_and_confirm_orchard_output() and fsm_msgZcashDisplayAddress() call the probe directly and fail with ActionCancelled/Other before confirm. Add a unit test with a max-width UA (all 'w'/'m' data chars) asserting the confirm is refused.

Verification: 1/2 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Reproduced at HEAD 71ba84436. (1) fsm_msg_zcash.h:322 shows the UA via confirm_with_custom_layout(&layout_zcash_address_text_notification,...); confirm_helper (confirm_sm.c:608-610) computes render_incomplete only when layout_notification_func == &layout_standard_notification, so no draw_string_fits probe/pager runs for this layout, and confirm_screen returns the user's hold as true. (2) layout_zcash_address_text_notification (app_layout.c:646) draws the address with the body font from y=TOP_MARGIN_FOR_THREE_LINES+ADDRESS_XPUB_TOP_MARGIN=14, width TRANSACTION_WIDTH-25=225, line height 10+4=14: rows y=14/28/42 fit; a 4th row at y=56 fails draw_char_impl's (img->height + p->y) <= canvas->height check (draw.c:73, 66>64) and draw_string_walk stops with have_space=false; draw_string() discards the completeness result ((void) at draw.c:236), so the tail is dropped with no ellipsis. (3) Body-font widths for the bech32 charset (font.c) are 2..8 px ('w','m'=8, 'l'=2, 'j'=3, r/f/t/k=5, rest 6); 'u1'+104x'w' = 841 px = 4 rows. Exact DP over the wrapping rule gives P(overflow)≈1.7e-9 for a uniformly random 104-char payload, i.e. ~6e8 candidates to find one — rare, but grindable: the recipient is the raw 43-byte host-supplied msg->recipient (only size checked at :254-256; pk_d is memcpy'd unvalidated, g_d only needs diversify to succeed), so an attacker can iterate diversifiers of their own FVK offline (hours of CPU) or, for the pure fail-closed demonstration, arbitrary bytes at F4Jumble/bech32m speed. The signature is then bound to the full receiver via cmx while the user saw a truncated UA. Same layout is the first screen of confirm_zcash_address(). Defect exists and is host-reachable; practical severity is limited because no prefix-forgery is possible (no fund redirection to a look-alike), matching the P2 framing.

Continuation: re-verify before deciding whether to fix.

### C3-040 — REFUTED — P3: verify_orchard_digest is a fail-open latch (always true today) guarding the only bundle-digest check

Unit: `audit:zcash`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_zcash.h:1285`.

zcash_signing.verify_orchard_digest is set true unconditionally in fsm_msgZcashSignPCZT (:927) after the request-status gate; it is never set false on any live path. Yet it still gates both the per-action field requirement (:1145, has_orchard_action_data starts with 'zcash_signing.verify_orchard_digest &&') and the final orchard/ironwood digest recomputation (:1285). If any future edit or memory corruption leaves the flag false, actions are accepted with no nullifier/cmx/rk fields (has_orchard_action_data false -> 'Missing Orchard action data', so that half fails closed), but more importantly the final memcmp against expected_orchard_digest is skipped and signatures are released over a host-attested orchard_digest. Sibling dead state: zcash_signing.total_amount (:93, :832) is stored and never read.

Host trace / impact: No live exploit at HEAD (flag is always true). The defect is the shape: the security decision is expressed as an optional mode with a fail-open default rather than an unconditional check, which is the same class the prior audit fixed elsewhere (guard-by-flag drift).

Proposed remediation: Delete the verify_orchard_digest field and make both the per-action field requirement and the end-of-flow digest verification unconditional. Delete total_amount from the session struct (it is only used for the summary prompt before the session starts).

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Verified at HEAD 71ba84436 (lib/firmware/fsm_msg_zcash.h): verify_orchard_digest is cleared at :849 and set true at :927 with no return/abort between them (:850-:926 is straight-line setup), and the only other consumers are :1145 and :1285. Every path that reaches the action handler has the flag true, so the digest memcmp is never skipped for any host-supplied input. The audit itself states "No live exploit at HEAD". This is a hardening/code-shape suggestion (make the check unconditional) contingent on a hypothetical future edit or memory corruption, not a reachable defect; total_amount being write-only is dead state, not a security issue.

### C3-041 — CONFIRMED — P3: Legacy host-supplied ZcashPCZTAction.sighash is silently accepted, unlike ZcashTransparentInput.sighash

Unit: `audit:zcash`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_zcash.h:1127`.

fsm_msgZcashTransparentInput rejects a present legacy sighash (:1539 'Host transparent sighash rejected') so a v1 host fails fast. fsm_msgZcashPCZTAction never inspects msg->has_sighash; the field is ignored and the device-computed sighash is used. A v1 host that still populates it and expects legacy semantics gets no error until signatures fail verification off-device. Nanopb caps it at 32 bytes so there is no memory-safety issue.
