Host trace / impact: None direct (the device never uses the value). Contract inconsistency: a mis-built host cannot tell that its supplied sighash was discarded.

Proposed remediation: Mirror the transparent-input handler: if (msg->has_sighash) fsm_sendFailure(SyntaxError, 'Host action sighash rejected'); abort session. Or remove field 3 from the proto and options and reserve the tag.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436 in /Users/highlander/WebstormProjects/keepkey-stack/projects/keepkey-firmware-alphafix/lib/firmware/fsm_msg_zcash.h. fsm_msgZcashPCZTAction (lines 1085-~1330) never reads msg->has_sighash or msg->sighash anywhere (grep for 'sighash' in the file shows no reference inside that handler); it requires zcash_signing.has_device_sighash (line 1136) and signs with `const uint8_t* sighash = zcash_signing.sighash` (line 1205). By contrast fsm_msgZcashTransparentInput line 1539 does `if (msg->has_sighash) fsm_sendFailure(..., "Host transparent sighash rejected")`. The proto (deps/device-protocol/messages-zcash.proto:140) still declares `optional bytes sighash = 3; // legacy mode` and messages-zcash.options caps it at max_size:32, so a host can populate it and the device silently accepts and discards it. Reachable from host input with no error. Severity as claimed (P3, contract inconsistency only): the device always uses its own computed sighash, so there is no signing-with-host-digest risk or memory-safety issue; a mis-built legacy host only learns of the discard when its signatures fail off-device.

### C3-042 — REFUTED — P3: Unused signing entry points compiled into firmware, including the rk-unvalidated signer the code itself calls out as unsafe

Unit: `audit:zcash`. Location at audited SHA: `deps/crypto/trezor-firmware/crypto/redpallas.c:382`.

redpallas_sign_digest_for_rk (:382, feeds a host-supplied rk into nonce/challenge with no check it belongs to the device key -- see the comment at fsm_msg_zcash.h:1252 explaining why production must not call it), redpallas_sign_digest (:469), redpallas_derive_rk (:541) and redpallas_verify_digest (:495) have no callers outside unittests/firmware/zcash.cpp and tools/check_pallas_api_boundary.py. Likewise in lib/firmware/zcash.c: zcash_orchard_diversify_hash, zcash_pczt_signing_request_is_clear, zcash_orchard_compute_cmx, zcash_ironwood_compute_cmx, zcash_derive_orchard_keys (non-progress) are test-only. Invariant (5): unreachable code should be deleted; the _for_rk variant is a live footgun that a future caller could pick up again (which is exactly how the previous bug happened per the in-file comment).

Host trace / impact: No runtime exploit; increases attack/maintenance surface and ROM on a size-constrained image.

Proposed remediation: Delete redpallas_sign_digest_for_rk, redpallas_sign_digest, redpallas_derive_rk from the firmware library (keep redpallas_verify_digest only under a unit-test build guard if tests need it), port the tests to redpallas_sign_digest_with_ak, and update tools/check_pallas_api_boundary.py accordingly. Move the test-only zcash.c wrappers behind the unit-test target or delete them.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Premise verified at HEAD 71ba84436: redpallas_sign_digest_for_rk/redpallas_sign_digest/redpallas_derive_rk/redpallas_verify_digest and the listed zcash.c helpers (zcash_orchard_diversify_hash, zcash_pczt_signing_request_is_clear, zcash_orchard_compute_cmx, zcash_ironwood_compute_cmx, non-progress zcash_derive_orchard_keys) have zero callers in lib/ or include/ — only unittests/firmware/zcash.cpp and tools/check_pallas_api_boundary.py, and that checker explicitly forbids the action handler from calling redpallas_sign_digest_for_rk (tools/check_pallas_api_boundary.py:177). Because nothing in the firmware message path invokes them, the unsafe _for_rk signer is not reachable from any host input; the claim itself states "No runtime exploit". It is a dead-code/ROM hygiene item, not a defect reachable from host input, and it is not a build-portability failure, so it does not meet the real=true bar.

### C3-043 — REFUTED — P3: Account-level ZIP-32 spending key (sk) is retained in the long-lived signing session but never used

Unit: `audit:zcash`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/zcash.c:664`.

zcash_derive_orchard_keys_with_progress() copies the derived account spending key into keys->sk (:664). No production code reads ZcashOrchardKeys.sk (only unit tests compare it). In fsm_msgZcashSignPCZT the whole struct is stored in the static zcash_signing.keys for the entire streaming session (potentially minutes while the host proves), so the root secret from which ask/nk/rivk/dk are all derived sits in RAM alongside the only key actually needed (ask). It is wiped by zcash_signing_abort() on every exit path, so this is hygiene rather than a leak, but it violates the minimise-secrets-at-rest rule that the rest of this file follows (e.g. caching ak to avoid re-touching ask).

Host trace / impact: None direct. Any RAM disclosure primitive during a signing session would yield sk (full account spend authority + viewing) instead of only ask.

Proposed remediation: Remove the sk member from ZcashOrchardKeys and the memcpy at :664; adjust the three unit tests at unittests/firmware/zcash.cpp:556/576/588 to compare ask (or ak) instead.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Facts confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436: lib/firmware/zcash.c:664 copies the account spending key into keys->sk; grep shows no non-test reader of .sk; fsm_msgZcashSignPCZT stores the full struct in static zcash_signing.keys (fsm_msg_zcash.h:820) for the whole session and only uses keys.ask/keys.ak (:1260); zcash_signing_abort() memzeros it on every exit path. However this is a secrets-at-rest hygiene observation, not a defect: sk is never exposed via any message, and no host input can read it. The claim itself states "Exploit: None direct" and requires a hypothetical RAM-disclosure primitive that does not exist in the code. Not reachable from host input, so not real under the verification standard (would be a low-priority hardening suggestion: memzero keys.sk after derivation or drop the field).

### C3-044 — CONFIRMED — P3: Empty recovery word (consecutive spaces) is counted as a word; with enforce_wordlist=false a real recovery commits a phrase shorter than the one the user typed

Unit: `audit:setup-ceremony-recovery`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/recovery_cipher.c:537`.

recovery_character() only validates the just-completed word when strlen(decoded_word) > 0 (line 537). A space arriving while decoded_word is empty (a leading space, or a second space right after a word boundary) skips validation, still runs words_entered++ (line 566) and appends ' ' to mnemonic (line 578). The on-screen counter simply skips a number. At finalize, strtok() collapses the empty token, so the token count is one less than words_entered. With enforce_wordlist=true this fails closed (mnemonic_check rejects an 11-word string). With enforce_wordlist=false (a host-chosen flag never disclosed on the device) and dry_run=false, line 720 commits final_mnemonic_scratch via setup_commit() even though it holds word_count-1 words -- an invalid-length phrase that mnemonic_to_seed still turns into a working wallet. Violates invariant (2): a malformed input is silently accepted rather than refused.

Host trace / impact: Host sends RecoveryDevice{word_count:12, enforce_wordlist:false, dry_run:false}. User types words 1-5 normally. Host injects one extra CharacterAck{character:" "} (or the user double-taps space). Screen shows the next word as "7." instead of "6."; if the user does not notice, their 11th word's terminating space hits words_entered == word_count and the device stores the 11-word phrase, imported=true, and reports "Device recovered". The user's 12-word paper backup does not reproduce this wallet; funds received on it are unrecoverable after device loss.

Proposed remediation: In recovery_character(), treat a space with strlen(decoded_word)==0 as a SyntaxError ("Empty word") and abort the ceremony, exactly like an unknown character; additionally, in recovery_cipher_finalize() count tokens while rebuilding final_mnemonic_scratch and refuse if the count != words_entered, so the length check does not depend on enforce_wordlist.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436 in lib/firmware/recovery_cipher.c. In recovery_character(), the space branch only validates/records a word when strlen(decoded_word) > 0 (line 537); a space arriving while decoded_word is empty skips validation entirely, but still reaches words_entered++ (line 569) and appends ' ' to mnemonic (line 578). words_entered starts at 1 (line 363), so with word_count=12: words 1-5 + spaces -> words_entered=6; one extra CharacterAck{" "} -> 7 (mnemonic now has a double space); words 6-10 + spaces -> 12; word 11's terminating space hits `words_entered == word_count` (line 560) and calls recovery_cipher_finalize() with only 11 real words in the buffer. In finalize, the words_entered != word_count check (line 656) passes, strtok(mnemonic, " ") collapses the empty token so 11 tokens all auto-complete (auto_completed stays true), and at line 720 with dry_run=false and enforce_wordlist=false the condition `(!enforce_wordlist || mnemonic_check(...))` short-circuits true, so setup_commit(final_mnemonic_scratch, imported=true) (reset.c:170) stores the 11-word string via storage_setMnemonic with no length/checksum validation and the device reports "Device recovered". enforce_wordlist is host-controlled (fsm_msg_common.h:739, defaults false when omitted) and the extra space is host-injectable via CharacterAck, so it is reachable from host input. With enforce_wordlist=true it correctly fails closed at mnemonic_check. Minor note: the audit's word numbering ("7." instead of "6.") is off by the display convention, but the substantive defect -- silently committing an invalid-length phrase -- holds.

### C3-045 — CONFIRMED — P3: prev_info retains a plaintext seed word after the ceremony ends: static, not CONFIDENTIAL, never memzero'd

Unit: `audit:setup-ceremony-recovery`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/recovery_cipher.c:445`.

next_character() formats "(N.word)" from last_completed_word into `static char prev_info[32]` (lines 445-449) for the previous-word indicator. Every other buffer that touches a decoded word (mnemonic, coded_word, decoded_word, last_completed_word, *_scratch) is CONFIDENTIAL and cleared in recovery_cipher_reset(); prev_info is neither. It is only reset to "" at the top of the NEXT next_character() call, so after a finished, cancelled, or failed recovery the last completed BIP-39 word of the user's seed persists in ordinary .bss until another recovery runs -- and it is outside the `confidential` section the bootloader wipes at boot. Violates invariant (4).

Host trace / impact: Complete or abort a RecoveryDevice ceremony. prev_info still holds e.g. "(23.zebra)" indefinitely. Any subsequent RAM disclosure (debug probe, memory-safety bug elsewhere, cold-boot) yields one seed word with its position, which is not otherwise obtainable after setup_abort(). Not host-reachable on its own.

Proposed remediation: Move prev_info to file scope as `static CONFIDENTIAL char prev_info[32];` and add `memzero(prev_info, sizeof(prev_info));` to recovery_cipher_reset(); also memzero it right after layout_cipher() returns in next_character(), matching the treatment of formatted_word_scratch on the line below.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436, lib/firmware/recovery_cipher.c. Line 445 declares `static char prev_info[32]` with no CONFIDENTIAL attribute (CMakeLists.txt:106 maps CONFIDENTIAL to section("confidential"); keepkey.ld:23-25 and tools/bootloader/main.c:81-83 wipe only that section at boot, so prev_info lives in ordinary .bss). Lines 447-449 snprintf "(N.word)" from last_completed_word into it whenever word_pos>0, i.e. once the host has completed at least one word via CharacterAck (fsm_msg_common.h:753 -> recovery_character -> next_character at line 584). recovery_cipher_reset() (lines 89-110) memzeros mnemonic, coded_mnemonic, cipher, coded_word, decoded_word, last_completed_word, and all four *_scratch buffers but never prev_info; recovery_cipher_abort() is just setup_abort(); the only other write is the `prev_info[0]='\0'` at line 446 on the NEXT next_character() call. grep shows no other reference to prev_info anywhere in lib/ or include/. layout_cipher (app_layout.c:799) takes it as const and does not clear it. So after a completed, cancelled, or failed recovery the last completed plaintext seed word plus its position persists in non-confidential RAM, and it is populated purely by host-driven input. Caveat on severity (matches the P3 rating): the residual is not host-readable by itself; extracting it requires a separate RAM-disclosure primitive (debug probe, unrelated memory-safety bug, cold boot). Fix is trivial: mark CONFIDENTIAL and memzero it in recovery_cipher_reset(), or make it a stack local memzero'd after layout_cipher.

### C3-046 — CONFIRMED — P3: Empty CharacterAck.character bypasses the 'a to z' check: strchr() matches the cipher's NUL terminator

Unit: `audit:setup-ceremony-recovery`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/recovery_cipher.c:482`.

recovery_character() validates the host byte with `pos = strchr(cipher, character[0])` and rejects only when character[0] != ' ' && pos == NULL. For character[0] == '\0' (an empty string is valid on the wire: max_size:2, and fsm_msgCharacterAck only forces character[1]='\0'), strchr returns a pointer to cipher's terminator, so the guard passes. decoded_character becomes english_alphabet[26] == '\0', all strlcat calls are no-ops, and next_character() runs -- re-randomising the displayed cipher without any user input or mnemonic change. No state corruption results, but the input filter documented at line 485 does not do what it says, and the same path lets a host re-roll the on-screen cipher at will (also reachable via del on an empty mnemonic).

Host trace / impact: During recovery send CharacterAck{character:""}. Device replies with a fresh CharacterRequest and redraws a new cipher; no Failure is raised even though the comment/failure text promise 'Character must be from a to z'.

Proposed remediation: Reject `character[0] == '\0'` explicitly before the strchr (or check `character[0] >= 'a' && character[0] <= 'z'` in addition to the space case) and abort with SyntaxError like any other invalid byte.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Reproduced at HEAD 71ba84436. messages.options gives CharacterAck.character max_size:2 (char[2]); fsm_msgCharacterAck only forces character[1]='\0' and does not check has_character/character[0]. In recovery_character() (recovery_cipher.c:482), strchr(cipher, '\0') returns a pointer to the terminator (C standard), so the 'a to z' guard at 485-490 passes. decoded_character becomes english_alphabet[26]=='\0', every strlcat is a no-op, and control reaches next_character() (line 584), which re-randomises cipher and redraws it, replying with a fresh CharacterRequest and no Failure. Host-reachable via CharacterAck{character:""}. Low severity (no state corruption), but the documented input filter is bypassed and the cipher can be re-rolled at will, exactly as claimed.

### C3-047 — REFUTED — P3: CHECK_NO_CEREMONY is defined twice, verbatim (dead duplicate)

Unit: `audit:setup-ceremony-recovery`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm.c:187`.

The ceremony-start guard macro and its explanatory comment appear identically at fsm.c:174-181 and again at fsm.c:187-194. Identical redefinition is legal C so it compiles under -Werror, but it is dead surface (invariant 5) and any future edit to one copy that leaves the other unchanged turns into a hard build error, or worse, silently loses the edit if the second copy is the one that survives.

Host trace / impact: None -- maintenance hazard only.

Proposed remediation: Delete the second definition (lines 183-194).

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Confirmed the textual fact at HEAD 71ba84436: lib/firmware/fsm.c has two verbatim, identical definitions of CHECK_NO_CEREMONY (lines 174-181 and 187-194, comment duplicated as well). However, identical macro redefinition is well-defined C (C11 6.10.3p2), so it compiles cleanly even with -Werror and produces no behavioral difference. It is not reachable from host input and does not fail device compile flags; the claim itself states 'Exploit: None -- maintenance hazard only.' Under the verifier criteria this is a cosmetic dead-duplicate cleanup, not a real defect.

### C3-048 — UNVERIFIED — P2: CRC-rejected record is either loaded unverified (sector 1) or silently erased (sectors 2/3) via the STORAGE_SECT_DEFAULT fallback

Unit: `audit:storage`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/storage.c:1720`.

find_active_storage() (lib/board/memory.c:312) deliberately rejects a record whose trailer is present but whose CRC does not match ("a torn/corrupt new record and must never be mistaken for legacy"). storage_init() then falls through to `storage_location = STORAGE_SECT_DEFAULT` (line 1722) and re-tests the sector with storage_isActiveSector() (line 1731), which checks ONLY the 4-byte "stor" magic. Two bad outcomes follow, neither of which matches the halt-on-doubt disposition the same function takes for recover_pending_storage() failure (lines 1713-1718):
(a) If the rejected record is in FLASH_STORAGE1 (the default and the most common resting sector), storage_fromFlash() parses the corrupt bytes with no integrity check at all, and because V17->V20 returns SUS_Updated (or any later commit) the corrupt content is re-serialised with a fresh, valid CRC -- the corruption is laundered permanently.
(b) If the rejected record is in FLASH_STORAGE2/3, sector 1 is not active, so storage_init() does storage_resetUuid()+storage_commit() (lines 1734-1735). storage_commit() writes the new empty record to next(next(S1)) = S3, erases S1, installs the marker, then at line 2066 erases next(S1) = S2 as the 'old marker' sector. Whichever of S2/S3 held the user's only wallet record is erased on the very first boot, with no prompt and no warning, and the device presents as factory-fresh.
This is a regression introduced by the CRC/generation change (aa88b996a): before it, a bit-flipped record was loaded as-is; now a single flipped bit in the 2572-byte payload (bit-rot, marginal cell, or a glitch during the verification memcmp in storage_commit) converts to wallet loss or silent corrupt-state adoption depending only on which sector the record happened to land in.

Host trace / impact: Any single-bit corruption of a finalised record in S2 or S3 (or a fault injected on the calc_crc32 comparison at memory.c:309 during boot) causes: no active record -> no pending record -> default S1 -> not active -> reset+commit -> line 2066 erases the sector holding the wallet. User with no seed backup loses funds; no confirmation is ever shown. For S1 the same corruption instead yields an unverified parse (e.g. flipped has_pin / passphrase_protection / wrapped key bits) that is then committed with a valid CRC.

Proposed remediation: After both find_active_storage() and find_pending_storage() fail, scan FLASH_STORAGE1..3 for any sector carrying STORAGE_MAGIC_STR (or a non-erased trailer). If one exists, treat storage as corrupt-but-present: layout_warning_static("Storage corrupt. Reboot device!") + shutdown() (the same disposition as the recover failure branch) rather than initialising a new wallet, and never let storage_commit() erase a sector that still carries magic unless the user explicitly wiped. Replace the magic-only storage_isActiveSector() gate at line 1731 with the verified/legacy result from find_active_storage() so a CRC-rejected sector can never be parsed.

Verification: 0/2 required verdicts recovered.

Continuation: re-verify before deciding whether to fix.

### C3-049 — UNVERIFIED — P3: Bitcoin-only version ladder maps burned versions 18/19 (and 0-10) onto the wrong reader instead of refusing -- sibling of the 'burned versions' fix

Unit: `audit:storage`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/storage.c:1613`.

d51752314/8c6895291 made the multi-chain switch refuse StorageVersion_18/19 explicitly (lines 1587-1589) because alpha builds at e109404ee wrote those layouts. The same commit range (e109404ee) also had the bitcoin-only band with BITCOIN_ONLY builds stamping STORAGE_VERSION_BTC_ONLY_BASE+18/19, and at that revision the band ladder dispatched underlying 18 -> storage_readV18 and 19 -> storage_readV19. At HEAD the ladder is `<=15 -> readV11, ==16 -> readV16, ==17 -> readV17, else -> readV20` (lines 1613-1621), so underlying 18 and 19 now fall into storage_readV20. For a 10019 blob the V19 PIN-KDF flag (bit 20) is silently dropped (pin_kdf_v2=false), so a key wrapped with PIN_KDF_V19 is unwrapped with V16 parameters -- exactly the permanent-lockout failure described at lines 528-534 -- and the passkey area is parsed from bytes that were random V17 reserved padding. Likewise underlying 0..10 (V1/V2 plaintext-PIN layouts) are fed to storage_readV11. All of these are bounded reads (16 KiB sector, fixed offsets) so no memory-safety issue, but the fail-closed disposition the multi-chain switch has for these versions is missing on the bitcoin-only build.

Host trace / impact: A bitcoin-only device that ran the e109404ee-era alpha and holds a 10019 record in FLASH_STORAGE1 (note: find_active_storage rejects it because bytes 2572.. hold the zeroed identity block, but storage_init's default-sector fallback still parses S1 by magic -- see finding 1). Boot -> storage_fromFlash -> BTC_ONLY branch -> readV20 -> every PIN entry fails -> pin_failed_attempts climbs to the wipe threshold -> wallet wiped after a confusing lockout rather than the intended immediate SUS_Invalid/Locked disposition. A physical attacker can also stamp 10018/10019 to exercise the untested reader path.

Proposed remediation: Mirror the multi-chain switch inside the band ladder: `if (underlying == 18 || underlying == 19 || underlying < 11) return SUS_Invalid;` (or SUS_BitcoinOnlyLocked if refuse-not-wipe is preferred), and add a unit test that feeds BASE+18 and BASE+19 records to storage_fromFlash under BITCOIN_ONLY=1.

Verification: 0/1 required verdicts recovered.

Continuation: re-verify before deciding whether to fix.

### C3-050 — REFUTED — P3: Length guards in the V11/V16/V17 (de)serialisers do not cover the bytes actually accessed -- sibling of the 8283b0abe off-by-one fix

Unit: `audit:storage`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/storage.c:1100`.

8283b0abe fixed storage_readPolicyV1/writePolicyV1 so the `len` guard matches the farthest access. The same pattern is wrong in every larger serialiser: storage_readStorageV11 guards `len < 852` (line 1100) but reads encrypted_sec at ptr+468..1492 (line 1159); storage_readStorageV16Plaintext/writeStorageV16Plaintext guard 852 while storage_readStorageV16 reads to 1501+512=2013 (line 1307) and storage_writeStorageV16 writes to 1497+1024 (line 1232); storage_readStorageV17/writeStorageV17 guard 852 while accessing 1501+1024=2525 (lines 1332,1350); storage_readV1 guards `len < 572` (line 1448) but readStorageV1 reads the cache at 484..559 of a 481-length window and passes literal 481. storage_readV11/readV16/readV17 (lines 1460-1494) pass the literal 852 instead of the remaining length, so the guard is dead text. Today every firmware caller hands a full 16 KiB sector or the 2580-byte flash_temp, so there is no live overflow, but the guards give the false impression that a 1 KiB buffer is safe (storage_readV11/writeV17 accept `len >= 1024`), and unittests/firmware/storage.cpp exercises exactly those APIs with caller-sized vectors.

Host trace / impact: Not reachable from the wire at HEAD. Latent: any future caller (or test) that sizes a buffer to the documented 852/1024 minimum and calls storage_writeStorageV17/storage_writeV17 writes 1.5 KiB past the buffer; a reader over a 1024-byte copy reads adjacent stack/static memory into encrypted_sec.

Proposed remediation: Set each guard to the farthest byte touched (V11: 1492, V16 read: 2013, V16/V17 write and V17 read: 2525, V1: 559) and make storage_readV11/readV16/readV17 and the write wrappers pass `len - 44` rather than literal 852, as storage_readV20/writeV20 already do.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Verified at 71ba84436: the length guards in storage_readStorageV11/V16/V17 (len < 852) and storage_readV11/V16/V17/writeV17 (len < 1024, passing literal 852) do not cover the farthest accessed bytes, as claimed. However, the only callers at HEAD are storage_fromFlash (lines 1569-1618) which always pass STORAGE_SECTOR_LEN (16 KiB flash sector) and the static flash_temp[STORAGE_RECORD_LEN] (2580 B) buffer; `len` and the buffer size are compile-time constants, never derived from host/wire input. No undersized buffer exists, so there is no out-of-bounds read/write reachable from host input. The audit itself states "Not reachable from the wire at HEAD" — this is a latent, hygiene-only defect (dead/misleading guards), not a real exploitable bug under the given criteria.

### C3-051 — UNVERIFIED — P3: Dead storage surface retained: burned-V19 reader/writer, flash-controlled policy V1 codec, unused writers and undefined prototypes

Unit: `audit:storage`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/storage.c:1415`.

Unreachable from firmware (only unittests/firmware/storage.cpp or nothing at all references them): storage_writeStorageV19/storage_readStorageV19 (lines 1415-1426) and storage_writeV19/storage_readV19 (lines 1513-1523) implement the BURNED version-19 format that storage_versions.inc says must never be parsed -- yet a live reader that honours flag bit 20 and a writer that emits it remain compiled in; storage_readPolicyV1/storage_writePolicyV1 (lines 321-345) are the flash-controlled-policy-name codec whose only firmware caller was removed by the prior audit fix in storage_readStorageV1; storage_writeV11/storage_writeV16/storage_writeV17 (lines 1466-1494) have no firmware callers; storage_getShadowMnemonic (line 2678) has no callers anywhere; storageHasWipeCode/storageChangeWipeCode (lib/firmware/storage.h:271-273) are declared and never defined; storage_dumpNode's non-DEBUG_LINK body is an empty stub exported unconditionally (line 2093). Each is extra attack surface for the next refactor (invariant 5).

Host trace / impact: None today; the risk is a future caller wiring storage_readStorageV19 or storage_readPolicyV1 back into storage_fromFlash and re-opening the burned-format / flash-controlled-policy paths the prior audit closed.

Proposed remediation: Delete the V19 codec, the PolicyV1 codec, the unused V11/V16/V17 write wrappers, storage_getShadowMnemonic, and the two undefined prototypes; move storage_dumpNode fully under #if DEBUG_LINK. Port the affected unit tests to the V20 codec or drop them.

Verification: 0/1 required verdicts recovered.

Continuation: re-verify before deciding whether to fix.

### C3-052 — CONFIRMED — P2: ApplySettings passphrase toggle leaves the session seed/passphrase cache intact, so the wallet in use no longer matches what the user confirmed

Unit: `audit:pin-passphrase-session`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_common.h:701`.

fsm_msgApplySettings applies msg->use_passphrase via storage_setPassphraseProtected() (a bare flag write, lib/firmware/storage.c:2591) and never calls session_clear(). The session keeps session.seedCached/session.seed (and session.passphraseCached/session.passphrase) from the previous setting. In storage_getRootNode (storage.c:2554) the mnemonic branch only computes a seed when !session.seedCached, and storage_getSeed (storage.c:2429) returns the cached seed whenever seedUsesPassphrase matches. Consequences: (a) ENABLE: device already unlocked with a seed derived from passphrase "" -> host enables passphrase -> next key op runs passphrase_protect(), the host supplies "X", the OLED shows the passphrase-confirmation screen with "X" and session_cachePassphrase("X") runs -- but seedCached is still true, so every address/signature comes from the NON-passphrase wallet while the user was just shown "X". (b) DISABLE: seed derived from hidden-wallet passphrase stays cached after passphrase protection is turned off; GetFeatures reports passphrase_protection=false and no passphrase is ever requested, yet the device keeps signing with the hidden wallet until Initialize/lock. Both are display-vs-actual mismatches in the unit's core guarantee (the review screen is 'the only place the user gets to see the passphrase their keys will be derived from', passphrase_sm.c:148). The xprv (has_node) branch is unaffected because it re-derives from session.passphrase each call.

Host trace / impact: User has passphrase enabled and has opened hidden wallet "X" (seed cached). Malware sends ApplySettings{use_passphrase:false}; the user approves 'Disable BIP39 passphrases?' and enters PIN. Malware then requests a GetAddress/SignTx: no passphrase prompt appears (protection off), but the derived keys are still wallet "X"'s -- funds are received to / spent from a wallet the user believes is closed. Mirror case: with passphrase just enabled, the user types "X", confirms "X" on-screen, and gets the plain-wallet address instead; a deposit intended for the hidden wallet lands in the wallet the host controls the view of.

Proposed remediation: In fsm_msgApplySettings, when msg->has_use_passphrase and the value differs from storage_getPassphraseProtected(), call session_clear(false) (drops seed + passphrase caches, keeps PIN) after storage_setPassphraseProtected(); or move the clear into storage_setPassphraseProtected() itself so every caller (recovery/reset paths too) gets it. Add a unit test: cache seed with passphrase off, toggle on, cache passphrase "X", assert storage_getRootNode yields the "X"-derived node.

Verification: 2/2 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. fsm_msgApplySettings (lib/firmware/fsm_msg_common.h, has_use_passphrase branch after CHECK_PIN_UNCACHED) calls storage_setPassphraseProtected(), which is a bare `shadow_config.storage.pub.passphrase_protection = passphrase;` (storage.c:2591-2593). No session invalidation anywhere on that path: session_clear() is only called from pin_sm.c:262 (wipe code), fsm.c:356 (Initialize) and home_sm.c:123 (auto-lock); pin_protect_uncached()/pin_protect() (pin_sm.c) never touches session.seedCached. All key ops go through fsm_getDerivedNode -> storage_getRootNode(curve, true, ...) (fsm.c:272). In the mnemonic branch (storage.c:2540-2575) passphrase_protect() runs first, then `if (!session.seedCached)` gates any recomputation, so with seedCached still true from before the toggle, hdnode_from_seed(session.seed) uses the stale seed. ENABLE case: passphrase_protect() (passphrase_sm.c:206) sees protection on and passphraseCached false, requests + confirms "X" on screen and session_cachePassphrase("X"), but the seed derived from "" stays cached -> keys are from the plain wallet despite the on-screen confirmation of "X". DISABLE case: passphrase_protect() short-circuits to true, no prompt, and the hidden-wallet seed stays cached -> device keeps signing/deriving for wallet "X" while GetFeatures reports passphrase_protection=false. Reachable from host input (ApplySettings + user's confirm/PIN, then any GetAddress/SignTx). The xprv (has_node) branch re-derives per call and is unaffected, as the claim states.

Verifier 2: REAL. Reproduced in code at HEAD 71ba84436 of keepkey-firmware-alphafix. fsm_msgApplySettings (lib/firmware/fsm_msg_common.h:701) calls storage_setPassphraseProtected(msg->use_passphrase), which is a bare flag write (storage.c:2591-2593), then storage_commit(); nothing on that path touches the session cache. CHECK_PIN_UNCACHED -> pin_protect() only calls session_clear() on the wipe-code branch (pin_sm.c:262); the success path just resets pin fails. session_clear() is otherwise only reached from Initialize, ClearSession, auto-lock, WipeDevice/reset paths. Every key op goes through fsm_getDerivedNode -> storage_getRootNode(curve, /*usePassphrase=*/true) (fsm.c:272). In the mnemonic branch (storage.c:2545-2575) passphrase_protect() is called, then the seed is only (re)derived under `if (!session.seedCached)` with no check of which passphrase produced it; hdnode_from_seed(session.seed) uses the stale seed. storage_getSeed (2429) likewise returns session.seed whenever seedCached && usePassphrase==seedUsesPassphrase (always true for these callers). DISABLE: hidden-wallet seed "X" cached (seedCached=true) -> ApplySettings{use_passphrase:false} approved -> next GetAddress: passphrase_protect() short-circuits (storage_getPassphraseProtected()==false, passphrase_sm.c:207) with no prompt, seedCached still true, signs/derives with wallet "X" while GetFeatures reports passphrase_protection=false. ENABLE: plain seed cached -> enable -> next op prompts, user confirms "X", session_cachePassphrase("X") (storage.c:2595) sets only passphraseCached, seedCached remains true, so the plain-wallet seed is used despite the on-screen confirmation of "X". The has_node/xprv branch re-decrypts from session.passphrase each call (storage.c:2512-2540) and is unaffected, as the claim states. Reachable from host input (ApplySettings + user confirm/PIN, then any address/sign request). Fix: call session_clear(false) (or at minimum reset seedCached/passphraseCached) whenever passphrase_protection changes in fsm_msgApplySettings.

### C3-053 — CONFIRMED — P3: Plaintext passphrase copied into an un-scrubbed stack buffer (sibling of the fc2c15a6d fix)

Unit: `audit:pin-passphrase-session`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/passphrase_sm.c:59`.

wait_for_passphrase_ack() declares `uint8_t msg_tiny_buf[MSG_TINY_BFR_SZ]` on the stack; wait_for_tiny_msg() memcpy's the whole decoded PassphraseAck (plaintext passphrase, up to 50 bytes) into it, strlcpy() takes a copy into passphrase_info, and the function returns without memzero. Commit fc2c15a6d moved PassphraseInfo into a static CONFIDENTIAL buffer and zeroes it on every exit precisely so the passphrase never sits unzeroed in RAM, but this second copy -- the one the plaintext arrives in -- was left on the stack. Every other tiny-message consumer in the tree already follows the fixed pattern: confirm_sm.c:197 and dice_input.c:212 use `static CONFIDENTIAL uint8_t msg_tiny_buf[]` and memzero it. The same residue also exists one level down in messages.c msg_read_tiny(): the raw 64-byte USB frame is copied into local `uint8_t buf[64]` (line 374) and never zeroed, and the CONFIDENTIAL static msg_tiny keeps the decoded PassphraseAck until the next tiny message (only boot clears the section).

Host trace / impact: Any later stack-disclosure or memory-read primitive (e.g. the msg_write over-read class reported as P3 'board-io' in the prior audit, a debug-link read, or a JTAG/glitch dump short of a power cycle) can recover the passphrase from stale stack frames or from msg_tiny after the user has finished entering it, defeating the reason the prior fix made PassphraseInfo CONFIDENTIAL. Passphrase = hidden-wallet key material.

Proposed remediation: Make msg_tiny_buf `static CONFIDENTIAL` in wait_for_passphrase_ack() and memzero it after the strlcpy (and on every switch exit), matching confirm_sm.c/dice_input.c. In messages.c msg_read_tiny(), memzero(buf) before return, and memzero(msg_tiny) in tiny_msg_poll_and_buffer() right after the memcpy to the caller's buffer so the plaintext has exactly one owner.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Traced at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/passphrase_sm.c:59-69: wait_for_passphrase_ack() declares an automatic `uint8_t msg_tiny_buf[MSG_TINY_BFR_SZ]`, wait_for_tiny_msg() -> tiny_msg_poll_and_buffer() (lib/board/messages.c:466-485) does `memcpy(buf, msg_tiny, sizeof(msg_tiny))` copying the pb_decode'd PassphraseAck (plaintext passphrase) into that stack buffer, strlcpy copies it into passphrase_info, and the function returns with no memzero of msg_tiny_buf. The same file's passphrase_protect() (lines 203-216, from fc2c15a6d) made PassphraseInfo static CONFIDENTIAL and zeroes it on entry/exit precisely so the passphrase never sits unzeroed in RAM, so this stack copy defeats that fix. One level down, msg_read_tiny() (messages.c:372-375) copies the raw 64-byte USB frame into automatic `uint8_t buf[64]` that is never zeroed, and the static CONFIDENTIAL `msg_tiny` (messages.c:357) retains the decoded PassphraseAck after the copy-out with no clear until the next tiny message or boot. Reachable from host input: any PassphraseAck sent while the device is in PASSPHRASE_WAITING with passphrase protection enabled. Minor inaccuracy in the claim: confirm_sm.c:197 uses a static CONFIDENTIAL buffer but does not itself memzero msg_tiny_buf (dice_input.c:385 does); this does not change the finding. It is a residue/hygiene defect requiring a secondary memory-read primitive to exploit, but the defect exists and is host-reachable.

### C3-054 — CONFIRMED — P3: PinMatrixAck stack copy never scrubbed; PIN key-positions survive on the stack after pin_protect() zeroes PINInfo

Unit: `audit:pin-passphrase-session`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/pin_sm.c:54`.

check_for_pin_ack() decodes the PinMatrixAck into a stack `uint8_t msg_tiny_buf[MSG_TINY_BFR_SZ]` and returns without memzero. Commit be47a9f4f added memzero of PINInfo on every exit path of pin_protect()/change_pin_staged()/change_wipe_code(), but the buffer the PIN arrives in was missed. The value is the matrix-position string, which together with the (also unzeroed until strlcpy'd over) pin_matrix at the same moment is the PIN; even alone it is the exact PinMatrixAck the host sent and is replayable within the same matrix. Same shape as the passphrase finding; reported separately because the fix commit differs.

Host trace / impact: A stack-disclosure primitive reached after PIN entry (same class as above) recovers the last PinMatrixAck; combined with pin_matrix residue or a same-session replay it yields the device PIN, undermining the wipe-on-fail / brute-force delay design.

Proposed remediation: Use `static CONFIDENTIAL uint8_t msg_tiny_buf[MSG_TINY_BFR_SZ]` and memzero it after the strlcpy into pin_info->pin (and on the Cancel/Initialize/default exits), as confirm_sm.c does.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/pin_sm.c:52-86 check_for_pin_ack() declares stack `uint8_t msg_tiny_buf[MSG_TINY_BFR_SZ]`, receives the host's PinMatrixAck via check_for_tiny_msg() (lib/board/messages.c:466-485 memcpy's the whole static CONFIDENTIAL msg_tiny into it), strlcpy's pma->pin into pin_info->pin, and returns without any memzero of msg_tiny_buf. All memzero calls in pin_sm.c (lines 233,252,265,272,277,312,313,323,350,351) cover PINInfo structs / pin[] / warn_msg_fmt only; the tiny-message buffer was missed. Reachable from host input on every PinMatrixAck through run_pin_state() PIN_WAITING. The residue is the raw matrix-position string exactly as the host sent it, so it is replayable within the same matrix and combines with pin_matrix residue to yield the PIN. Note: the same unscrubbed-local pattern also exists in passphrase_sm.c:59 (msg_tiny_buf holding PassphraseAck) and the static msg_tiny in messages.c is a separate, longer-lived copy (marked CONFIDENTIAL but not cleared after consumption).

### C3-055 — CONFIRMED — P3: CHECK_NO_CEREMONY macro (and its comment block) is defined twice verbatim

Unit: `audit:pin-passphrase-session`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm.c:183`.

Lines 170-181 and 183-194 are byte-identical definitions of CHECK_NO_CEREMONY. C permits a benign redefinition only while the replacement lists are token-identical, so this compiles under -Werror today, but the second copy is dead surface: the first edit that touches one copy and not the other turns into a hard 'redefined' error under the device flags, and a reviewer reading the second copy may miss that the first is the live one. Likely a merge/rebase artifact of the ceremony-hardening fix.

Host trace / impact: No direct exploit; dead-surface/build-portability defect (invariants 5 and 6). Any future divergence between the two copies fails the device build.

Proposed remediation: Delete lines 183-194 (the duplicate comment and #define), leaving a single definition.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Verified at HEAD 71ba84436: lib/firmware/fsm.c contains two byte-identical `#define CHECK_NO_CEREMONY` blocks (lines 174-181 and 187-194, each preceded by the same comment). Identical redefinition is legal C so it compiles today, but any edit to one copy that does not mirror the other produces a hard "CHECK_NO_CEREMONY redefined" error under -Werror, and the duplicate is dead/misleading surface. Not host-reachable (no runtime effect); it is the build-portability/dead-surface defect as claimed. Claim's line numbers are off by ~4 but the finding is accurate.

### C3-056 — CONFIRMED — P3: CTAPHID_INIT-abort during a CTAP2 user-presence wait re-opens the in-flight request buffer for host writes; the command keeps running on it and still signs

Unit: `audit:u2f-ctap`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/u2f.c:749`.

While ctap2_handle() is executing on reader->buf (u2fhid_cbor(), u2f.c:436) the transport sits in request_user_presence()'s loop calling usbPoll() (u2f.c:748-763), which re-enters u2fhid_read(tiny=1). The loop's only abort test is `reader->cmd == U2FHID_CANCEL` (u2f.c:749). A CTAPHID_INIT frame on the active cid takes the abort branch at u2f.c:146-151 and sets reader->cmd = 0, reader->len = 0, reader->seq = 255 -- but the CTAP2 command is not told and keeps waiting for the button. A following CTAPHID_CBOR init frame on the same cid now passes the busy guard at u2f.c:163 (reader->cmd is 0, not U2FHID_CBOR), reaches u2fhid_init_cmd() (u2f.c:197-205) and memcpy()s 57 fresh host bytes into reader->buf[0..57), after which continuation frames overwrite the rest. make_credential()/get_assertion() still hold CborValue pointers into that buffer (client_hash.data, user_id.data) and, once the user presses the button for the ORIGINAL prompt, sign/store from the rewritten bytes and send the response on the current cid. Every pointer stays inside the 7609-byte buffer (lengths were captured before the overwrite), so this is not a memory-safety bug, and rp_id/rp_id_hash were copied to the stack before the wait, so the RP shown on screen is the RP bound -- but the transport spec says an INIT on an active channel discards the transaction, whereas here the transaction completes, the second request is silently dropped at u2f.c:294 with no reply, and the response for the first request is emitted after the host believes the channel was resynchronized. A broadcast INIT in the same window additionally rotates the global cid via next_cid() (u2f.c:368) so KEEPALIVEs and the final response go out on a channel that was never used for the request.

Host trace / impact: Host sends CBOR authenticatorGetAssertion(rpId=X, clientDataHash=A); device shows 'Sign in to X?'. Before the user presses, host sends CTAPHID_INIT on the same cid (abort), then a second CTAPHID_CBOR message with clientDataHash=B (or a whole different command byte). User presses for the X prompt; device returns an assertion over B (or, for makeCredential, stores user.id/user.name taken from the second message) and never answers the second message. Impact is confined to host-controlled fields and same-host channels, so this is a transaction-integrity/state-machine defect rather than a display-vs-signed break.

Proposed remediation: Make the in-flight command own the transport state: in request_user_presence() treat any reader->cmd other than U2FHID_CBOR (0 after an INIT abort, or CANCEL) as an abort and return false; and in u2fhid_read()'s tiny init branch refuse (ERR_CHANNEL_BUSY) any non-CANCEL init frame while a command is executing (track an explicit `in_flight` flag set around the switch at u2f.c:254-291 rather than inferring it from reader->cmd, which the INIT abort clears). Do not let next_cid() rotate the global cid while a command is in flight (allocate the new cid into the INIT response only).

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Reproduced in code at HEAD 71ba84436. u2fhid_cbor() (u2f.c:436) passes reader->buf directly to ctap2_handle(); get_assertion()/make_credential() keep CborValue pointers into that buffer (ctap2_cbor.c:179 sets value->data = decoder->buffer + offset) and use client_hash.data / user_id.data AFTER ctap2_request_user_presence() (ctap2.c ~833 then ~853/871 for get_assertion; 713 then 726/743 for make_credential). request_user_presence()'s loop (u2f.c:748-763) calls usbPoll() and only aborts on reader->cmd == U2FHID_CANCEL. A CTAPHID_INIT on the active cid hits u2f.c:146-151 (tiny=1, reader!=0, f->cid==cid) and zeroes reader->cmd/len, seq=255, with no signal to the running command. A subsequent CBOR init frame on that cid then reaches the TYPE_INIT && seq==255 branch, skips the busy guard (reader->cmd is 0, not U2FHID_CBOR), and u2fhid_init_cmd() (u2f.c:197-205) memcpys 57 host bytes into reader->buf, with continuation frames overwriting further bytes (bounds check keeps them inside the 7609-byte buffer). The presence loop keeps running (cmd is now U2FHID_CBOR, not CANCEL); when the user presses for the original prompt, the assertion/credential is signed/stored from the rewritten bytes and the second request is never dispatched (after the command returns, the outer loop resets reader->cmd=0 at u2f.c:294). Broadcast INIT also rotates cid via next_cid() (u2f.c:368), moving keepalives/response to a fresh channel. Host-reachable via ordinary CTAPHID frames; severity as stated (transaction-integrity/state-machine defect, host-controlled fields only, not memory-unsafe, RP binding intact).

### C3-057 — CONFIRMED — P3: user.name silently truncated at 47 bytes without a UTF-8 boundary; a discoverable credential can then emit an invalid CBOR text string in every UV assertion

Unit: `audit:u2f-ctap`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/ctap2.c:660`.

make_credential() copies user.name with `copy = min(value.length, sizeof(user_name)-1)` (ctap2.c:660-663) and NUL-terminates -- a byte truncation with no code-point boundary check, unlike copy_text() which fails closed. The decoder only guaranteed the ORIGINAL string was valid UTF-8 (ctap2_cbor.c:182-184); cutting a 2-4 byte sequence at byte 47 leaves a dangling lead byte. The truncated name is persisted via strlcpy at ctap2.c:479 and later re-emitted verbatim with cbor_encode_text() in get_assertion (ctap2.c:901) and get_next_assertion/encode_assertion_response (ctap2.c:549) whenever uv_verified is set. cbor_encode_text() does no UTF-8 check, so the device produces a major-type-3 string that is not valid UTF-8. This violates invariant (2): a bounds failure is papered over by truncation instead of being refused, and the result is a malformed response. CTAP 2.1 permits truncating user.name only at a code-point boundary and only to >= 64 bytes; 47 bytes violates both.

Host trace / impact: RP registers a resident credential with rk=true and a user.name whose 47th byte falls inside a multi-byte character (e.g. 46 ASCII chars followed by 'é'). Registration succeeds. On the next PIN-verified getAssertion the device returns {4: {id, name: <invalid UTF-8>}}; strict CBOR decoders (Chromium's, python-fido2) reject the whole response, so login with that passkey fails until the credential is recreated. Not a key/fund risk; a fail-open truncation that yields a protocol-invalid response.

Proposed remediation: Either fail closed (reject user.name longer than the store can hold with CTAP2_ERR_LIMIT_EXCEEDED / CTAP2_ERR_INVALID_LENGTH, as copy_text does for rp.id), or truncate at the last complete UTF-8 code point below the cap and raise PASSKEY_USER_NAME_MAX to at least 65 so the CTAP 2.1 minimum of 64 bytes is met. In either case validate stored user_name with valid_utf8() before cbor_encode_text().

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. include/keepkey/firmware/passkey.h:10 sets PASSKEY_USER_NAME_MAX 48; lib/firmware/ctap2.c:657-663 copies user.name with `copy = min(value.length, sizeof(user_name)-1)` via raw memcpy and NUL-terminates — a pure byte truncation at 47 bytes with no code-point-boundary check (contrast copy_text() at ctap2.c:191-200, which rejects oversize input). The decoder (ctap2_cbor.c:182-184) only validates the original text as UTF-8, so a multi-byte sequence straddling byte 47 leaves a dangling lead byte. That truncated buffer is persisted at ctap2.c:479 via strlcpy (storage.c:1394 only NUL-terminates, no sanitization) and re-emitted verbatim in get_assertion (ctap2.c:899-902) and encode_assertion_response (ctap2.c:547-550) with cbor_encode_text(), which (ctap2_cbor.c:106-109) just writes head + raw bytes with no UTF-8 check. Reachable from host input: makeCredential with rk=true and a user.name ≥48 bytes whose 47th byte is inside a multi-byte char succeeds, then any uv_verified getAssertion for that RP emits a major-type-3 string that is invalid UTF-8. Not a key/fund risk; a fail-open truncation producing a protocol-invalid response.

### C3-058 — REFUTED — P3: make_credential leaves the credential private key un-scrubbed on the encode_authenticator_data() failure exit

Unit: `audit:u2f-ctap`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/ctap2.c:735`.

After u2f_generate_credential() fills the stack buffer private_key (ctap2.c:716-720), every subsequent early return scrubs it (store_discoverable_credential failure at 726, ecdsa_sign failure at 745) except the `if (!encode_authenticator_data(...)) { write_error(CTAP2_ERR_OTHER,...); return; }` path at ctap2.c:735-741, which returns with private_key intact on the stack. With auth_data[256] and the 196-byte encoding this branch is unreachable today, so it is a latent sibling of the scrub-on-every-exit pattern the prior audit enforced elsewhere, not a live leak.

Host trace / impact: Not reachable with current buffer sizes; becomes a live 32-byte ES256 private key left in SRAM (later readable via any stack-disclosure bug, e.g. the class of over-read the previous audit found in usb.c) if auth_data shrinks or the COSE/AAGUID encoding grows.

Proposed remediation: Add memzero(private_key, sizeof(private_key)) before the return at ctap2.c:739 (or restructure make_credential with a single cleanup label that scrubs private_key, signature_base and signature on every exit).

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Code matches the description (lib/firmware/ctap2.c:735-741 returns without memzero(private_key) unlike the sibling exits at 726/745), but encode_authenticator_data() can only fail on compile-time capacity checks (capacity<37, capacity<119) or encode_cose_public_key overflowing the remaining 137 bytes of the fixed 256-byte auth_data with a fixed ~77-byte COSE key. No host input influences the encoded size, so the branch is dead at HEAD 71ba84436; the claim itself concedes it is "not a live leak" and "not reachable with current buffer sizes". Latent hygiene inconsistency only, not a reachable defect.

### C3-059 — REFUTED — P3: Dead surface in the U2F/CTAP unit: #if 0 blocks, prototypes without definitions, and CBOR helpers used only by tests

Unit: `audit:u2f-ctap`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/u2f.c:70`.

Invariant (5) violations at HEAD: (a) u2f.c:70-75 and 379-401 are `#if 0` circular-buffer code (u2f_out_packets, queue_u2f_pkt, u2f_out_data) superseded by the board queue; u2f.c:786-796 is an `#if 0`/`#else` BOGUS_APPID branch, and BOGUS_APPID (u2f.c:89) is otherwise unused. (b) include/keepkey/firmware/u2f.h declares u2fhid_write(), u2fhid_sync(), u2fhid_lock(), u2f_out_data() which have no definition anywhere in lib/ -- any future call site links only by accident of the #if 0 body. (c) ctap2_cbor.c exports cbor_validate() (no callers at all) and cbor_map_find_int()/cbor_map_find_int_slice() (called only from unittests/firmware/ctap2.cpp); ctap2.c re-implements the same lookup as its private map_find()/valid_request_map(), so the firmware carries two divergent map-walkers -- the exported ones enforce sorted-uint keys, the private one does not -- and only the private one is fuzzed through ctap2_handle. (d) the `legacy_credentials_enabled` output of storage_getPasskeyCredentialGeneration() in generateKeyHandle() is fetched and then `(void)`-discarded (u2f.c:641-648).

Host trace / impact: No direct exploit; the risk is the audited-vs-shipped divergence (two CBOR map walkers with different canonicality rules; declared-but-undefined transport commands) that lets a later change wire in an unreviewed path.

Proposed remediation: Delete the #if 0 blocks and BOGUS_APPID; remove the undefined prototypes from u2f.h; either make ctap2.c use cbor_map_find_int_slice()/cbor_validate() (one walker, one rule set) or move the test-only helpers into the unit test and drop them from the firmware image; drop the unused legacy flag fetch in generateKeyHandle() or pass a NULL-tolerant signature.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Facts verified at 71ba84436 (u2f.c #if 0 blocks at 70-75/379-401/786-796; u2f.h:42-52 declares u2fhid_write/u2fhid_sync/u2fhid_lock/u2f_out_data with no definition in lib/; cbor_validate has no callers and cbor_map_find_int/_slice are called only from unittests/firmware/*.cpp; u2f.c:648 discards legacy_credentials_enabled). But every item is dead/uncompiled code or an unused export: nothing here executes on any host-driven path (#if 0 code is not compiled, undefined prototypes have zero call sites, the test-only CBOR walkers are not on the ctap2_handle path, and the discarded flag does not alter key-handle derivation). No defect reachable from host input and no compile failure under device flags, so by the verifier criterion this is code hygiene, not a real finding.

### C3-060 — CONFIRMED — P2: Plaintext TOTP cache is keyed to nothing: survives Initialize/passphrase change, so a different passphrase wallet reads and overwrites the wrong authblock

Unit: `audit:authenticator`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/authenticator.c:67`.

getAuthData() (authenticator.c:66-76) loads the decrypted authblock into the static `authData[]` once and then trusts it forever (`localAuthdataUpdate=false`). The cache is only dropped by authenticator_clear_cache(), which storage.c:1849-1852 calls from session_clear() ONLY when clear_pin==true. fsm_msgInitialize (fsm_msg_common.h:9) calls session_clear(false): that path does `ss->passphraseCached=false; memset(passphrase)` (storage.c:1911-1914) but leaves `authData[]` and `localAuthdataUpdate=false` intact. The stored authblock, however, is encrypted under a key derived from the session passphrase (storage_deriveAuthdataKey, storage.c:730) and fingerprinted per passphrase. After Initialize, checkPassphrase() re-prompts (passphrase_sm.c:206-210) and the host's PassphraseAck can carry any passphrase B; getAuthData() then returns true without re-decrypting, so every Ping authenticator op runs against passphrase-A plaintext while session.passphrase == B. addAuthAccount()/removeAuthAccount() then call setAuthData() (authenticator.c:213/423) which re-fingerprints A's slots and storage_setAuthData (storage.c:923-946) encrypts them under B's key and commits over B's authblock. The same stale-cache path is hit when ApplySettings toggles passphrase_protection off while the cache is warm: setAuthData then writes A's secrets back in plaintext with authdata_encrypted=false. Hosts send Initialize before nearly every operation (the storage.c comment at 1893-1895 says so), so this is a routine sequence, not an exotic one.

Host trace / impact: 1. User unlocks (PIN) with passphrase A and generates one OTP: authData[] now holds A's TOTP secrets. 2. Host (any software, or malware, on an unlocked device) sends Initialize, then Ping 'initializeAuth:x:y:<base32>' and answers the passphrase prompt with B (an empty string or another wallet's passphrase). 3. Device shows only 'Add Auth Account x/y' + the secret screen; user approves what looks like a normal add. 4. setAuthData() writes A's whole slot table plus the new entry encrypted under B and overwrites B's authblock and fingerprint. Result: wallet B's previously stored TOTP accounts are destroyed; wallet A's accounts now fail with 'passphrase incorrect for authdata' (fingerprint mismatch) forever unless A knows B. The reverse direction (generateOTP under B from A's cache) also lets a host that only knows passphrase B drive OTP generation from A's secrets (button-gated, but the screen never names which wallet the secret belongs to).

Proposed remediation: Invalidate the cache whenever the passphrase that keyed it can change: call authenticator_clear_cache() unconditionally in session_clear() (not only under clear_pin), and also in session_cachePassphrase() and storage_setPassphraseProtected(). Alternatively, store alongside the cache the authdata_fingerprint it was decrypted against and, in getAuthData(), re-load when `shadow_config.storage.pub.authdata_fingerprint` or the session passphrase state differs. Add a unit test: load cache under passphrase A, session_clear(false), cache passphrase B, expect getAuthAccount() to return BADPASS rather than A's data.

Verification: 2/2 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Reproduced at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/authenticator.c:66-76 getAuthData() only calls storage_getAuthData() when localAuthdataUpdate==true and then latches it false; the static authData[] plaintext is otherwise trusted indefinitely. authenticator_clear_cache() callers are: storage.c:1791 (storage_reset), :1836 (wipe/lock path), :1851 (session_clear ONLY inside `if (clear_pin)`), fsm_msg_common.h:192 (checkPassphrase failure only), :625 (fsm_msgCancel). fsm_msgInitialize (fsm_msg_common.h:9) calls session_clear(false): session_clear_impl (storage.c:1911-1914) sets passphraseCached=false and zeroes session.passphrase but never touches authData/localAuthdataUpdate. session_cachePassphrase (storage.c:2595-2598) just strlcpy's the new passphrase and sets cached=true — no cache invalidation. fsm_msgPing authenticator branch (fsm_msg_common.h:~263) does CHECK_PIN + checkPassphrase() → passphrase_protect() (passphrase_sm.c:206-210) re-prompts host for any passphrase B on success. addAuthAccount (authenticator.c:168) then calls getAuthData() which returns true from the stale A-plaintext, mutates a slot, and setAuthData() → storage_setAuthData (storage.c:923-946) re-fingerprints the A-derived table and encrypts it under storage_deriveAuthdataKey(session.passphrase == B), committing over B's authblock. storage_setPassphraseProtected (storage.c:2592) likewise does not clear the cache, so ApplySettings use_passphrase=false with a warm cache writes A's plaintext unencrypted on the next setAuthData. Sequence is entirely host-driven (Initialize, Ping, PassphraseAck) with only the ordinary "Add Auth Account"/"TOTP Secret" confirmations shown; no wallet identity is displayed. Defect exists and is reachable from host input.

Verifier 2: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. Cache lifecycle in lib/firmware/authenticator.c: `localAuthdataUpdate` flips to false after the first successful storage_getAuthData() (lines 66-76) and the only resets are authenticator_clear_cache() callers: session_clear(clear_pin=true) (storage.c:1849-1852), storage_reset/storage_wipe (storage.c:1791/1836), wipeAuthData, fsm_msgCancel (fsm_msg_common.h:625), and the checkPassphrase() failure branch (fsm_msg_common.h:190-198). fsm_msgInitialize (fsm_msg_common.h:9) calls session_clear(false), which in session_clear_impl clears passphraseCached and memsets session.passphrase (storage.c:1913-1914) but does NOT touch authData[]/localAuthdataUpdate. Next Ping auth op (fsm_msg_common.h:265) calls checkPassphrase() -> passphrase_protect(); since passphrase is uncached, passphrase_sm.c:206-210 prompts the host and session_cachePassphrase() (storage.c:2595-2598) stores whatever PassphraseAck carries, with no cache invalidation. getAuthData() then returns true without re-decrypting, so addAuthAccount/removeAuthAccount operate on passphrase-A plaintext and setAuthData() -> storage_setAuthData (storage.c:923-946) re-fingerprints that table and encrypts it under storage_deriveAuthdataKey(session.passphrase = B), overwriting B's authblock and fingerprint. Confirm screens (authenticator.c:200-206) show only domain/account/secret, never which wallet. The ApplySettings variant also holds: fsm_msg_common.h:700-702 calls storage_setPassphraseProtected(false) without clearing the cache, so a later setAuthData writes the cached plaintext with authdata_encrypted=false. All steps are reachable from ordinary host messages (Initialize, PassphraseAck, Ping) on an unlocked device; only the standard button confirms gate the write.

### C3-061 — CONFIRMED — P3: generateOTP: host-supplied 'time remaining' is unbounded; a large value wedges the device in a blocking delay_ms loop for hours to years with the OTP on screen

Unit: `audit:authenticator`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/authenticator.c:336`.

generateOTP() parses tRemainStr with strtol (authenticator.c:271) and applies only a lower bound (`tRemainVal < 4`, line 326). On the success path it computes `unsigned remainingdmSec = tRemainVal * 10` (line 336) and then spins `for (; remainingdmSec > 0; remainingdmSec--) delay_ms(100);` (lines 339-343) with no button check, no cancel, no cap. On the device `long` is 32-bit, so tRemainVal up to 2147483647 is accepted; the multiplication overflows (signed UB) and the unsigned result can be anything up to ~4.29e9 tenths of a second. TOTP semantics bound this field to the 30-second period; nothing enforces that. The permil expression `(1000 * remainingdmSec) / 300` also overflows unsigned for remainingdmSec > 4294967 (clamped downstream in progress_render_ex, so only cosmetic).

Host trace / impact: After the user approves 'Press button to display OTP' for a legitimate-looking request, host malware has already supplied e.g. 'generateOTPFrom:dom:acct:12345:2000000000'. The device draws the OTP and account in large font and then blocks in delay_ms for 2e9*10*100 ms (decades); it does not process USB messages or buttons until power-cycled. The OTP (and the account name) stays on the OLED indefinitely, defeating the 30-second on-screen exposure window the design assumes; the device is unusable for any other purpose until unplugged.

Proposed remediation: Fail closed on out-of-range input: reject tRemainVal < 0 or tRemainVal > 30 (or > a small constant such as 60) with TOKERR before doing any work, and cap remainingdmSec accordingly. Optionally make the countdown loop abort on button press. Use a bounded integer type for the countdown and compute permil from a clamped value.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Reproduced at HEAD 71ba84436. Host-controlled 'generateOTPFrom:' message content is passed straight into generateOTP() (fsm_msg_common.h:278). tRemainStr is parsed via strtol (authenticator.c:271) with only a lower-bound guard (tRemainVal < 4, line 326) — no upper cap, no enforcement of the 30s TOTP period. On the success branch remainingdmSec = tRemainVal * 10 (line 336) drives for(; remainingdmSec>0; remainingdmSec--) delay_ms(100); (lines 339-343). Device delay_ms (timer.c:294) is a plain blocking spin decremented by the timer ISR with no button/cancel check inside the loop, so USB messages and buttons are not serviced until it drains. After the single 'Press button to display OTP' approval, a large host-supplied tRemainVal (long is 32-bit, up to 2147483647) blocks the device for hours to decades with the OTP and account name on the OLED, defeating the intended 30s exposure window; device is unusable until power-cycled. Signed multiply overflow is additional UB but an in-range large value already wedges it. Reachable from host input with only lower-bound validation.

### C3-062 — CONFIRMED — P3: Dead surface / secret residue: generateOTP copies the OTP into an fsm stack buffer that production never uses or scrubs; unreachable token checks retained

Unit: `audit:authenticator`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/authenticator.c:345`.

generateOTP() ends with `strlcpy(otpStr, otp_candidate, 9)` (line 345). The only caller, fsm_msgPing (fsm_msg_common.h:278), passes `char otp[9]` which is consumed only under `#if DEBUG_LINK` (lines 279-285); in the device build (`-DDEBUG_LINK=0`) `otp` is written and then falls off the stack without memzero, while every buffer inside generateOTP is carefully scrubbed at cleanup (lines 349-353). The out-parameter is therefore dead surface in production and the one copy of the code that is not scrubbed. Separately, the `0 == strlen(account)` / `strlen(tIntervalStr)` / `strlen(tRemainStr)` checks at lines 244, 251, 258 are unreachable: strtok() never returns an empty token (it skips leading delimiters and returns NULL when none remain), so those branches can never execute.

Host trace / impact: Not directly exploitable; the 6-digit OTP lingers in main-loop stack memory after the handler returns, contrary to the unit's own scrub-on-every-exit discipline (invariant 4), and the DEBUG-only output channel widens the production function signature for no reason (invariant 5).

Proposed remediation: Drop the otpStr out-parameter from generateOTP() in non-DEBUG_LINK builds (or memzero `otp` in fsm_msgPing before return on all paths), and delete the three unreachable strlen()==0 checks.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/authenticator.c:345 `strlcpy(otpStr, otp_candidate, 9)` writes the 6-digit OTP into the caller's buffer, then cleanup (349-353) memzero's every local except that out-param. Sole caller fsm_msgPing (lib/firmware/fsm_msg_common.h:260 `char otp[9]`, :278 call) uses `otp` only inside `#if DEBUG_LINK` (281-287); CMakeLists.txt:170 sets `-DDEBUG_LINK=0` for the device build (only emulator cache enables it), so in production `otp` is written and never read nor memzero'd anywhere in the handler (checked through the errcode/return path at 314-318 — no scrub). Reachable from host input: Ping message with the "genotp" prefix after PIN/passphrase and the on-device button confirm. Also confirmed the `0 == strlen(...)` checks at 244/251/258 follow strtok(), which never returns an empty token, so they are dead branches. Severity as stated (P3, hygiene/dead surface, not exploitable) is accurate.

### C3-063 — CONFIRMED — P3: cryptoMessageSign leaks an uninitialised stack byte as signature[0] on ed25519 nodes (SignIdentity with host-chosen curve)

Unit: `audit:crypto-rng-bip85`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/crypto.c:165`.

cryptoMessageSign declares `uint8_t pby;` uninitialised and passes it to hdnode_sign_digest(). For a node whose curve has no ECDSA params (ed25519 family), hdnode_sign_digest (deps/crypto/trezor-firmware/crypto/bip32.c:870) falls through to hdnode_sign(), which calls ed25519_sign() and returns 0 without ever touching *pby. The caller then computes `signature[0] = 31 + pby` (default script_type branch) from the garbage byte and the 65-byte signature is returned to the host. Reachable from lib/firmware/fsm_msg_crypto.h:120-145: SignIdentity accepts a host-supplied `ecdsa_curve_name`; fsm_getDerivedNode() (fsm.c:266) accepts any curve get_curve_by_name() knows, including "ed25519"; when proto is neither "ssh" nor "gpg" the generic branch calls cryptoMessageSign(coinByName("Bitcoin"), node, SPENDADDRESS, digest, 64, ...) with the ed25519 node. The stack slot holding pby was last written by the preceding bignum/hdnode derivation and SHA-256 work in the same handler, so the byte disclosed is drawn from freed key-derivation scratch. One byte per request, repeatable with different stack layouts by varying identity/challenge sizes.

Host trace / impact: Host sends SignIdentity{identity.proto="https", ecdsa_curve_name="ed25519", challenge_hidden=..., challenge_visual=...}; user approves the (legitimate-looking) identity confirmation. SignedIdentity.signature.bytes[0] = 31 + <uninitialised stack byte>; bytes 1..64 are a valid ed25519 signature. Repeating with differing challenge lengths lets the host sample stale stack contents left by the private-key derivation path, one byte per approved request.

Proposed remediation: Initialise `uint8_t pby = 0;` and, better, refuse recovery-id formatting for non-ECDSA curves: in cryptoMessageSign return 1 if `!curve->params` (or if node->curve->params == NULL), mirroring cryptoMessageVerify which already requires curve->params. Alternatively restrict SignIdentity's generic (non-ssh/gpg) branch to SECP256K1_NAME.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. cryptoMessageSign (lib/firmware/crypto.c:154-186) declares `uint8_t pby;` uninitialized and passes &pby to hdnode_sign_digest. For an ed25519 node, hdnode_sign_digest (deps/crypto/trezor-firmware/crypto/bip32.c:869-882) sees node->curve->params == NULL and delegates to hdnode_sign, which for ed25519_info calls ed25519_sign(...) and `return 0` WITHOUT ever writing *pby. Back in cryptoMessageSign, result==0 so the default (SPENDADDRESS) branch executes `signature[0] = 31 + pby`, disclosing the uninitialized stack byte. Reachability is fully host-controlled: fsm_msg_crypto.h:120-145 SignIdentity takes msg->ecdsa_curve_name; fsm_getDerivedNode (fsm.c:258) accepts any name get_curve_by_name knows, and get_curve_by_name (bip32.c:1003) returns ed25519_info for ED25519_NAME (\"ed25519\"). When proto is neither ssh nor gpg, the generic branch calls cryptoMessageSign(coinByName(\"Bitcoin\"), node, InputScriptType_SPENDADDRESS, digest, 64, resp->signature.bytes) with the attacker-selected ed25519 node — note hdnode_sign_digest keys off the NODE's curve, not the Bitcoin coin's secp256k1 curve, so params is NULL. The 65-byte signature (byte0 = 31 + garbage, bytes 1..64 a valid ed25519 sig) is returned to the host. One uninitialised stack byte leaked per approved SignIdentity request; user approval of the (legitimate-looking) identity confirmation is required but does not break host-input reachability. Genuine uninitialized-memory disclosure.

### C3-064 — REFUTED — P3: Transient seed-error branch keeps and returns the RNG_DR word generated under the seed fault (sibling of the reset_rng first-word-discard fix)

Unit: `audit:crypto-rng-bip85`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/rand/rng.c:157`.

random32() has three branches on RNG_SR. In the transient branch (SEIS|CEIS set, SECS|CECS clear) the code now latches rng_seed_error_seen and clears SEIS/CEIS, then loops. On the next iteration the status is clean and, if DRDY is set, `new = RNG_DR` is returned. But the word sitting in RNG_DR at that moment is the one the analog noise source produced while the seed-error condition was active -- exactly the sample the STM32F2 reference manual's error-management procedure says must be discarded and the peripheral re-initialised (RNGEN 0->1) before further words are trusted. The persistent-error path was fixed (reset_rng() now discards the first post-RNGEN word via rng_discard_pending), but the transient path neither resets nor sets rng_discard_pending, so a word from a faulted seed is handed to the caller. Only the opt-in random_buffer_checked() sites later notice via rng_seed_error_latched(); every plain random32()/random_buffer() consumer (ECDSA nonces via trezor rand, session ids, etc.) receives the tainted word.

Host trace / impact: Not host-triggerable; requires a hardware seed glitch (voltage/clock perturbation on the RNG analog block). Under fault injection an attacker who can induce a momentary seed error while the device is producing an ECDSA nonce or session key gets a low-entropy word from the faulted LFSR state used directly, while the software mirror records the fault only for the checked draw sites.

Proposed remediation: In the transient branch, after latching and clearing SEIS/CEIS, either call reset_rng() (which re-initialises and arms the discard) or at minimum set `rng_discard_pending = true` and additionally drain RNG_DR if DRDY is already set, so the next returned word is one generated after the fault cleared. Keep rng_seed_error_seen latched as today.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Code at HEAD 71ba84436 lib/rand/rng.c random32() does behave as described: the transient branch (SEIS|CEIS set, SECS|CECS clear) sets rng_seed_error_seen, clears SEIS/CEIS, loops, and the next clean+DRDY iteration returns RNG_DR without reset_rng() or rng_discard_pending, so the word sampled under the seed fault is returned to plain random32()/random_buffer() callers. But the defect is not reachable from host input: RNG_SR SEIS/CEIS are set only by the hardware noise-source/clock monitors, and the claim itself states it requires physical fault injection (voltage/clock perturbation) on the RNG analog block. No host message, USB path, or build flag triggers it, and it is not a build-portability issue. Per the verification criterion (must be host-reachable or a device-compile-flag failure), this is a hardening/fault-injection nit, not a real reachable defect.

### C3-065 — REFUTED — P3: cryptoGetECDHSessionKey is dead exported code and dereferences a NULL curve for non-ECDSA nodes

Unit: `audit:crypto-rng-bip85`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/crypto.c:116`.

cryptoGetECDHSessionKey() has zero callers in lib/, include/ or unittests/ (grep confirms only the declaration in include/keepkey/firmware/crypto.h:45 and the definition). If it were wired to a GetECDHSessionKey handler as in upstream Trezor, it would take `node->curve->params` and pass it straight to ecdsa_read_pubkey(); for ed25519/curve25519 nodes params is NULL and the function faults (upstream guards this with an `if (node->curve->params)` else-branch for curve25519, which this copy lacks). Same dead-surface pattern the prior audit flagged for deser_length.

Host trace / impact: Not reachable today (no caller). Latent: a future handler passing a host-selected curve name (as SignIdentity already does) would hard-fault the device on the first ed25519 request.

Proposed remediation: Delete cryptoGetECDHSessionKey and its declaration in crypto.h (dead-surface invariant). If it is retained for a future handler, add `if (!node->curve->params) return 1;` (or route curve25519 through hdnode_get_shared_key as trezor-crypto does) before ecdsa_read_pubkey.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. At HEAD 71ba84436, cryptoGetECDHSessionKey (lib/firmware/crypto.c:116) does dereference node->curve->params without a NULL guard, but grep across lib/, include/, unittests/, board/, src/ finds only the declaration (include/keepkey/firmware/crypto.h:45) and the definition — zero callers and no GetECDHSessionKey protobuf message or handler. The claim itself concedes "Not reachable today (no caller)". Dead code with a latent hazard is not reachable from host input, so per the verifier criteria this is not a real defect.

### C3-066 — CONFIRMED — P3: SignIdentity and CipherKeyValue leave the derived private key resident in fsm_derived_node after use

Unit: `audit:crypto-rng-bip85`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_crypto.h:124`.

fsm_getDerivedNode() derives into the static `fsm_derived_node` (fsm.c:109) and returns a pointer to it. Sibling handlers (e.g. fsm_msgSignMessage, fsm_msg_coin.h) call `memzero(node, sizeof(*node))` on every exit path, and signing.c calls fsm_clearDerivedNode() when a tx completes. fsm_msgSignIdentity (lines 124-173) and fsm_msgCipherKeyValue (lines 12-61) do neither: after msg_write() the identity/cipher child private key (and, for the no-address_n case, the root node itself) stays in SRAM until the next storage lock or Bitcoin signing. fsm_clearDerivedNode() is only invoked from storage.c (lock/wipe) and signing.c:2220, not from the dispatcher.

Host trace / impact: Requires a separate memory-disclosure or physical-readout primitive; it extends the window during which an SSH/GPG identity key or the CipherKeyValue HMAC key (and for CipherKeyValue with empty address_n, the BIP32 root private key) can be recovered from RAM from 'during the operation' to 'until the next lock or UTXO sign'.

Proposed remediation: Add `memzero(node, sizeof(*node))` (or fsm_clearDerivedNode()) on every exit path of fsm_msgSignIdentity and fsm_msgCipherKeyValue, matching fsm_msgSignMessage; better, have fsm.c's dispatcher call fsm_clearDerivedNode() after every handler returns so no handler can forget.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/firmware/fsm.c:109 declares `static HDNode CONFIDENTIAL fsm_derived_node`; fsm_getDerivedNode (fsm.c:259-290) derives into it and returns `&fsm_derived_node`, and when `address_n_count == 0` (fsm.c:279) it returns the unmodified root node. fsm_msgCipherKeyValue (fsm_msg_crypto.h:1-61) and fsm_msgSignIdentity (fsm_msg_crypto.h:64-173) both obtain this pointer, use `node->private_key`, and exit via msg_write()/fsm_sendFailure()/layoutHome() without any `memzero(node, ...)` — the only memzero calls in that file (lines 47, 54, 56) are for the AES ctx and derived HMAC `data`, not the node. By contrast fsm_msg_coin.h clears the node on every exit path (lines 51, 70, 78, 229, 258, 269, 277, 312, 319, 322). fsm_clearDerivedNode() is invoked only from storage.c:1792/1837/1852 (reset/lock/wipe) and signing.c:2220 (signing_abort), never from the message dispatcher after a handler returns. Both handlers are host-reachable: registered in lib/firmware/messagemap.def:19 (CipherKeyValue) and :26 (SignIdentity) and included unconditionally in fsm.c:364 (not gated by BITCOIN_ONLY). So after a CipherKeyValue with empty address_n, the BIP32 root private key remains in SRAM; after SignIdentity, the identity child key remains, until the next lock/wipe or UTXO signing completion. Severity as stated (P3, needs a separate memory-disclosure primitive) is accurate.

### C3-067 — CONFIRMED — P3: TOKEN_AMOUNT confirm body is 2 bytes too small: worst-case symbol is silently truncated by snprintf

Unit: `audit:clearsign-metadata`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/signed_metadata.c:1318`.

signed_metadata_confirm_screens() sizes `body` as METADATA_MAX_ARG_NAME_LEN + 2 + METADATA_MAX_ARG_VALUE_LEN*2 + 1 = 123 bytes, i.e. for the hex rendering only (the prior audit's fix for the RAW/BYTES ellipsis). The ARG_FORMAT_TOKEN_AMOUNT branch renders name(<=32) + ":\n" + bn_format(amount, suffix=" "+symbol(<=10), decimals<=36).
