For a 78-digit uint256 with decimals=36 (both host/delegate-permitted by arg_value_ok / parse_v2_args) bn_format emits 42 integer digits + '.' + 36 fraction digits = 79 chars, plus the 11-char suffix = 90 chars, which fits `formatted[96]` and returns non-zero, so the 'AMOUNT TOO LARGE' guard does not fire. snprintf(body, 123, "%s:\n%s", name, formatted) then needs 32+2+90+1 = 125 bytes and truncates the last two characters of the token symbol; the return value is ignored. The AMOUNT branch (" wei", 82 chars) and hex branch (88 chars) fit exactly, so this is the one sibling the buffer resize missed. Trace: host EthereumTxMetadata (v2/v3 envelope, arg name 32 chars, TOKEN_AMOUNT decimals 36 symbol 10 chars) -> parse OK -> matches_tx decodes a 32-byte amount word from calldata -> confirm screen shows amount with a symbol missing its last two characters. Violates invariant (2) fail-closed/never-truncate and (1) a signed field (the attested symbol) is not shown in full.

Host trace / impact: Requires a delegate/runtime-signer-attested schema whose arg name is 32 chars and whose symbol is 10 chars (e.g. two tokens on one router whose symbols share an 8-char prefix, 'USDCBRIDGE' vs 'USDCBRIDGX'); a host then supplies a calldata amount word large enough that bn_format's rendering reaches 79 chars, and the screen shows 'USDCBRID' for either token. Practical impact is low because the signer must have attested the schema and the amount itself is fully shown, but it is the same silent-truncation primitive the prior audit closed for hex values, and in the KeepKey suppression tier there is no raw review behind it.

Proposed remediation: Size `body` for the widest renderer, not just the hex one (e.g. METADATA_MAX_ARG_NAME_LEN + 2 + sizeof(formatted) + 1), and treat snprintf's return value >= sizeof(body) as a hard failure (return false) in every arg branch so truncation can never be silent. Add a unit test rendering a 32-char name + 10-char symbol + decimals 36 + 0xFF..FE amount.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. body is sized METADATA_MAX_ARG_NAME_LEN(32)+2+METADATA_MAX_ARG_VALUE_LEN(44)*2+1 = 123 (signed_metadata.c:1106). TOKEN_AMOUNT parse allows decimals<=36 and symlen<=10 (lines 179, 275); read_arg_name allows 32-char names; matches_tx copies a host-controlled 32-byte calldata word as the amount (lines 391-403). trezor bn_format (bignum.c:1673) on 2^256-1 with decimals=36 and non-zero trailing digit yields 42+1+36 = 79 chars plus 11-char " SYMBOL" suffix = 90, fitting formatted[96] and returning non-zero, so the TOO LARGE guard does not trigger. snprintf(body,123,"%s:\n%s",name,formatted) at line 1318 needs 32+2+90+1 = 125 bytes, return ignored, so the last two symbol characters are silently truncated. Reachable from host input given a signer-attested schema with a 32-char arg name and 10-char symbol. Low practical impact but a real silent-truncation of a signed field, matching the claim.

### C3-068 — REFUTED — P3: Dead surface: signed_metadata_signer_is_runtime() has no caller; METADATA_TIER_NONE arm in signed_metadata_process() is unreachable

Unit: `audit:clearsign-metadata`. Location at audited SHA: `include/keepkey/firmware/signed_metadata.h:228`.

signed_metadata_signer_is_runtime() (declared signed_metadata.h:228, defined signed_metadata.c:822) is not referenced anywhere in lib/, include/ or unittests/ — the header comment claims non-EVM callers use it, but fsm_msg_solana.h uses signed_metadata_signer_alias()/_fingerprint() instead. Separately, in signed_metadata_process() (signed_metadata.c:911) `metadata_tier = is_loaded ? METADATA_TIER_RUNTIME : METADATA_TIER_NONE` — metadata_pubkey_for() only ever returns a non-NULL pointer with is_loaded=true, so the NONE arm can never execute while metadata_available is true. Neither is exploitable; both are dead code in the trust-tier surface that invariant (5) asks to be removed so a future edit cannot silently make it live (e.g. a built-in key path resurrecting TIER_NONE, which the confirm flow would present under the 'Insight Verified' branding).

Host trace / impact: None directly. Risk is latent: a later change that makes metadata_pubkey_for() return a key with is_loaded=false would land in TIER_NONE and take the 'Insight Verified' else-branch in signed_metadata_confirm_screens() with no identity screen, without any reviewed decision.

Proposed remediation: Delete signed_metadata_signer_is_runtime() and its declaration; replace the ternary with `metadata_tier = METADATA_TIER_RUNTIME;` (or fail closed if !is_loaded) so the tier assignment is explicit.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Facts verified at 71ba84436: signed_metadata_signer_is_runtime() (signed_metadata.h:231, signed_metadata.c:822) has zero callers in lib/, include/, unittests/, firmware/; metadata_pubkey_for() (signed_metadata.c:810-820) returns non-NULL only after setting *is_loaded=true, so `metadata_tier = is_loaded ? METADATA_TIER_RUNTIME : METADATA_TIER_NONE` at line 911 can never assign TIER_NONE with metadata_available=true. However, there is no defect at HEAD: no host input reaches a wrong tier, no wrong screen is shown, no build-flag failure. The claim is a hygiene/latent-risk note (unused function + dead ternary arm), explicitly 'Exploit: None directly'. Per the verification standard (reachable defect at HEAD), this is NOT real; it is a valid cleanup suggestion only.

### C3-069 — UNVERIFIED — P3: Custom address layouts (cosmos/ethereum/osmosis/nano) clip the signed address at two rows with no indicator and no fit probe

Unit: `audit:confirm-display`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/app_layout.c:468`.

layout_cosmos_address_notification (line 468) and layout_ethereum_address_notification (line 552) draw the body from y=27 with line height 14 and wrap width 140 px. Rows land at y=27 and y=41; a third row at y=55 is rejected by draw_char_impl (55+10 > 64), so draw_string_walk stops and drops the tail silently. confirm_helper() (confirm_sm.c:608-610) runs the render-completeness probe ONLY for layout_standard_notification -- 'Custom layouts that place their own body still opt out' -- so nothing warns or pages. Replaying draw_string_walk with the real font tables (body font: 6 px avg, w/m/W/M 8 px): a 52-char cosmosvaloper1... address is clipped for 99.7% of random addresses (up to 8 chars dropped: 2 data chars + the whole 6-char checksum); a 53-char Hive public key (STM + 50 base58) is clipped 100% (up to 11 chars, which reaches into key data); a 48-char TON user-friendly address is clipped 56% (up to 5 chars); 44-char Solana and 45-char cosmos1 addresses clip 0.2-0.3%. Trace: host CosmosMsgDelegate.validator_address (nanopb max_size 53) -> fsm_msg_cosmos.h:180 confirm_cosmos_address() -> confirm_with_custom_layout() -> confirm_helper() (probe skipped) -> layout_cosmos_address_notification -> tail dropped -> user holds -> the FULL validator_address is serialized into the amino sign bytes. Same for Undelegate/Redelegate src+dst/Rewards (fsm_msg_cosmos.h:230,291,299,360), Hive GetPublicKey (fsm_msg_hive.h:71), TON GetAddress (fsm_msg_ton.h:79). This is the sibling the prior audit's 'probe must start where the real draw starts' fix missed: it added probes for the standard and constant-power layouts and left every custom layout unmeasured.

Host trace / impact: A malicious host sends CosmosMsgDelegate with validator_address = 'cosmosvaloper1' + 38 bech32 chars. The device shows the first ~44 characters across two rows and silently omits the last 3-8 (the checksum and, for wide-glyph addresses, trailing data chars); the user cannot verify the checksum or the tail, holds to approve, and the complete address is committed to the signature. Practical substitution requires a ~44-char prefix collision, so the direct impact is a broken 'every signed byte is shown' guarantee rather than an easy redirect; for Hive the dropped span includes actual key bytes.

Proposed remediation: Give custom layouts the same completeness contract as the standard one: add a per-layout fit probe (draw_string_fits at the layout's own origin/width/line-height), select it in fits_probe_for()/confirm_helper(), and page or refuse when it fails. Cheapest immediate fix for these four layouts: draw the address with line height font_height (10) instead of 14 so rows sit at 27/37/47 (three rows, ~69 chars at 140 px), and still probe. Alternatively route validator addresses and Hive/TON keys through confirm_bytes(), which already pages exactly.

Verification: 0/1 required verdicts recovered.

Continuation: re-verify before deciding whether to fix.

### C3-070 — UNVERIFIED — P3: layoutU2FDialog measures with the no-icon geometry but draws with whatever icon geometry the last confirm left behind

Unit: `audit:confirm-display`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/app_layout.c:912`.

layoutU2FDialog() computes fits = confirm_body_fits(strbuf, BODY_WIDTH), which probes from x=LEFT_MARGIN(4) with wrap width 225. It then calls layout_standard_notification(), which reads the file-static iconLayout flag (layout.c:277) and, when set, draws from x=40 with width 185 (BODY_WIDTH_WITH_ICON). iconLayout is set only by layout_has_icon() inside confirm_screen() (confirm_sm.c:206) and is never reset by layoutHome() or anything else, so after any confirm_with_icon() screen (signed_metadata.c:791/1190/1199/1208/1222/1348, eip712.c:634) it stays true until the next confirm_screen. The U2F/CTAP path never goes through confirm_screen, so the stale flag persists: the probe says a 64-char rp_id body fits, the actual draw at x=40/w185 drops the tail ('Sign in to aaaa...aaa.com?' loses 'com?' in the replayed walk). fits==true is returned, request_user_presence() (u2f.c:715-740) proceeds, and the credential is bound to an rp_id the user did not fully read. No icon is drawn either (layout_add_icon runs only in confirm_screen), so the body is simply shifted right and clipped. This is a regression-shaped sibling of the prior audit's probe-origin fix: the probe was added to layoutU2FDialog but at a fixed origin, while the draw origin is stateful.

Host trace / impact: 1) Host drives any clear-signed EVM flow that shows an identity icon (confirm_with_icon with RUNTIME_ICON/VERIFIED_ICON) and the user approves or cancels; iconLayout is now true. 2) The same host (or a compromised client on the same machine) sends a CTAP2 getAssertion/makeCredential with an rp_id of ~60-90 chars. The device renders 'Sign in to <rp_id minus its last several chars>?', reports fits, and waits for a press; the user approves an rp_id whose tail they never saw, and the assertion/credential is bound to the full string.

Proposed remediation: Make the draw geometry explicit rather than ambient: call layout_has_icon(false) at the top of layoutU2FDialog() before both the probe and the draw (or have confirm_screen() reset iconLayout on exit, and layout_home() clear it). Better, add a unit test that runs a confirm_with_icon() followed by layoutU2FDialog() with a body that fits at 225 but not 185 and asserts fits==false / the body is rendered from x=4.

Verification: 0/1 required verdicts recovered.

Continuation: re-verify before deciding whether to fix.

### C3-071 — UNVERIFIED — P3: Seed-backup rows copied into a stack buffer in confirm_constant_power_subpage_take() are never scrubbed; page_buf also escapes memzero on the pages<=1 early return

Unit: `audit:confirm-display`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/board/confirm_sm.c:689`.

confirm_constant_power_subpage_take() declares char probe[BODY_CHAR_MAX] on the stack and memcpy()s successive prefixes of the body into it to run the fit probe. Its only caller in production is confirm_constant_power_paged(), which reset.c:485 drives with formatted_mnemonic[current_page] -- the user's seed words -- so probe holds up to 351 bytes of mnemonic on every call and the function returns (both the normal path at line 704 and the 'take >= sizeof(probe)' break at 690) without memzero(). The static buffers around it (strbuf, page_buf, sub) are all CONFIDENTIAL and memzero'd, so this is the one copy of the seed on the display path that is not scrubbed; it stays in the stack frame until overwritten. Sibling in page_body_confirm(): the 'pages <= 1' branch at lines 487-490 returns confirm_screen() directly and bypasses the done: label, so page_buf (which pass-1 filled with a prefix of the body) and page_title are not zeroed on that path; the >99-page refusal at 482 and the normal path at 528 do zero them.

Host trace / impact: Not remotely triggerable on its own; it weakens the seed-hygiene invariant (secrets scrubbed on every exit path). Any later stack disclosure primitive (e.g. an uninitialised-stack read in another handler, or a debug/JTAG stack dump) recovers seed-word rows from the dead frame after a backup/BIP-85 display.

Proposed remediation: memzero(probe, sizeof(probe)) before every return in confirm_constant_power_subpage_take() (or hoist the buffer to a static CONFIDENTIAL and zero it at exit). In page_body_confirm(), route the pages<=1 case through the done: cleanup (ok = confirm_screen(...); goto done;) so page_buf/page_title are always zeroed.

Verification: 0/1 required verdicts recovered.

Continuation: re-verify before deciding whether to fix.

### C3-072 — UNVERIFIED — P2: confirm_omni hides the signed property id for unknown tokens and formats every property as 8-decimal divisible, misstating indivisible Omni amounts by 10^8

Unit: `audit:confirm-display`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/app_confirm.c:537`.

For an Omni SimpleSend (tx_type 0) the screen is built from the 32-bit currency id (bytes 8..11) and the 64-bit amount (bytes 12..19) of the OP_RETURN payload. The currency id is mapped to one of four suffixes and every other id collapses to 'UNKN' -- the id itself is never displayed, so two sends of different unknown properties are indistinguishable on the device although the id is signed byte-for-byte in the OP_RETURN. Independently, bn_format_uint64(amount, NULL, suffix, BITCOIN_DIVISIBILITY=8, ...) is applied unconditionally, but Omni properties are either divisible (amount in 1e-8 units) or indivisible (amount is the whole-unit count); property 3 (MAID) in the switch is an indivisible property, so an amount field of 100000000 is shown as '1.00000000 MAID' while 100,000,000 MAID are actually transferred. (The 'UNKN' suffix also lacks the leading space the others carry, rendering as '1.5UNKN'.) The bn_format_uint64 return value is unchecked here, the same pattern the prior audit's P1 fixed in fsm_msg_thorchain.h; with str_out[32] and max 21-char number + 6-char suffix + NUL it cannot overflow today, but nothing pins that.

Host trace / impact: Host builds a Bitcoin SignTx whose OP_RETURN output is 'omni' || 0x00000000 (SimpleSend) || 0x00000003 (MAID, indivisible) || 0x0000000005F5E100 (100000000). transaction.c:349 calls confirm_omni; the user sees 'Do you want to send 1.00000000 MAID?' and holds; the signed OP_RETURN transfers 100,000,000 MAID. For any property id outside {1,2,3,31} the user sees only '<amount>UNKN' and cannot tell which asset is being moved.

Proposed remediation: Display the property id numerically for unmapped ids ('property #N') and include it alongside known tickers; carry a per-property divisibility flag in the switch (divisible: 1,2,31; indivisible: 3) and format indivisible amounts with 0 decimals -- or, since Omni is legacy, drop the SimpleSend clear-sign path and always fall through to confirm_bytes() so the exact bytes are approved. Check the bn_format_uint64 return and refuse on false, as the thorchain send path does.

Verification: 0/2 required verdicts recovered.

Continuation: re-verify before deciding whether to fix.

### C3-073 — UNVERIFIED — P3: Dead confirmation/layout surface: confirm_constant_power() and five app-level helpers have no callers, plus a declared-but-undefined layout_tx_info()

Unit: `audit:confirm-display`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/board/confirm_sm.c:798`.

confirm_constant_power() (confirm_sm.c:798-819, the varargs wrapper) has zero callers anywhere in lib/, tools/ or unittests/ -- the seed/BIP-85 flows use confirm_constant_power_paged() -- yet it is the one path that would render a constant-power body through confirm_helper() with the completeness probe deliberately disabled (confirm_helper comment at 592-607), i.e. the exact 'silent clip' the paged variant was written to remove. In app_confirm.c, confirm_encrypt_msg (87), confirm_decrypt_msg (111), confirm_erc_token_transfer (173) and confirm_transaction_output_no_bold (191) have no callers, and layout_notification_no_title_no_bold (app_layout.c:389) exists only for those. layout_tx_info is declared in app_layout.h:102 but has no definition. layout_remove_animation (layout.c:93) is fenced behind the never-defined AGGRO_UNDEFINED_FN, and NO_WIDTH (layout.h:84, with a stray semicolon) is unused. Each of these is reachable-by-future-edit consent surface that the audited probes do not cover.

Host trace / impact: No direct exploit; a future caller of confirm_constant_power() would get a constant-power screen with no completeness check, and the dead wrappers keep unreviewed display code alive.

Proposed remediation: Delete confirm_constant_power() (or make it call confirm_constant_power_paged internally), delete the four unused app_confirm helpers and layout_notification_no_title_no_bold, remove the layout_tx_info declaration, layout_remove_animation and NO_WIDTH.

Verification: 0/1 required verdicts recovered.

Continuation: re-verify before deciding whether to fix.

### C3-074 — UNVERIFIED — P2: Main-channel messages are dispatched and repaint the screen while a U2F/CTAP2 request is waiting for user presence, decoupling the passkey consent from what is displayed

Unit: `audit:board-io`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/board/usb.c:325`.

u2fhid_read() marks a U2F transaction in progress with usbTiny(1) (lib/firmware/u2f.c:229) and then spins on usbPoll() while waiting for the rest of the command (u2f.c:246), for the next command/button (u2f.c:301) and, inside request_user_presence(), for the physical button press that approves 'Sign in to <rp>?' / 'Create a passkey for <rp>?' (u2f.c:762). The `tiny` flag is only threaded to the U2F endpoint callback: main_rx_callback() (usb.c:318-328) hands every 64-byte packet to user_rx_callback unconditionally, and handle_usb_rx() (messages.c:437) only consults msg_tiny_flag, which nobody sets on the U2F path. So during the presence wait, every main-channel protobuf goes through usb_rx_helper() -> dispatch() -> the full FSM handler, nested inside the U2F loop. Almost every FSM handler ends with layoutHome() (e.g. fsm_msgPing with no protections: msg_write(Success); layoutHome();), which overwrites the RP dialog with the home screen; handlers with button_protection put their own host-worded confirm on the screen. The U2F loop resumes afterwards still armed: `saw_button_up && keepkey_button_down()` returns true on the next press, and the signature is produced. Invariant 1 (display-vs-signed) is broken at the transport layer: the RP whose credential is signed is no longer what is on the screen when the user presses. This is a sibling of the u2f-ctap findings the prior audit fixed (up=false, reset wording), missed because the gap is in board I/O, not in ctap2.c.

Host trace / impact: Host malware waits for the user to start a legitimate passkey login, forwards a getAssertion/authenticate for the attacker's RP (evil.com) over the HID interface, and, one poll later, sends a main-channel Ping (or any cheap message) over the WebUSB interface. The nested fsm_msgPing runs and calls layoutHome(); the 'Sign in to evil.com?' dialog is visible for a few milliseconds at most, replaced by the home screen (or, with button_protection=true, by a 'Ping / <attacker text>' confirm whose body the host writes). The user, expecting the passkey prompt they just triggered, presses the button; request_user_presence() (u2f.c:755-764) sees saw_button_up && button_down and returns true, and the device emits a valid assertion for evil.com. ctap2 getAssertion/makeCredential and legacy U2F AUTH/REG all share this loop.

Proposed remediation: Gate the main channel on the U2F 'tiny' state the way the transport already gates U2F on the main channel: in handle_usb_rx()/main_rx_callback(), when usbTiny() is set (a U2F transaction is in progress) either route the packet through msg_read_tiny() (Trezor's behaviour, which only accepts Cancel/Initialize/acks) or answer with a Failure and drop it, never dispatch() a full handler. Additionally make request_user_presence() fail closed on display change: track a layout generation counter bumped by every layout/layoutHome call and, on each loop iteration, if the counter moved since the dialog was drawn, redraw the dialog and require a fresh button-up before accepting a press (or abort with CTAP2_ERR_USER_ACTION_TIMEOUT).

Verification: 0/2 required verdicts recovered.

Continuation: re-verify before deciding whether to fix.

### C3-075 — CONFIRMED — P3: Decoded inbound messages (mnemonic, xprv node, passphrase) are left in static RAM after the handler returns; only the raw frame arena is scrubbed

Unit: `audit:board-io`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/board/messages.c:184`.

dispatch() decodes every inbound protobuf into the static, non-CONFIDENTIAL `decode_buffer[MAX_DECODE_SIZE]` (messages.c:184) and calls entry->process_func on it; after the handler returns nothing clears it -- the buffer is only memset at the START of the next dispatch (messages.c:185). The raw frame is handled correctly: usb_rx_helper() reaches `reset: frame_arena_rx_reset()` (messages.c:352) on every exit path and zeroes frame_arena.rx. The decoded copy is not. So after LoadDevice (mnemonic[241] and/or HDNode private key), RecoveryDevice/CharacterAck, CipherKeyValue, or any signing message carrying key material, the plaintext sits in .bss until the host chooses to send another message. The tiny channel has the same shape: tiny_msg_poll_and_buffer() (messages.c:466-484) memcpy's msg_tiny (holding a PassphraseAck.passphrase) into the caller's buffer and leaves msg_tiny populated; passphrase_sm.c:59 then keeps a second, unscrubbed copy on the stack. Invariant 4 (scrub on every exit path) is met for the frame arena and for fsm_derived_node but not for the decode buffer that sits between them.

Host trace / impact: Any memory-disclosure primitive that reads .bss -- the over-read class the prior audit found in msg_write (now clamped), a future sibling in another response encoder, a debug-link state dump, or a fault-injection/JTAG read on a device whose firmware relies on CONFIDENTIAL placement -- recovers the last LoadDevice mnemonic / xprv or the last passphrase verbatim, because the host controls when (if ever) the next message overwrites it. A host that loads a seed and then sends nothing leaves the mnemonic resident for the whole session.

Proposed remediation: In dispatch(), memzero(decode_buffer, sizeof(decode_buffer)) after process_func returns and on the pb_parse failure path; mark decode_buffer CONFIDENTIAL so it lives in the .confidential section with the other key-material buffers. In tiny_msg_poll_and_buffer(), memzero(msg_tiny) after the memcpy to the caller; in passphrase_sm.c wait_for_passphrase_ack(), memzero(msg_tiny_buf) before returning.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/board/messages.c:184-198: dispatch() decodes every inbound protobuf into `static uint8_t decode_buffer[MAX_DECODE_SIZE]` (plain .bss, no CONFIDENTIAL attribute), memsets it only at entry (line 185), calls entry->process_func(decode_buffer), and returns with no scrub. usb_rx_helper() (line 350-353) zeroes only frame_arena.rx via frame_arena_rx_reset(). fsm_msgLoadDevice (lib/firmware/fsm_msg_common.h:565-591) passes msg to storage_loadDevice and returns without memzero(msg); the only handlers that scrub their msg are a few chain-specific ones (zcash.c:573, thorchain.c:250 etc.), and those scrub local copies, not decode_buffer. So after a host-sent LoadDevice (mnemonic/HDNode xprv), CharacterAck, CipherKeyValue, etc., the plaintext remains resident in .bss until the host chooses to send another dispatch()ed message. Tiny channel also confirmed: tiny_msg_poll_and_buffer() (messages.c:466-484) memcpy's msg_tiny to the caller buffer and does not clear msg_tiny (it is CONFIDENTIAL so it is only wiped at boot), and passphrase_sm.c:59-68 keeps the PassphraseAck in an automatic `uint8_t msg_tiny_buf[MSG_TINY_BFR_SZ]` with no memzero on exit (grep shows memzero only for passphrase_info at 204/216). Reachable purely from host input; residue persists for the session. Severity is a hygiene/defense-in-depth finding (needs a separate disclosure primitive), consistent with the P3 rating, but the defect as described exists.

### C3-076 — REFUTED — P3: USART_DEBUG_ON console path is unreachable in every build configuration and, if enabled, opens USART3 to unprivileged code; dead surface should be deleted

Unit: `audit:board-io`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/board/keepkey_usart.c:40`.

The entire serial console (put_console_char, get_console_input, display_debug_string, usart_init body, dbg_print body, read_console) is under `#ifdef USART_DEBUG_ON`; the macro is not defined by any CMake cache, CMakeLists.txt, or source file in the tree (only referenced at keepkey_usart.c:40/243 and memory.c:118). Every device build compiles the stub `dbg_print(){}` / `usart_init(){}` at lines 244-247. The dead body also contains code that cannot work as written: dbg_print's `if (strlen(str) + 1 <= LARGE_DEBUG_BUF)` (line 175) is always true after vsnprintf truncation so the 'exceeds buffer size' branch never fires, and read_console() (lines 226-242) is a `while (1)` with an unreachable return. memory.c:118-123 additionally remaps MPU region 5 from protecting SYSCFG (read-only, unpriv-none) to exposing USART3 read-write to unprivileged code whenever the flag is set, so accidentally defining it for a release image silently drops an MPU protection. Invariant 5: unreachable code should be deleted rather than carried.

Host trace / impact: No runtime exploit in shipped images (the code is not compiled in). The risk is process-level: a developer enabling -DUSART_DEBUG_ON to debug a field issue ships an image with the SYSCFG MPU region replaced by an unprivileged USART3 window and a debug console that transmits whatever dbg_print callers format, with no build-time signal that a protection was removed.

Proposed remediation: Delete the USART_DEBUG_ON blocks in keepkey_usart.c and the matching MPU branch in memory.c, leaving only the empty dbg_print/usart_init stubs (or remove dbg_print callers entirely). If a serial console is ever wanted again, add it as a CMake option that is force-disabled when signing/release flags are set, and keep the SYSCFG MPU region unconditional.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436: USART_DEBUG_ON is referenced only at lib/board/keepkey_usart.c:40/243 and lib/board/memory.c:118 and is defined by no CMake file or source, so every device build compiles the empty stubs `dbg_print(){}` / `usart_init(){}` (keepkey_usart.c:244-247) and the release MPU region-5 SYSCFG protection (memory.c:125-129). The guarded console body and the unprivileged USART3 MPU remap are never compiled into any shipped image, no host-supplied input can enable a compile-time macro, and the device compile flags succeed as-is. This is a dead-code/process hygiene finding (the audit itself states 'No runtime exploit in shipped images'), not a host-reachable defect or a build-portability failure, so it does not meet the bar for real=true.

### C3-077 — CONFIRMED — P3: decode_buffer retains LoadDevice mnemonic / xprv node after the handler returns (RX arena is scrubbed, decoded copy is not)

Unit: `audit:json-fsm-core`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/board/messages.c:198`.

dispatch() pb_decodes every inbound message into the file-static `decode_buffer[MAX_DECODE_SIZE]` (not marked CONFIDENTIAL) and calls the handler, then returns. usb_rx_helper's `reset:` label immediately memsets frame_arena.rx (the wire copy), but decode_buffer is only zeroed at the START of the next dispatch (line 185). For LoadDevice the decoded struct holds `mnemonic[241]` and `node.private_key[32]` in plaintext (storage_loadDevice copies them into shadow_config but never memzeroes the message). So after LoadDevice completes, the seed material sits in SRAM until the host sends the next full-frame message; on a device that is then left idle, or if the host only sends tiny messages, it persists indefinitely. This is a sibling of the secret-hygiene fixes already applied to the frame arena and to fsm_derived_node (fsm_clearDerivedNode): the wire buffer was scrubbed, the decoded buffer was missed.

Host trace / impact: Load a wallet via LoadDevice (mnemonic or node). Any subsequent SRAM read primitive (cold-boot / debug probe / a future OOB read of the kind the prior audit found in msg_write) recovers the full mnemonic from decode_buffer even though storage has since encrypted it and frame_arena.rx was wiped. No user interaction beyond the LoadDevice confirmation.

Proposed remediation: In dispatch(), after `entry->process_func(decode_buffer);` add `memzero(decode_buffer, sizeof(decode_buffer));` (and on the pb_parse failure path). Optionally mark decode_buffer CONFIDENTIAL.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Confirmed at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/board/messages.c dispatch() (lines 184-198) pb_decodes into the file-static, non-CONFIDENTIAL `decode_buffer[MAX_DECODE_SIZE]`, memsets it only at entry (line 185), calls entry->process_func, and returns without scrubbing. The only post-handler wipes are `memset(frame_arena.rx, ...)` at lines 262/290 (the wire copy), never decode_buffer. LoadDevice is a production MSG_IN entry (lib/firmware/messagemap.def:13 -> fsm_msgLoadDevice), so it takes this path. fsm_msgLoadDevice (lib/firmware/fsm_msg_common.h:565-591) and storage_loadDevice (lib/firmware/storage.c:2147+) copy msg->mnemonic (strlcpy) / msg->node (memcpy) into shadow_config and never memzero the LoadDevice struct. Therefore after LoadDevice returns, the plaintext mnemonic / xprv private_key remains in decode_buffer SRAM until the next pb-dispatched message overwrites it via the entry memset. Reachable from ordinary host input (LoadDevice on an uninitialized device, single on-device confirm). Impact is limited to SRAM-residue recovery (debug probe / cold boot / future OOB read), matching the claim's P3 severity.

### C3-078 — CONFIRMED — P3: msg_tiny keeps the last PassphraseAck passphrase in RAM after it is handed to the caller

Unit: `audit:json-fsm-core`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/board/messages.c:481`.

msg_read_tiny() decodes PassphraseAck into the file-static `msg_tiny[64]` (CONFIDENTIAL). tiny_msg_poll_and_buffer() memcpy's it into the caller's stack buffer (line 481) but never clears msg_tiny; the passphrase (up to 50 bytes) remains there until the next tiny message (ButtonAck/PinMatrixAck/Cancel) overwrites that region -- and since later tiny messages are usually shorter (ButtonAck is empty), the tail of the passphrase survives even that. passphrase_sm.c wait_for_passphrase_ack() likewise leaves its stack copy `msg_tiny_buf` unscrubbed after strlcpy into passphrase_info. The passphrase is seed-derivation material (BIP39 salt); every other holder of it (session cache) is memzero'd by session_clear, this copy is not.

Host trace / impact: Enter a passphrase once; the plaintext stays resident in msg_tiny across the whole session and across session_clear/ClearSession/auto-lock. Any SRAM disclosure recovers it, defeating the hidden-wallet property that ClearSession is supposed to provide.

Proposed remediation: In tiny_msg_poll_and_buffer(), memzero(msg_tiny, sizeof(msg_tiny)) after the memcpy (and on the error path). In passphrase_sm.c wait_for_passphrase_ack(), memzero(msg_tiny_buf) before returning.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. Reproduced at HEAD 71ba84436. lib/board/messages.c: `static CONFIDENTIAL uint8_t msg_tiny[MSG_TINY_BFR_SZ]` (line 357) is written by pb_decode in msg_read_tiny (line 424) and copied out in tiny_msg_poll_and_buffer (line 481 memcpy) with no memzero anywhere in the file (grep memzero -> zero hits). lib/firmware/passphrase_sm.c wait_for_passphrase_ack (lines 57-84): stack `msg_tiny_buf` receives the PassphraseAck, strlcpy's into passphrase_info, and returns without scrubbing. passphrase_protect() (lines 203-217) was hardened to memzero its own static CONFIDENTIAL passphrase_info on entry/exit, but that fix does not cover msg_tiny or msg_tiny_buf. session_clear / fsm_msgClearSession (fsm.c:354) never touch msg_tiny, and CONFIDENTIAL is only cleared at boot by the bootloader, so a host-supplied PassphraseAck leaves the plaintext passphrase resident in SRAM for the session until a later (usually shorter, e.g. empty ButtonAck) tiny message partially overwrites it. Reachable from host input via the normal PassphraseRequest/PassphraseAck exchange.

### C3-079 — UNVERIFIED — P3: Features.wipe_code_protection is never populated: has_ flag set to storage_hasWipeCode() but the value stays false

Unit: `audit:json-fsm-core`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_common.h:62`.

`resp->has_wipe_code_protection = storage_hasWipeCode();` sets only the presence flag; `resp->wipe_code_protection` itself is left at the RESP_INIT memset value (false). When a wipe code IS set the host receives has=true, value=false, i.e. an explicit assertion that no wipe code exists; when none is set the field is absent. Compare pin_protection/passphrase_protection two lines above which set both. Currently latent because ENABLE_WIPECODE is off (fsm_msgChangeWipeCode returns 'not available'), but the storage path (pin_sm.c:346 storage_setWipeCode) and the Features field are compiled in, so the moment ENABLE_WIPECODE is turned on the device lies to every host about wipe-code protection.

Host trace / impact: With ENABLE_WIPECODE defined: set a wipe code, then GetFeatures reports wipe_code_protection=false. A host/user relying on Features to verify the wipe code was applied (or to warn before entering a PIN that could be the wipe code) is misled.

Proposed remediation: `resp->has_wipe_code_protection = true; resp->wipe_code_protection = storage_hasWipeCode();`

Verification: 0/1 required verdicts recovered.

Continuation: re-verify before deciding whether to fix.

### C3-080 — REFUTED — P3: Dead RAW dispatch surface: raw_dispatch(), RAW_IN, parse_pb_varint(), RawMessage/RawMessageState, RAW_TX_ACK_VARINT_COUNT have no registered user

Unit: `audit:json-fsm-core`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/board/messages.c:213`.

messagemap.def contains no RAW_IN entry, so `rxEntry->dispatch == RAW` is unreachable and raw_dispatch() (messages.c:213), the `raw_msg_handler_t` function-pointer cast, parse_pb_varint() (messages.c:612, which calls pb_decode_varint without checking its return), RawMessage/RawMessageState (messages.h:88-100), the RAW_IN macro and the fsm.c RAW_IN static-assert shim, and RAW_TX_ACK_VARINT_COUNT (fsm.h:41) are all dead. The RAW path is also the only inbound path that hands unbounded continuation frames (frameSize = length-1, never clamped to rxMsgSize) to a handler, so leaving it compiled keeps a latent unbounded-length entry point alive for any future RAW_IN registration. Same category: fsm.c defines CHECK_NO_CEREMONY twice verbatim (lines 174 and 187), and fsm.h declares fsm_msgWordAck/fsm_msgFlashWrite/fsm_msgFlashHash/fsm_msgSoftReset which have no definition anywhere in lib/.

Host trace / impact: None today (unreachable). Risk is a future RAW_IN registration inheriting the unclamped continuation-frame length and the unchecked varint parser.

Proposed remediation: Delete raw_dispatch, the RAW branch in usb_rx_helper, RAW_IN (messages.h + fsm.c shim), parse_pb_varint, RawMessage/RawMessageState/raw_msg_handler_t, RAW_TX_ACK_VARINT_COUNT, the duplicate CHECK_NO_CEREMONY block, and the four orphan prototypes in fsm.h.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Not real. The claim itself concedes "Exploit: None today (unreachable)" — a dead-code/hygiene finding, which by the verification standard (must be reachable from host input at HEAD) does not qualify. Worse, the premise "no registered user" is false at HEAD 71ba84436: /Users/highlander/WebstormProjects/keepkey-stack/projects/keepkey-firmware-alphafix/tools/bootloader/usb_flash.c:68 registers `RAW_IN(MessageType_MessageType_FirmwareUpload, FirmwareUpload, ...)`, so raw_dispatch()/parse_pb_varint()/RawMessage in lib/board/messages.c are live shared-board code consumed by the bootloader, not dead. In the application firmware map (lib/firmware/fsm.c:228 redefines RAW_IN to a no-op/static-assert shim) there is indeed no RAW entry, so `rxEntry->dispatch == RAW` (messages.c:319) is unreachable from host input in the firmware image — meaning no defect is reachable. The duplicate CHECK_NO_CEREMONY macro and undefined fsm_msg* prototypes are cosmetic and have no runtime or host-reachable effect.

### C3-081 — REFUTED — P3: Maya ADD cap at 5 fields still signs affiliate/fee unseen, in a function with no production caller

Unit: `diff:regress`. Location at audited SHA: `/Users/highlander/WebstormProjects/keepkey-stack/projects/keepkey-firmware-alphafix/lib/firmware/mayachain.c:379`.

The new `if (nfields > 5) return false;` in the ADD branch of mayachain_parseConfirmMemo() copies the THORChain limit but not the THORChain disclosure: thorchain.c:446-480 caps at 5 AND shows fields[3] (affiliate) and fields[4] (fee bps) on a screen. The Maya branch after the cap only shows asset/chain and the optional pool (fields[2]) and then returns true, so a memo `ADD:BTC.BTC:pool:affil:100` (nfields==5) passes the cap, is confirmed with 2 screens, and the affiliate + fee are signed without ever being displayed (invariant 1). Separately, the function is dead surface in the device build (invariant 5): grep shows the only callers are unittests/firmware/mayachain.cpp; fsm_msg_mayachain.h:250-270 deliberately pages the raw memo via thorchain_confirm_full_memo() instead, and transaction.c:358 routes OP_RETURN memos through thorchain_parseConfirmMemo() only. The hunk therefore hardens code no user path reaches, while the commit message presents it as closing the SWAP/WITHDRAW sibling.

Host trace / impact: Not exploitable on-device today because fsm_msgMayachainMsgAck never calls the parser (raw memo is fully paged). If a future change re-wires the fsm to this parser (as the comment at fsm_msg_mayachain.h:261-263 anticipates), a host sends deposit memo `+:BTC.BTC::attacker-affil:1000`: cap passes (5 fields), user sees 'Confirm add asset BTC on chain BTC pool' only, function returns true, and a 10% affiliate skim to the attacker is signed unseen.

Proposed remediation: Either mirror thorchain.c's ADD branch exactly (extract affiliate/has_fee and add the 'Affiliate fee %s bps to %s' confirm before returning true, plus a unit test with a 5-field ADD memo expecting 3 screens), or, since the function is unreachable in production, delete mayachain_parseConfirmMemo()/its header decl/its tests rather than partially hardening dead code.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. At HEAD 71ba84436, mayachain_parseConfirmMemo() has no production caller: grep of lib/, include/, unittests/ shows only the definition (lib/firmware/mayachain.c:254), the header, comment mentions, and unittests/firmware/mayachain.cpp:225. The device deposit path (lib/firmware/fsm_msg_mayachain.h:250-270) pages the full raw memo via thorchain_confirm_full_memo(), so affiliate/fee fields in an ADD memo ARE displayed to the user; transaction.c:358 only routes OP_RETURN through thorchain_parseConfirmMemo. The ADD branch's missing affiliate screen is a real gap inside dead code, but it is unreachable from host input, and the claim itself concedes it is not exploitable on-device. Hypothetical future re-wiring is not a defect at HEAD.

### C3-082 — REFUTED — P3: Maya ADD cap added to a dead function; affiliate/fee fields (3,4) still unseen behind a true result

Unit: `diff:incomplete`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/mayachain.c:379`.

The commit adds `if (nfields > 5) return false;` to the ADD branch of mayachain_parseConfirmMemo(), but (a) the function has no caller in any signing path — fsm_msg_mayachain.h:252 deliberately bypasses it for thorchain_confirm_full_memo(); the only references are include/keepkey/firmware/mayachain.h:38, unittests/firmware/mayachain.cpp:225 and FSM comments; the prior audit (docs/security/audits/2026-09-01-alpha-91af1cfc8.md:129) already recommended deleting it — and (b) even within the function the fix is not the same as the THORChain sibling it claims to mirror. thorchain.c:446-486 ADD branch shows affiliate (field 3) and fee bps (field 4) via an 'Affiliate fee %s bps to %s' screen; mayachain.c ADD only confirms asset/chain and pool (field 2), then returns true for nfields==4 or 5. Trace: memo 'ADD:BTC.BTC:pool:affiliate:100' → nfields=5 → passes the new cap → confirms asset + pool only → returns true with affiliate and 100 bps fee never displayed. Invariant 5 (dead surface) and invariant 1 (display-vs-signed, if ever wired in).

Host trace / impact: No live exploit today because the function is unreachable. If a future change routes deposit memos through mayachain_parseConfirmMemo() (the FSM comment explicitly contemplates 'layering the labeled structured screens back on top'), an ADD memo with an affiliate + fee tail is approved with those fund-directing fields hidden.

Proposed remediation: Delete mayachain_parseConfirmMemo() (declaration, definition, unit test) as the earlier audit recommended, or port the THORChain ADD branch verbatim: parse fields[3]/fields[4], show 'Affiliate fee %s bps to %s' when either is present, and return a tri-state rather than a bare bool.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: NOT REAL. Verified at HEAD 71ba84436: lib/firmware/mayachain.c ADD branch (~:379) caps nfields>5 but only confirms asset/chain and pool (fields[2]), returning true with affiliate (field 3) and fee bps (field 4) undisplayed — unlike the thorchain.c sibling. However, mayachain_parseConfirmMemo() has zero callers in any signing path: git grep shows only include/keepkey/firmware/mayachain.h:38, unittests/firmware/mayachain.cpp:225, FSM comments, and the prior audit doc. The live MAYAChain deposit path (lib/firmware/fsm_msg_mayachain.h ~:250-270) deliberately pages the complete raw memo via thorchain_confirm_full_memo() and never invokes the parser. No host input reaches the defective branch, so the affiliate/fee fields are in fact fully disclosed on the only reachable path. This is dead-surface code hygiene (already flagged for deletion in docs/security/audits/2026-09-01-alpha-91af1cfc8.md:129), not a reachable defect at HEAD.

### C3-083 — CONFIRMED — P3: THORChain/Maya sign screens show account/sequence but still hide signed fee_amount and gas (Cosmos/Osmosis sibling not fully mirrored)

Unit: `diff:incomplete`. Location at audited SHA: `lib/firmware/fsm_msg_thorchain.h:329`.

The commit's stated goal is 'show account_number/sequence like cosmos/tendermint now do'. But the Cosmos (fsm_msg_cosmos.h:507-512), Tendermint (:225-229) and Osmosis screens also render fee_amount and gas, which are required fields (fsm_msg_thorchain.h:85, fsm_msg_mayachain.h:85) and are serialized into the sign bytes (thorchain.c:91-97 '"fee":{"amount":[{"amount":"%PRIu32"..."gas":"%PRIu32"'). The THORChain (fsm_msg_thorchain.h:329-333) and Mayachain (fsm_msg_mayachain.h:311-316) confirm text still reads only 'Additional network fees apply.' with no value. Trace: host sends ThorchainSignTx{fee_amount=4000000000, gas=...} → passes has_ checks → fee serialized into sign doc → user sees 'Additional network fees apply. Account N, sequence M.' → signs a fee value never displayed. Invariant 1 sibling left open by the same change that closed account/sequence.

Host trace / impact: A compromised host sets an arbitrarily large fee_amount (uint32, up to ~4.29e9 base units) in a THORChain/Maya MsgSend/MsgDeposit; the device signs it with no on-screen disclosure of the amount. Practical impact depends on whether the chain's ante handler honors the tx-specified fee (THORChain normally charges a fixed network fee), so this is rated P3 rather than P2.

Proposed remediation: Extend the two confirm() formats to include fee_amount and gas exactly as fsm_msg_cosmos.h:508-512 does, e.g. 'It includes a fee of %PRIu32 RUNE-base and %PRIu32 gas. Account ..., sequence ...'.

Verification: 1/1 required verdicts recovered.

Verifier 1: REAL. At HEAD 71ba84436, lib/firmware/fsm_msg_thorchain.h:329-333 confirm text is "Sign this RUNE transaction on %s? Additional network fees apply. Account %PRIu64, sequence %PRIu64." with no fee_amount/gas args; lib/firmware/fsm_msg_mayachain.h:311-316 is identical in shape. fsm_msg_thorchain.h:85 requires has_fee_amount && has_gas, and lib/firmware/thorchain.c:91-97 serializes msg.fee_amount and msg.gas into the signed JSON ("fee":{"amount":[{"amount":"%PRIu32"...,"gas":"%PRIu32"}). By contrast fsm_msg_cosmos.h:507-512 displays "It includes a fee of %PRIu32 uATOM and %PRIu32 gas." So a host-controlled fee_amount/gas is committed to the signature without any on-device disclosure on THORChain/Maya — the claimed sibling gap is reachable from host input. Severity remains modest (THORChain ante handler charges fixed network fee), consistent with P3.

### audit:bitcoin-signing

Read in full at HEAD 71ba84436: lib/firmware/signing.c (2378 lines), transaction.c (1219), txin_check.c (109), fsm_msg_coin.h (358), coins.c (521), policy.c (48). Supporting callees read: app_confirm.c (confirm_transfer_output/confirm_transaction_output/confirm_transaction/confirm_omni/confirm_data/confirm_bytes/confirm_address/confirm_xpub), confirm_sm.c (format_body, confirm_with_custom_layout, confirm_helper source/render truncation -> 'Cut Off' fail-closed), fsm.c (fsm_getCoin/fsm_getDerivedNode/fsm_clearDerivedNode), crypto.c (ser_length, cryptoHDNodePathToPubkey, cryptoMultisigPubkeyIndex, cryptoMultisigFingerprint), bip340.c (tweak_seckey/tweak_pubkey/bip341_sighash/bip340_sign incl. verify-before-release and memzero), bip32.c (hdnode_private_ckd_cached, get_curve_by_name), cash_addr.c/segwit_addr.c decode bounds, sha2.c sha256_Final scrub, thorchain_parseConfirmMemo entry, board/util.c dec64_to_str, coins.def rows (BTC/Testnet/BCH/LTC/DOGE/DASH/BTG/ZEC/DGB/BSV/DCR/GRS-testnet), types.options/messages.options caps, cmake/caches/device.cmake, CMakeLists BITCOIN_ONLY/DEBUG_LINK definitions, prior audit 2026-09-01-alpha-91af1cfc8.md and the two fix commits touching this unit (txin_check.c strnlen copy; coins.c binance removal). Verified at HEAD: prior cashaddr digest OOB is fixed (txin_check.c:105-106) and the fix is correct (NUL-bounded copy, zeroed tail); multisig signature size capped at 72 (signing.c:876) and witness build uses a local 73-byte buffer; taproot contexts initialised independent of overwintered; taproot_transaction_confirmed gate; prevout amount/scriptPubKey cross-check for legacy inputs on taproot coins; every signing_abort() path scrubs node/privkey/tweaked/hasher state; compile_output/fill_input_script_pubkey scrub their static nodes on all exits; message decode buffer is zeroed before pb_decode so TxAck with zero repeated counts yields all-zero inputs[0]/outputs[0]/bin_outputs[0] that fail validation. Bounds checked against nanopb caps: script_sig 1650 (multisig serialize max ~1642), serialized_tx 2048 (worst witness ~1630, worst input ~1700, output ~560), signature 73 (DER 72 + sighash byte), op_return_data 220 -> script 224 < 520, address 130 NUL-terminated by nanopb, address_n max 8 vs in_address_n[8], segwit program <=40 / cashaddr prog <=65 vs addr_raw[65]. Overflow guards present on to_spend/spending/inputs+outputs/prev inputs+outputs and prev_index vs outputs_cnt. Build portability: BITCOIN_ONLY and DEBUG_LINK are always add_definitions()'d to 0/1 so `#if !BITCOIN_ONLY` / `#if DEBUG_LINK` are -Wundef-clean; no non-literal format strings in the unit. Non-defect observations (not reported): (a) txin_dgst_final() runs per output, so from the second payment output onward the 'current digest' is sha256('') -- the duplicate-transaction heuristic can false-positive only when two outputs in one tx share amount+address, and it never fires for legacy-only inputs because txin_dgst_addto() is only called for segwit inputs (by design per the comment); (b) account_prefix() in coins.c has no purpose-86' case so bip32_node_to_string() fails for taproot transfer/xpub paths and callers fall back to the raw address / raw path -- complete disclosure, just a poorer label; (c) the cashaddr display-prefix strip in compile_output() compares strlen+1 bytes including the NUL against 'bitcoincash:' so it never matches and the full prefixed address is shown -- fail-safe. Not run: no build or tests (read-only mandate).

Checked and found clean: every tendermint_snprintf segment fits the 128-byte buffer with the strnlen caps (max ~72 bytes) and tendermint_snprintf fails closed on truncation; bn_format_uint64 with ' ATOM' suffix cannot overflow amount_str[32] for any uint64 (max 26 chars) so the THORChain blank-amount pattern does not recur here; msg_count==0 rejected; canUpdate/finalize gate on initialized/msgs_remaining/has_message; tendermint_signAbort scrubs node and tmsg on every failure and after successful finalize; generic MsgAck re-binds chain_name/denom/message_type_prefix to the SignTx session; memo confirmed with exact bytes; chain_name is bounded to coin-table names; no #ifndef EMULATOR blocks exist in this unit, tendermint_snprintf carries __attribute__((format)) and all confirm()/snprintf format strings are literals, so nothing in the unit trips -Wformat-nonliteral/-Wshadow/-Wundef under device.cmake. No P1/P2 defects found; three P3 items above (one sibling of the fixed confirm-display truncation, one sibling of the fixed IBC-escaping issue, one fail-closed fall-through in GetAddress). The unused TendermintGetAddress.address_prefix proto field is dead surface but not a code defect.

### audit:ripple-nano

Read in full at HEAD 71ba84436: lib/firmware/ripple.c (313 lines), lib/firmware/fsm_msg_ripple.h (180), lib/firmware/nano.c (371), lib/firmware/fsm_msg_nano.h (160), include/keepkey/firmware/ripple.h, include/keepkey/firmware/nano.h, messages-ripple.options, messages-nano.options, generated messages-ripple.pb.h field types. Supporting callees read: deps/crypto/trezor-firmware/crypto/nano.c (nano_get_address/nano_validate_address), bip32.c hdnode_sign_digest, lib/board/confirm_sm.c format_body/confirm_helper (BODY_CHAR_MAX 352, source-truncation warning + render paging), thorchain_confirm_full_memo -> confirm_bytes, fsm_getCoin/fsm_getDerivedNode (single static CONFIDENTIAL HDNode), coins.c path formatters, coins.def curve/prefix columns, and the 2026-09-01 audit doc.

Verified fixed at HEAD (not re-reported): Ripple VL varint 192 boundary (ripple.c:148-158), flags refused in both FSM (fsm_msg_ripple.h:104) and ripple_signTx (ripple.c:272), second serialization bounded by sizeof(serialized_tx.bytes) (ripple.c:306), amount ceiling 1e17 drops enforced in FSM and serializer, full memo paged via confirm_bytes.

Ripple checked clean: every signed value-bearing field (amount, fee, destination, destination_tag, memo, flags) is displayed or refused; sequence/last_ledger_sequence are unsigned-display-exempt metadata; bn_format cannot overflow the 25-byte buffers because amount<=1e17 drops (16 chars) and fee<=1e6; serialized worst case (~420 B incl. 199-byte memo, 75-byte sig) fits 1024 with fail-closed ok flag on every append; nanopb caps (memo 200, destination 36, address_n 8) bound all host lengths; node memzero'd on all 7 exit paths in SignTx and all 3 in GetAddress; ecdsa DER sig <=72 fits signature[75]. Minor non-security notes: fee/flags/amount rejection paths (fsm_msg_ripple.h:94-124) omit layoutHome(); `needs_confirm` is a constant; ripple_serializeVarint's 3-byte branch and the >918744 branch are unreachable with current caps (dead surface). has_destination/has_amount are not required, but an absent field is only omitted from the blob (rippled rejects a Payment without Destination/Amount) and shows as an empty string, so no loss path.

Nano checked clean apart from the two findings: block hash binds every host-supplied parent field so a lied-about parent balance yields a wrong `previous`; balance/link/hash sizes enforced (16/32); representative and recipient are base32+checksum validated with fixed 60-char length; send/representative-change always confirmed, address bodies (<=120 chars) fit BODY_CHAR_MAX and are paged if they overflow the canvas; recip and node alias one static HDNode and are memzero'd on every exit; static nano_* state holds only public data. Build: nano.c includes assert.h only under EMULATOR and uses no assert; ripple.c includes assert.h unconditionally; no shadowing (nano uses _coin params vs static coin); ternary-literal confirm format is accepted by gcc -Wformat-nonliteral.

### audit:eos

Read in full at HEAD 71ba84436: lib/firmware/eos.c (617 lines), lib/firmware/eos.h, lib/firmware/fsm_msg_eos.h (203), lib/firmware/eos-contracts/eosio.token.c (110), lib/firmware/eos-contracts/eosio.system.c (900), include/keepkey/firmware/eos.h, eos-contracts.h, eos-contracts/eosio.{system,token}.h, include/keepkey/transport/messages-eos.options, the EosTxHeader/EosAsset/EosAuthorization*/EosActionCommon/EosActionUnknown/VoteProducer/UpdateAuth/NewAccount proto definitions, confirm_data()->confirm_bytes() in app_confirm.c, the eos_signingAbort hooks (fsm_msg_common.h:8,628; bitcoin-only stub fsm.c:390), coins.def EOS row, cmake/caches/device.cmake warning flags, and the diffs of the three EOS fix commits (0f4dafcca, e11c80828, 71ba84436).

Verified fixed at HEAD (not re-reported): precision>18 refused; interior-NUL/non-canonical symbol refused; max_net_usage_words bounded to UINT16_MAX and labelled NET; delegatebw verb corrected plus dedicated 'Transfer Stake' screen; undelegate NET label; isStandardAuthorization rejects accounts_count>0; arbitrary auth requires key.size in {0,33} XOR address_n; waits loop iterates waits_count.

Checked clean: all host-controlled counts/lengths are bounded by nanopb caps (authorization 16, producers 30, memo 256, data_chunk 256, keys/accounts/waits 16, address_n 8, chain_id 32 enforced) and every local buffer is sized above the worst case (formatAsset max 30 of 36, formatName 14, producers page buffer 97 for 6x15, WIF 58 of 65, title MEDIUM/SMALL_STR_BUF); unknown-action chunk accounting (data_size constancy, remaining >= chunk); action count accounting (actions_remaining decremented once per action, finish gate requires both counters zero); per-action size varints and field order match the eosio.token/eosio.system ABI; header fields displayed match hashed widths (cpu 1 byte <=255, ref_block_num 2 bytes <=65535, net words varuint <=65535, delay full uint32 in h/m/s); weight fields hashed as 2 bytes and displayed as uint16 consistently; threshold hashed/displayed as uint32; secret hygiene: root/node memzero'd on every abort path and on sign success, hashers/header/address_n scrubbed in eos_signingAbort, GetPublicKey zeroes the node on all exits; AdvancedMode gate for unknown actions is unconditional and session-scoped. The ternary format string at eosio.system.c:73 relies on GCC checking both COND_EXPR arms under -Wformat-nonliteral (a pre-existing pattern, accepted).

Not reported / unverified: (a) EosTxHeader.expiration >= 2^31 would display a pre-1970 date if the device newlib time_t is 32-bit -- no arm-none-eabi toolchain is present locally to confirm the width, and such expirations are rejected on-chain by max_transaction_lifetime, so it is not a concrete defect; (b) action authorization actors/permissions (eos.c:377-395) and ref_block_prefix are signed but not displayed -- chain-enforced against the signing key, no realistic exploit; (c) `inited` stays true after a successful eos_signTx (only header/address_n remain, keys are scrubbed; further acks are refused by the action-count check); (d) VoteProducer page counter over-counts by one when producers_count is a multiple of 6 (cosmetic). No build was attempted (read-only mandate).

### audit:tron

Read in full at HEAD 71ba84436: lib/firmware/tron.c (727 lines), lib/firmware/fsm_msg_tron.h (462 lines), include/keepkey/firmware/tron.h, include/keepkey/transport/messages-tron.options, deps/device-protocol/messages-tron.proto and .options, plus callees the invariants depend on: confirm()/review()/format_body()/confirm_helper() in lib/board/confirm_sm.c (SOURCE-truncation latch + RENDER-overflow paging, so long TRC-20 amount/address bodies are paged rather than clipped), confirm_bytes() in lib/firmware/app_confirm.c, thorchain_confirm_full_memo() in lib/firmware/thorchain.c, bn_format() in deps/crypto bignum.c (zeroes buffer and returns 0 on overflow), base58_encode_check(), fsm.c CHECK_* macros/fsm_getDerivedNode/messagemap.def, cmake/caches/device.cmake warning flags, and unittests/firmware/tron.cpp. Invariants checked: (1) display-vs-signed: every disclosed datum in the parsed path (owner-vs-derived-key, to, TRX amount, TRC-20 contract + uint256 base units, fee_limit, complete memo via paged confirm_bytes) is decoded from raw_data, the exact bytes hashed by tron_signTx; the blind path now shows the sha256(raw_data) digest (prior P3 fixed and verified: tron_formatRawTxDigest at tron.c:447 matches the hash at tron.c:484) behind the AdvancedMode gate; TIP-712 shows both 32-byte hashes in full; TIP-191 pages every message byte. (2) fail-closed: the raw_data protobuf reader rejects field 0, overlong/overflowing varints (including the 10th-byte bit check), truncated length-delimited fields, unknown top-level fields (auths/scripts), repeated contracts, Permission_id, non-zero call_value, TRC-10 token fields, wrong Any type_url/enum agreement, non-64-byte-plus-selector calldata, and non-0x41-prefixed 21-byte addresses; every parse failure memsets the output and returns UNVERIFIED. Amount buffers are provably sufficient (INT64_MAX SUN formats to 25 chars in 32; uint256 formats to 78 digits in 90) so the ignored bn_format return in tron_formatAmount is unreachable-failure only. (3) memory safety: raw_data <= 2048, message <= 1024, hashes == 32 enforced, address_n <= 8 with path prefix check, all parser reads are bounded by len/pos with pos never exceeding len; memo_len bounded to UINT16_MAX. (4) secret hygiene: every fsm exit after fsm_getDerivedNode memzeros the static node; tron.c zeroes hash on all paths of signTx/message_sign/typed_hash_sign/message_verify. (6) portability: no #ifndef EMULATOR blocks; no non-literal format strings; no shadowed identifiers; sign-compare-clean; snprintf/%02x compatible with the sniprintf redirect. Siblings of the prior 25 fixes (byte-count-only blind screens, unhashed side-channel display, clipped memos) were specifically searched for in this unit and none remain; no regressions from the digest/paging fixes found. Not built (read-only mandate).

### audit:authenticator

Read in full at HEAD 71ba84436 (read-only, no build): lib/firmware/authenticator.c (465 lines), include/keepkey/firmware/authenticator.h, the authenticator dispatch in lib/firmware/fsm_msg_common.h (checkPassphrase, fsm_msgPing 190-347, fsm_msgInitialize, fsm_msgCancel), and supporting callees: lib/firmware/storage.c storage_getAuthData/storage_setAuthData/storage_wipeAuthData/storage_cipherBlock/storage_deriveAuthdataKey/session_clear/session_clear_impl/session_cachePassphrase, lib/firmware/passphrase_sm.c passphrase_protect, deps/crypto/trezor-firmware/crypto/base32.c (decode/decoded_length/decode_unsafe), lib/board/layout.c layoutProgressForAuth/progress_render_ex clamp, lib/board/messages.c dispatch buffer handling, confirm_sm.c entry points, include/keepkey/transport/messages.options (Ping.message/Success.message max_size 256), cmake/caches/device.cmake warning flags, unittests/firmware/authenticator.cpp, and the 2026-09-01 audit (no authenticator entries in it; git log shows earlier PR-604 fixes already landed: display-field validation, secret-on-own-screen, duplicate rejection, min secret size, cancel results, cleanup on validated inputs). Checked and found clean: all host strings are nanopb-NUL-terminated within 256 bytes; domain/account bounded to 11 printable chars before storage/display in add/remove; base32 secret bounded to 16..20 decoded bytes (<=33 chars) and alphabet-validated BEFORE it is displayed and stored; add/remove/wipe are button-gated with every stored field shown; hmac offset math bounded (offset<=15, reads <=hmac[18]); hmac key <= 20 bytes so the sha1-of-key branch is unreachable but harmless; i_key_pad/hctx/authSecret/hmac/otp buffers memzero'd on every exit inside authenticator.c; getAuthSlot output (<=85 chars) fits the 128-byte DEBUG buffer and the 256-byte Success.message; no -Wshadow/-Wformat-nonliteral/-Wundef/-Wsign-compare violations spotted by inspection (DEBUG_LINK is always defined 0/1 by CMakeLists). Minor non-security nits not filed: getAuthAccount casts strtol() to uint8_t so '256' aliases slot 0 and non-numeric input selects slot 0 (harmless: same PIN+passphrase-gated listing data); generateOTP matches stored slots by 11-char prefix, so a longer host domain matches its prefix (display comes from the stored slot, so no disclosure mismatch). Not verified by compilation since the task was read-only.

### audit:crypto-rng-bip85

Read in full at HEAD 71ba84436 (read-only, no build): lib/rand/rng.c, include/keepkey/rand/rng.h, lib/rand/rng_health.c, include/keepkey/rand/rng_health.h, lib/firmware/crypto.c, include/keepkey/firmware/crypto.h (declarations), lib/firmware/bip85.c, include/keepkey/firmware/bip85.h, lib/firmware/fsm_msg_bip85.h, lib/firmware/fsm_msg_crypto.h; supporting callees/callers: fsm_msg_coin.h SignMessage/VerifyMessage, fsm.c fsm_getDerivedNode/fsm_clearDerivedNode, reset.h scratch sizes, deps bip32.c hdnode_sign/hdnode_sign_digest, nanopb caps in include/keepkey/transport/{messages,types}.options, cmake/caches/device.cmake warning flags, and the 2026-09-01 audit. Verified fixed and not re-reported: random32()/reset_rng() recursion (now bounded via rng_discard_pending + RNG_MAX_RESETS halt); deser_length was already reported (still present, uncalled, verdict real=False) and is not repeated. Checked clean: BIP-85 path validation (index<2^31, word_count in {12,18,24}) happens before any key material is touched, both in fsm and bip85.c; node/hmac/entropy/mnemonic buffers are wiped on every exit path incl. mnemonic_clear(); display flow shows word_count and index before derivation, fails closed on MAX_PAGES, and never emits the mnemonic on the wire; scratch buffer sizes (241-byte mnemonic vs TOKENED_MNEMONIC_BUF=265, word buffer 15 vs max 12 chars) fit. crypto.c: cryptoMessageVerify bounds (MAX_ADDR_RAW_SIZE=65 matches cash_addr_decode max, base58 failure yields len=0 then length-mismatch reject), multisig fingerprint n/m bounds and nanopb max_count 15/address_n max_count 8, CipherKeyValue data[260] vs key max 256 + 4, SignIdentity challenge bounds (256). rng_health.c RCT/APT logic and continuous latch consistent with header derivations. Latent-but-unreachable (not reported as a finding): RANDOM_PERMUTE with COUNT==0 would wrap i to SIZE_MAX and call random_uniform(0) (mod-by-zero); all callers pass fixed non-zero counts (recovery_cipher.c 2048, memcmp_s.c DECOY_COUNT+2) whereas the checked variant guards len<2 -- consider adding the same guard. Build portability: no #ifndef EMULATOR code found that would trip -Wshadow/-Wundef/-Wformat-nonliteral (the ternary of two literal format strings in fsm_msg_bip85.h is accepted by GCC's format checker and mirrors reset.c).

### audit:clearsign-metadata

Read in full at HEAD 71ba84436 (all unchanged since the 2026-09-01 audit commit 91af1cfc8): lib/firmware/signed_metadata.c (1419 lines), lib/firmware/clearsign_root.c, lib/firmware/fsm_msg_clearsign_attestor.h, include/keepkey/firmware/clearsign_root.h, include/keepkey/firmware/signed_metadata.h. Supporting callees read: fsm_msg_ethereum.h (EthereumTxMetadata / LoadClearsignSigner handlers), ethereum.c metadata call sites (~L809-908, L330-340 enforce), fsm_msg_solana.h certified-cert use, fsm_getDerivedNode, bn_format (deps/crypto/trezor-firmware/crypto/bignum.c), confirm_sm.c body buffer (BODY_CHAR_MAX 352), solana_parseInstrSchema disc_len bounds, nanopb caps (EthereumTxMetadata.signed_payload 1024, LoadClearsignSigner.pubkey 33/alias 32/icon 384, ClearsignAttestorSign.payload 256, EthereumSignTx value 32 / data_initial_chunk 1024), cmake/caches/device.cmake warning flags and ZCASH_PRIVACY definition (always -D'd 0/1, so -Wundef is satisfied). Checked: parser bounds (read_* helpers, METADATA_MAX_* caps vs buffer sizes, v1/v2/v4 minimum lengths, trailer exactness), certificate fixed-layout checks and EIP-712 digest, delegate-sentinel non-promotion, tier/suppression predicate, matches_tx/enforce state resets, Portals decoder offsets against initsz/total (no underflow: total>=452), icon geometry recheck at use, attestor secret scrubbing on every exit path, format-string discipline (all literals; host/cert text only via %s, '%' excluded from metadata text, cert alias allows '%' but only ever reaches a %s argument), -Wshadow/-Wsign-compare/-Wformat-nonliteral exposure. Not re-reported: the prior audit's P3 on the v4 Portals Call[] tail (signed_metadata.c ~L476) is unchanged at HEAD and remains a known, unfixed item. No P1/P2 defects found in this unit.

### audit:confirm-display

Read in full at HEAD 71ba84436: lib/board/confirm_sm.c (989 lines), lib/firmware/app_confirm.c (558), lib/firmware/app_layout.c (926), lib/board/layout.c (995), plus their headers (confirm_sm.h, layout.h, app_confirm.h, app_layout.h) and the callees the bounds/disclosure depend on: lib/board/draw.c (draw_char_impl/draw_string_walk/draw_string_fits), lib/board/font.c (font tables, calc_str_line_n, calc_str_page), keepkey_display.h, cmake/caches/device.cmake, the relevant nanopb .options caps (IdentityType, SignIdentity, Cosmos/Osmosis/Thor/Maya/TON/Hive/Solana address sizes, PublicKey.xpub), and every caller of the unit's public functions (transaction.c, fsm_msg_*.h, u2f.c, reset.c, signed_metadata.c). The prior audit's only confirm-display finding (page_body_confirm 100-page cap) is fixed at HEAD (line 478-484 now refuses). To avoid modelling the renderer by hand I replayed draw_string_walk/calc_str_line_n in Python against the real glyph widths parsed from font.c and checked: standard-layout probe vs draw (consistent, including icon width); constant-power probe (consistent); page_take binary search (every rendered page is independently verified, so non-monotonicity is harmless); confirm_bytes token paging vs the renderer probe (agree: tokens contain no spaces); layout_notification_no_title bold Send screens with worst-case inputs (max-uint64 amounts, 62/64/90-char bech32, cashaddr) -- all fit in its 4 rows, no defect; confirm_xpub 111-char xpub/zpub and 106-char Zcash orchard UA (fit in 3 rows); layout_address_notification 62-char bech32 (QR overlaps the text by one pixel row only, not reportable); ETH EIP-55 addresses (never clip at 140 px). Memory safety: all host lengths are bounded by the nanopb caps before reaching this unit; confirm_sign_identity's 416-byte body cannot overflow (max 401) and its 32-byte title is bounded by proto max_size 9; confirm_omni reads data+4..+19 only when size==20; page_title/BODY_CHAR_MAX snprintf results are checked and refuse. Build portability: DEBUG_LINK/ZCASH_PRIVACY/BITCOIN_ONLY are always defined 0/1 by CMakeLists so #if under -Wundef is safe; no -Wshadow collisions (the global constant_power is not declared in any header included by confirm_sm.c); all format strings are literals. Secret hygiene: strbuf/page_buf/sub are zeroed on the normal paths (see finding for the two gaps). No new instance of the unchecked-bn_format blanking pattern is reachable in this unit (confirm_omni's buffer is provably large enough). The one design-level residual worth stating: confirm_helper still exempts every custom layout from the render-completeness probe, which is the root of finding 1; the bold no-title layout happens to have enough capacity for its current callers, but nothing enforces that.

### audit:board-io

Read in full at HEAD 71ba84436 (no edits, no builds): lib/board/usb.c, lib/board/messages.c, lib/board/keepkey_usart.c, lib/board/keepkey_flash.c, lib/board/timer.c, include/keepkey/board/{messages.h,usb.h,keepkey_usart.h,keepkey_flash.h,timer.h}, include/keepkey/transport/trezor_transport.h (frame layout, MAX_FRAME_SIZE) and interface.h (MAX_DECODE_SIZE), tools/bootloader/usb_flash.c (only RAW_IN user; bootloader-only), cmake/caches/device.cmake. lib/transport/src/* does not exist in this tree (lib/transport holds only nanopb pb_*.c). Supporting callees read: fsm.c message map + _Static_assert(sizeof(msg) <= MAX_DECODE_SIZE), fsm_msg_common.h (Ping/LoadDevice/CharacterAck), u2f.c u2fhid_read/read_start/request_user_presence loops, confirm_sm.c button state machine, keepkey_button.c, passphrase_sm.c, recovery_cipher.c (frame_arena_scratch2049 user), memory.c MPU/USART block, storage.c flash_write_word caller, keepkey_main.c main loop, keepkey.ld .confidential.

Prior-audit fix verified at HEAD: msg_write/msg_debug_write now clamp via msg_write_chunk_len() (usb.c:441-444, 475-476, 518-519); loop bound frame_len = 9 + bytes_written <= sizeof(TrezorFrameBuffer), so no over-read remains; no sibling encoder found (queue_u2f_pkt sends a fixed 64-byte frame). FrameArena refactor (7dc66f786) checked for regressions: rx reset on every usb_rx_helper exit, tx/scratch acquisition drops partial rx by design, tiny path never touches the arena, dispatch decodes before any handler can re-acquire the arena. usb_rx_helper bounds: header length check, checked add + sizeof(frame_arena.rx) cap on cursor, dispatch length = rxMsgSize; msg_read_tiny caps msgSize at 55 and decodes only the fixed allow-list. message_map_entry/message_fields bound msg_id by map_size. timer.c: MAX_RUNNABLES=3 vs 2 distinct callbacks in use, no host-controlled delays; runnable list manipulation is consistent. keepkey_flash.c: flash_write_word's unaligned-prefix underflow (len < align_cnt) is unreachable — the only caller (storage.c:2043) writes at STORAGE_MAGIC_LEN with sizeof(flash_temp)-STORAGE_MAGIC_LEN; no host-controlled offset/len reaches flash_write* in firmware (bootloader raw_handler_upload bounds against FLASH_APP_LEN+FLASH_META_DESC_LEN before flash_write). Bootloader-only oddities not reported as firmware defects: after a RAW message rxFirstFrame is never reset (messages.c:325), and timer_init's 120000 prescaler truncates in the 16-bit PSC. Build portability: all #ifndef EMULATOR code in the unit uses literal formats, no -Wshadow/-Wundef hazards seen (DEBUG_LINK is always defined 0/1 by CMakeLists.txt:167-170); dead USART block was inspected for -Werror hazards and would compile on arm-none-eabi (size_t == unsigned int).

### audit:json-fsm-core

Read in full at HEAD 71ba84436 (read-only, no build): lib/firmware/fsm.c (401 lines), lib/firmware/fsm_msg_common.h (831), lib/firmware/fsm_msg_debug.h (105), include/keepkey/firmware/fsm.h, lib/firmware/messagemap.def (dispatch table), include/keepkey/board/messages.h (MSG_IN/MessagesMap_t designated-initializer map), lib/board/messages.c (usb_rx_helper reassembly, dispatch, tiny-message path, frame arena), lib/board/usb.c msg_write/msg_debug_write, include/keepkey/transport/messages.options and types.options, include/keepkey/transport/interface.h (MAX_DECODE_SIZE), cmake/caches/device.cmake, plus callees the unit's bounds depend on: storage.c (storage_loadDevice, storage_reset/session_clear/storage_clearKeys scrub sites, storage_setAutoLockDelayMs clamp, storage_setLanguage allowlist, storage_hasWipeCode), reset.c reset_init (strength validation), recovery_cipher.c (word_count validation, setup_isArmedAs guards on character/delete/finalize, dry-run PIN gate), pin_sm.c/passphrase_sm.c/dice_input.c/confirm_sm.c reset_msg_stack handling, keepkey_flash.h flash_setModel signature. Prior audit (2026-09-01) had no findings in this unit; its fixed items (msg_write chunk read, frame arena) were re-checked and hold: msg_write_chunk_len clamps to remaining bytes; usb_rx_helper bounds rxCursor+frameSize against sizeof(frame_arena.rx) with overflow check; dispatch only fires when rxCursor>=rxMsgSize<=arena. Verified clean: every handler in fsm_msg_common.h uses nanopb-capped strings via strlcpy/sizeof (Ping 256->32 model buffer, ApplyPolicies policy_name 15 into ButtonRequest.data 256, CharacterAck character[2] NUL-terminated, LoadDevice mnemonic 241); GetCoinTable range/chunk arithmetic (start<count, end<=count, end>=start, end-start<=24); GetEntropy clamps to ENTROPY_BUF and press-free budget is byte-denominated and only on an uninitialized device; MessagesMap lookup guarded by map_size>msg_id and msg_id/type/dir equality so debug-only IDs are unreachable on the normal interface and vice versa; every _Static_assert sizeof(msg)<=MAX_DECODE_SIZE and RESP_INIT sizeof<=msg_resp; fsm_getDerivedNode failure paths send Failure and all coin handlers memzero the node on every exit (spot-checked coin/cosmos/eos/nano/attestor), fsm_clearDerivedNode wired into session_clear(clear_pin)/storage_reset/storage_clearKeys/signing_abort; ceremony gating (CHECK_NOT_INITIALIZED + bitcoin-only-locked + CHECK_NO_CEREMONY) and setup_stage refusing re-entrancy; ResetDevice.display_random correctly ignored. Build portability: no #ifndef EMULATOR block in the unit uses non-literal formats, VLAs, or shadowing that GCC>=4.8 -Wshadow flags (isValidModelNumber's `model` parameter shadows a function, which -Wshadow no longer reports); DebugLinkFlashDump's arbitrary-address memcpy is DEBUG_LINK-only (KK_DEBUG_LINK defaults OFF for device; ON only in emulator.cmake) and reachable only on the debug interface, so not reported. Not covered: the per-coin fsm_msg_*.h handlers beyond node-scrub spot checks, nanopb decoder itself, USB driver below usb_rx_helper.

### diff:regress

Read git show 71ba84436 and every touched function in full: eos_compileActionUndelegate (eosio.system.c), fsm_msgMayachainMsgAck / fsm_msgOsmosisMsgAck / fsm_msgThorchainMsgAck confirm blocks, mayachain_parseConfirmMemo ADD branch, request_user_presence + both ctap2 wrappers (u2f.c), reset_rng/random32 (rng.c). Cross-checked: account_number/sequence are uint64_t in the generated .pb.h and PRIu64 is already used identically in fsm_msg_cosmos.h/tendermint.h (inttypes.h via app_confirm.h), chain_id max_size:32 so bodies stay far under BODY_CHAR_MAX=352 and confirm() fails closed on truncation via body_truncated; u2f switch assigns title in every branch (default covers RESET) and all layoutU2FDialog formats are literals, satisfying -Wformat-nonliteral -Werror in cmake/caches/device.cmake; rng_discard_pending is declared and used only inside #ifndef EMULATOR blocks, the discard branch leaves new==last so the loop simply waits for the next word, no recursion; EOS label change is display-only, no wire change. No compile, memory-safety, or secret-hygiene regressions found. Single finding: the Maya ADD hunk is display-vs-signed incomplete relative to thorchain.c and lives in a function with no production caller.

### diff:incomplete

Reviewed git show 71ba84436 and read in full every touched function: eosio.system.c eos_compileActionUndelegate (label fix correct, cpu/net arg order matches); fsm_msg_{mayachain,osmosis,thorchain}.h MsgAck confirm sites plus their SignTx has_* validation (account_number/sequence required, so displayed values equal signed values; PRIu64 pattern pre-exists in cosmos/tendermint; inttypes via app_confirm.h/tendermint.h); mayachain.c mayachain_parseConfirmMemo whole function and its thorchain.c sibling ADD branch plus repo-wide caller search; u2f.c request_user_presence and both ctap2_* wrappers (all layoutU2FDialog formats are literals, title assigned on every switch path, no -Wshadow/-Wundef/-Wswitch issues under cmake/caches/device.cmake WARN_FLAGS, emulator path unchanged); lib/rand/rng.c reset_rng/random32 in full plus rng_health.c RNG_DR reader (rng_discard_pending is #ifndef EMULATOR on both definition and use, no RNG ISR exists, discard path leaves new==last so loop re-arms, health test consuming the pending word only delays the discard by one word — no defect). No memory-safety or secret-hygiene surface was touched by this diff. Could not confirm newlib _WANT_IO_LONG_LONG for %llu under -Dsnprintf=sniprintf locally (toolchain headers not found), but that risk predates the commit. Two P3 findings; no P1/P2.
